APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Tuesday 3-Nov, on the 2-Nov close

Readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026, Monday's close (the dashboard data built at 22:47 UK on 2-Nov). The previous note read the Fri 30-Oct month-end; this one adds Monday's session, which changed several charts. The rules are your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026: the 10-day stop-loss rule for Early Innings Probing Bets (each tranche's MA and its stop-loss, the MA less 6%), the six Sell Criteria alone for Middle Innings Core Investments. The next reading is Fri 6-Nov (Industry RS below 30; the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month); the next month-end reading is Mon 30-Nov.

The three things that matter most this week

1. The rules sell two holdings at today's Tue 3-Nov session: Arteche (4.28% of capital) and Alfen (4.6%), and Monday's close made neither case weaker.

  • Arteche, the whole holding: its relative price closed the 30-Oct month-end 34.7% below its 52W high, against the 25% line; at the 2-Nov close it is 38.6% below. Arteche is not under your one-off in/out link, so it sells today. What Monday added: Arteche fell 5.2% on 2-Nov, on 1.38 times its 50D average volume, while its industry and every large peer rose, and closed 6.1% below its rising absolute 200D MA. The cause is not in the sources read.
  • Alfen, all three tranches: exit confirmed on 28-Sep under the 10-day stop-loss rule (three consecutive closes at or below the 150D MA in the bottom half of the day's range), not sold. The 150D MA is €12.77, the stop-loss €12.00, the 2-Nov close €10.37, 13.6% below the stop-loss. Why it matters now: Alfen's third-quarter update comes on Wed 4-Nov, the day after the sale; consensus has not changed for a month while the SP fell 21.5% from 22-Sep to 26-Oct. Alfen's 4.2% rise in the week to 2-Nov was a sector bounce on light volume, and nothing in it marks a low.

2. Thursday 5-Nov can move two holdings at once: Helios Towers (5.17%) and IMCD (2.0%) both report.

  • Helios Towers: the update can fire a fundamental exit. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's Invalidation test is organic tenancy additions below 600 in a quarter; Morgan Stanley's preview implies about 526, Deutsche Bank's about 800. On the daily readings, "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" already reads present (Sector RS 43, Stock RS 68 on 2-Nov); the test is read at the 30-Nov month-end.
  • IMCD: tranche 1 is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule, 3.0% above its rising 20D MA (€101.36). Because the 20D MA keeps rising, a results-day fall of about 2.2% (to about €102.2) on Thu 5-Nov would close at or below it and start a test; a second close at or below it on ordinary volume would confirm the exit, which would sell at Tue 10-Nov.

3. Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry, now reads Industry RS 40, and Friday 6-Nov reads it against the 30 line. It was 79 on 2-Oct and 46 at the 30-Oct month-end. Below 30 on Friday would sell Volex (5.52%) and Sdiptech (6.05%) in full at Tue 10-Nov, though both stocks' own trends are intact; for IMCD, a Probing Bet, the test is information only. Why it matters: the next sale the rules could add is set by the industry's relative strength, not by these stocks' own prices.

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the fortnight and the month, and answers your 2-Nov note on Healthcare. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then come what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

About this run

  • Your three AlphaSense daily screens could not be read: Watson's Pink Chrome browser was not connected at 03:35 UK on 3-Nov. The Developments rest on the research filed, the earnings reviews and the data files.
  • Your Notion position notes were read: "[PM] All live stocks" (28 to 31-Oct) and "[PM] ART" (2-Nov). No separate position note was found on Bally's Intralot (your 28 to 31-Oct note has a short entry on it); each section quotes you before answering.
  • No sale is made by this note. Every sale and add above is for your decision.

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.4% in the week to Monday 2-Nov-2026, 1.5% over the fortnight and 2.9% over the month, and Monday's 0.4% rise was led by cyclicals and Consumer staples, not by Healthcare. Over three months the Stoxx Europe 600 is down 1.2%; over twelve months it is up 13.6%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 26-Oct close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 19-Oct close, and the month the 21 sessions from the 2-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases.

  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 2-Nov): Consumer staples +9.7%, Healthcare +8.6%, Professional, business and consumer services +7.3%, Consumer discretionary +6.0%, Defence +4.7% and Infrastructure +4.5%.
  • The month's laggards: Technology (1.5%), Industrials and capital goods (1.4%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (0.1%), Transportation +0.2% and Materials +0.6%.
  • The fortnight: Healthcare +5.0% led, then Real assets +3.7% and Infrastructure +2.9%; Energy, commodities and metals mining (2.1%), Technology (0.6%) and Media (0.5%) lagged, and Industrials and capital goods rose only 0.4%.
  • The week: Consumer discretionary +2.9% and Materials +2.1% led, Defence +1.8%, Industrials and capital goods +1.5% and Healthcare +1.2%; Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.3%), Technology (0.5%) and Transportation (0.5%) fell.
  • Monday 2-Nov: Consumer discretionary +2.1%, Media +1.6%, Real assets +1.4%, Materials +1.4%, Consumer staples +1.2% and Industrials and capital goods +1.0%; Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.0%), Technology (0.2%), Healthcare (0.1%) and Defence (0.1%) fell.
  • Over three months the split is wider: Transportation +9.3%, Healthcare +7.6% and Energy, commodities and metals mining +5.4%; Real assets (7.8%), Defence (6.8%) and Media (4.8%).

Monday 2-Nov was a broad risk-on day inside a month of defensive leadership, and it explains several of the holdings' moves. Carlsberg (a 52W high), Hilton Food (a 52W high), CVS Group (its first close above the 7-Sep high) and D'Ieteren (above its ceiling, on Consumer discretionary's best day of the week) all rose with their sectors, but each on below-average or only moderately heavy volume. Against the day, Arteche fell 5.2% while Industrials and capital goods rose 1.0%, and Theon fell with Hensoldt and Exosens while Defence was flat: those two moves are the stocks' own. What would settle it: whether the Monday leaders hold their gains in a week when the index falls.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO, that is because HC has been so weak over LTMish… It's simple reversion (short covering, or buying the laggards) - classic counter-momentum", and "It means HC might be catching a bid now… Prioritise…! ASAP!". The data support the bid; whether it is reversion or a lasting rotation is not yet settled.

  • For the bid: Healthcare is the strongest industry over the fortnight (+5.0%) and second over the month (+8.6%), and it has risen in each of the last two weeks.
  • For counter-momentum rather than a new trend: Healthcare paused on Monday 2-Nov at (0.1%) on the day the index and the cyclicals rose, which is what a short-covering move does when risk appetite returns. Over three months it is up 7.6%, so the reversion has run for longer than a fortnight.
  • Inside the book: CVS Group is the only Healthcare holding moving with the bid (+19.3% over the month); Xvivo, the other Healthcare holding, closed on 2-Nov at its lowest since 23-Mar-2026, with its analysts still cutting estimates. Your instruction to prioritise Healthcare is listed under what I will do, for the role it goes to.
  • The reading that would decide it: Healthcare holding its fortnight's gain in a week when Consumer discretionary and Industrials and capital goods lead again.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of 30-Oct-2026 keeps the ideal portfolio defensive. Its tilts are: Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Media and Defence; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included. It holds Momentum at Underweight and Fragility at Double Underweight. Its reason is a risk-off regime beneath a steady index, "led now by credit and the cost of debt". On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote that the tilts as they then stood, without the Defence Underweight that the 30-Oct assessment added, are "still sensible — perhaps even more so than a month ago". Monday's tension with it: the two Double Underweights, Consumer discretionary and Real assets, were among Monday's leaders, and Energy, an Overweight, fell 1.0%; one day does not change the assessment, and the next is due on Fri 6-Nov.

Arteche

Middle Innings Core Investment · 4.28% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 2.00%, bought 26-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.28%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 1.00%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Arteche's SP broke down on Monday 2-Nov: it fell 5.2% to €29.0 on a day its industry rose 1.0% and every large grid and electrification name rose, closing 6.1% below its rising absolute 200D MA (€30.89) and below its 23-Oct low (€30.1). Arteche is down 23.1% from its €37.7 close of 30-Sep and 34.1% below its €44.0 52W closing high (8-May); over 12 months it is still up 54.2%, over 3 months up 2.8%.

The legs since the 30-Sep high, against the industry (Industrials and capital goods) and peers (chart files):

  • 1-Oct, (5.8%), Arteche's own (industry (1.3%)); 6 to 23-Oct, (17.5%), the sector's (industry (5.0%), Prysmian (24.0%)); 23 to 29-Oct, +5.6%, a bounce Arteche lagged (Prysmian +16.0%, Legrand +8.5%); 30-Oct, (3.8%), the sector's (Nexans (7.6%)).
  • 2-Nov, (5.2%), Arteche's own: the industry rose 1.0%, Siemens Energy 2.4%, Nexans 1.9%, Legrand 1.4%, NKT 1.0%. Arteche opened 1.3% higher at €31.0, the day's high, and closed at 0.09 of the day's range.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) records GE Vernova's agreement on 28-Oct to buy Trench, the instrument-transformer leader (item 9 (Peers)). The cause of the 2-Nov fall is not in the sources read; a web search found no Arteche announcement dated 2-Nov.

Hypothesis 1, now the better supported: the market has started to de-rate Arteche's own premium, the "Pfisterer template" the memo warns of. For it: the 30-Oct note's test was whether Arteche would again rise "less than half as much as Legrand, Siemens Energy and ABB" on the next sector up day; on 2-Nov those three rose 0.9% to 2.4% and Arteche fell 5.2%. Against it: no estimate, PT or rating has moved (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), and items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)), and no Development explains the day. Hypothesis 2: a sector de-rating plus noise in a thin stock (about €1m traded a day). Against it: Arteche's two falls against its sector (1-Oct, 2-Nov) came on the two heaviest volumes of the decline (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Ergo, its a sentiment related issue at the thematic level (AI + DCs) not grid concerns nor stock concerns". Through 30-Oct the data agreed; 2-Nov does not: on a day the theme rose, Arteche fell on above-average volume. You also wrote that Arteche is "probably high beta version of the grid trade"; on the data it is not (beta 0.87 against the market; the memo measures 0.45 against the peer basket, which explains 5% of its moves). Its swings are large but mostly its own, so its own days decide its sell tests.

The MAs: Arteche closed below its 20D MA on 8-Oct, its 100D and 150D MAs on 19-Oct, its 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 22-Oct, back above the absolute 200D MA on 27-Oct, 0.8% below it on 30-Oct and 6.1% below it on 2-Nov. The 20D (€32.58) and 100D (€31.86) MAs are falling; the 50D (€32.31), 150D (€33.05) and absolute 200D MAs are rising. Precedent: Arteche closed 1.9% above the absolute 200D MA at the 29-Jul low and 1.9% below it at the 1-Sep low; 6.1% below is its widest close below the line in 2026.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO SP is clearly holding/testing below the 200D MA - consolidating or about to negatively break downwards". The 2-Nov close broke down on both tests on file: it is more than 5% below the line, past your principle that a price holding "< 5% below an MA" is still "holding/testing" (your Carlsberg note), and the 30-Oct note's broken-trend test is present: a close more than 5% below the absolute 200D MA on above-average volume (1.38x the 50D average without mechanical-volume days; 1.07x on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis). It is one close.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Arteche against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+3.5% over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€33.05 against €30.89).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA: partly (the 50D MA is above the absolute 200D MA, but has been below the 150D MA since 1-Sep).
  • The SP above each MA: fails (10.2% below the 50D, 12.3% below the 150D, 6.1% below the absolute 200D MA).
  • Near its 52W high: fails (34.1% below).
  • Away from its 52W low: passes (48.0% above the €19.6 closing low of 6-Nov-2025).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "APM says "seven Stage 3 flags present or partly present" - what are they". They are your seven Stage 3 (topping) red flags, counted from the 29-Jul low where the advance began; on the 2-Nov close:

  • The largest one-day fall since the advance began: present. 1-Oct, (5.8%), the session after the 30-Sep high, on 1.94x volume. 2-Nov's (5.2%) is the fourth largest and came two sessions after the 29-Oct gap-up on the Trench news: a fall after good news.
  • The largest one-week fall: present. (8.2%) in the 5 sessions to 23-Oct; the two sessions to 2-Nov, (8.8%), exceeded it.
  • Wider swings: partly. The average daily range was 3.1% of the SP in August, 4.0% in October and 4.5% in the last 5 sessions, but 4.2% in the September advance.
  • Lower lows: present. €34.3 (8-Oct), €30.1 (23-Oct), €29.0 (2-Nov); still above €28.0 (1-Sep) and €27.8 (29-Jul).
  • More down days than up days: present. 14 down against 9 up since 30-Sep.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly. The 30-Sep high came on 0.76x and was reversed on 1-Oct on 1.94x; but the advance had two heavy up days (25 and 29-Sep).
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: resolved downward. Arteche tracked the absolute 200D MA for seven sessions (22 to 30-Oct, within 3.3%) without a bounce, then closed 6.1% below it.

Verdict: a broken medium-term trend on one close, inside a long-term trend that still passes (absolute 200D MA rising, 150D above it). What would settle it: a close back above the absolute 200D MA (€30.89) within days on volume would make 2-Nov a shakeout; a close below €28.0 (the 1-Sep low) would leave no higher low since July and break the long-term trend as well.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets (its 50D average is 31,155 shares, against 24,083 without them).

For the first time in the decline, Arteche's own holders sold on above-average volume, on a day its sector was bought. On 2-Nov Arteche fell 5.2% on 33,355 shares (€0.97m), 1.38x (1.07x) its 50D average, opening at the day's high and closing at 0.09 of its range. On 30-Oct the pattern was the reverse: the large names fell on 1.36x to 1.76x their 50D averages (Nexans 1.76x, Schneider Electric 1.58x; Siemens alone on 1.02x) while Arteche fell on 0.60x (0.46x). On 2-Nov the large names rose on 0.72x to 1.13x; only Landis+Gyr traded heavily (1.66x, (1.0%)).

Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: two, both at the top.

  • 29-Sep: +5.0% on 47,667 shares (€1.79m), 1.75x (1.39x), closing at 0.89 of its range after a 2.0% gap up: an accumulation day.
  • 1-Oct: (5.8%) on 53,152 shares (€1.89m), 1.94x (1.55x), closing at 0.31 of its range: the dashboard's only distribution day in 25 sessions.
  • 2-Nov, at 1.38x, is below the 1.5x line but above your 1.25x distribution-day line on the basis without mechanical-volume days; the dashboard (1.07x) does not count it. On that basis Arteche has two distribution days in 25 sessions, at the start and at the end of the decline.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I read the volume charts L3M positively - why does this clash with the SP trends + the APM comments". Both readings are right, about different months.

  • The 3-month chart is dominated by September: 15 up days against 3 down in the 1 to 30-Sep advance, two accumulation days (25 and 29-Sep), and the 18-Sep index review, the tallest bar on the chart (13.69x) and an up day. Without that day, the average up day in September traded about the same as the average down day (ratio 1.04).
  • October's volume is light selling with lighter buying: since 30-Sep the average down day traded 0.90x the 50D average (1 of 14 heavy) and the average up day 0.72x (none heavy); the up/down volume ratio is 0.80 since 30-Sep, and the PMS Per-stock Dashboard reads 0.84 over 20 sessions and 0.67 over 5.
  • Light selling is the positive you read: holders were not dumping. But no buyer of size has appeared at any October low, and on 2-Nov the selling became heavier.

Every MA event in the decline came on below-average volume except the last: the 22-Oct break of the 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 0.79x (0.61x), the 27-Oct recovery on 0.75x (0.58x), the 2-Nov break on 1.38x (1.07x).

How Arteche's 2026 declines ended: the June to July decline with a 6.1% up day on 2.14x the day after the 29-Jul low; the May to June decline quietly, its light-volume rebound failing into a deeper fall. October's lows have had no heavy up day: so far the May to June pattern.

Arteche's relative price is 38.6% below its 52W high (12-May) and 10.9% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. It confirmed the SP's lower low on 2-Nov: relative price (15.95) is back at its 3-Sep level. It was 23.2% below its 52W high on 5-Oct.

Confirms a turn: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 36,100 shares, €1.05m), closing in the top third of its range and back above the absolute 200D MA (€30.89). Confirms the decline continues: a close below €28.0 (the 1-Sep low) on more than 24,100 shares.

3. RS

Arteche's Stock RS fell 12 points in one session, from 76 at the 30-Oct month-end to 64 on 2-Nov, its lowest reading in the data file's 13 months and below the 70 line the Sector RS test reads.

  • Stock RS: 94 (2-Oct), 83 (23-Oct), 76 (30-Oct), 64 (2-Nov): (30) points in a month; 94 three months ago, 98 twelve months ago; previous low 73 (11-Sep).
  • Sector RS (Electrical equipment, grid and automation): 75 (2-Oct), 13 (23-Oct), 39 (29-Oct), 25 (30-Oct), 21 (2-Nov): below 50 since 16-Oct.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 (9-Oct), 46 (23-Oct), 46 (30-Oct), 40 (2-Nov): 10 points above 30, and level with its 12-month low (40, March 2026).

The order of the turn: sector first, industry second, Arteche last, and now all three are falling together. The sector broke between the 9 and 16-Oct Fridays, the industry between 16 and 23-Oct, Arteche's Stock RS in the last week of October; on 2-Nov all three fell again. Arteche's Stock RS is now falling faster than its sector because Arteche is falling on days the sector rises.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Industry RS is still fine. Same for the 200D MA slope and Stock RS". On today's readings only the relative 200D MA slope (+2.91% over the month) is still clearly fine. Industry RS is at its 12-month low, and Stock RS is below 70: on today's values both parts of the Sector RS test are present; it is read at the 30-Nov month-end.

Against peers: Arteche's 64 now ranks behind Cenergy and Landis+Gyr (69 each) in its sector; Schneider Electric is 18, Siemens Energy 20. Precedent: the last time Arteche's Stock RS stood near 70 (73, 11-Sep), it was back at 96 within two weeks, on Spain's €17bn grid plan (La Moncloa, 16-Sep). No comparable dated catalyst is in the sources before 30-Nov except the undated Strategic Plan 2030. The mechanical drag: as November 2025's closes (€19.6 to €23.2) become the 12-month base, Arteche's 12-month gain at a flat €29.0 narrows from 54% to about 25% by early December, which weighs on Stock RS with no further fall. Test: the Fri 6-Nov Stock RS reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

Arteche's consensus has not moved since 30-Sep, through a 23.1% fall: the SP is leading, and the memo (1-Nov) expects estimates to follow it down rather than the SP to follow estimates up. FY2026 EPS (€1.077), FY2027 EPS (€1.333) and FY2028 EPS (€1.56) are unchanged over one month, with three analysts each (1-Nov).

  • Over 3 months the nearer year rose fastest: FY2026 EPS +9.3%, FY2027 +5.4%, FY2028 +1.3%; EBITDA rose 8.0%, 7.4% and 10.8% for the same three years. Over 6 months FY2026 and FY2027 EPS rose 17.5% and 17.6% while FY2028 EPS fell 1.3% and FY2028 EBITDA 1.0%: analysts pulled earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory.
  • Implied growth: FY2026 to FY2027, EPS +23.8%, EBITDA +21.7%, revenue +16.6%; FY2027 to FY2028, EPS +17.0%, EBITDA +17.6%, revenue +14.9%.
  • Implied EBITDA margin: 17.7% (FY2026), 18.5% (FY2027), 18.9% (FY2028). FY2026 consensus EBITDA (€103.7m) is 3.3% above the top of the guidance Arteche raised on 28-Jul (€92.1m to €100.4m).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "The stock is showing EPS, EBITDA and revenue upgrades for 2026 to 2028". Over 3 and 12 months, yes; over one month nothing has moved, and over 6 months FY2028 EPS and EBITDA are slightly down. The upgrades are behind the SP, not happening now.

The memo's view of the next move is down: it puts FY2027 EPS after minorities at €1.22 against the €1.333 mean, rates cuts of 5% or more to FY2027 and FY2028 consensus at about 60% and 65%, and expects the first FY2027 guidance (24-Mar-2027) at about €112m to €120m of EBITDA against €126.2m consensus. Most of the gap is below EBIT (tax, financial items, minorities). Hypothesis: the SP is discounting the cuts the memo expects. Against it: no analyst has cut, and the dated event that tests it is in March 2027. Test: the first change to FY2027 consensus after the Strategic Plan 2030.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst has cut anything on Arteche: 3 Buys of 3, and EPS, EBITDA, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth all +33% (one of three raising, none cutting). Revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth are unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end; EPS and EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth halved in October, from +67% at the 30-Sep month-end (two of three raising) to +33% at the 31-Oct month-end, as a September raise left the counting window. There is no broker cutting to report. There has been no Sell in 24 months; coverage has varied between 1 and 3 analysts.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "And upgrade breadth is positive (not even neutral)". Confirmed, with a qualification: the +33% reflects one raise inside the counting window (late September), not new raises; with consensus flat for a month, Net Upgrade Breadth is positive by inheritance, and EPS and EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth already fell from +67% to +33% over October as the raises aged. It keeps the Net Upgrade Breadth test far from a sell signal (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion)).

Arteche's average PT (€39.11) is 34.9% above the SP (€29.0), wider than at any month-end since August 2024.

  • The PT has followed the SP: it rose 58% in six months, in steps after SP rises (€24.8 to €34.2 over the May and June 2026 month-ends, after the May spike), and has not moved since 29-Sep. The memo puts the two domestic brokers' PTs at €37 to €42.
  • Precedent: the earlier gaps above 20% (February and March 2025; August 2026) were followed by SP rises, but opened while estimates were rising; this one opened on flat estimates.
  • Hypothesis: the gap is wide because three domestic analysts have not reacted to the de-rating, so the PT will not cushion a further fall; the memo expects "a consensus cut with no downgrade" as international coverage arrives.
  • A data fault, still open: the daily average PT read €47.0 on 15, 16 and 17-Oct and €39.11 on every other day; no broker note explains it.

9. Peers

Arteche has gone from falling with the large grid and electrification names to falling without them: from 23 to 29-Oct it rose 5.6% against 7% to 16% for them, and on 2-Nov it fell 5.2% while they rose. From 30-Sep to 2-Nov Arteche fell 23.1%, more than Schneider Electric (17.7%), Prysmian (10.3%) and Legrand (6.2%) (chart files). Legrand (average correlation of daily moves with Arteche 0.42) and Siemens Energy (0.35), outside its cohort, are still the stocks whose daily moves track Arteche's most closely: on 2-Nov that link broke.

Your four peer checks:

  • Sell-side estimates: rising at the large names, falling at the small cohort peers. Over 3 months EPS rose 4% to 7% at Schneider Electric, ABB, Prysmian and NKT, with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of 52% to 64% at ABB, Prysmian and Schneider Electric; it fell 7% at Pfisterer and 5% at R&S Group Holding, with negative revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((20%) and (17%)), and Landis+Gyr's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is (20%). Oddo BHF cut its Pfisterer PT to €81 from €91 on 23-Oct (boerse-global.de, 24-Oct).
  • SP technicals: Arteche has lost its place at the top of the sector. On 30-Oct Arteche was nearer its absolute 200D MA than any sector name below it (0.8% below); on 2-Nov it is 6.1% below, alongside ABB (6.3%), NKT (6.0%) and Legrand (5.2%). Only Landis+Gyr (+20.0%), Cenergy and Siemens close above the line.
  • Newsflow: the leaders' results are strong, and the niche's leader has been bought. Schneider Electric raised guidance (16-Oct), ABB's third-quarter orders rose 40% (20-Oct), Prysmian beat and raised (Jefferies, 29-Oct).
  • Company guidance: raised at the leaders, cut at R&S Group Holding ("temporary demand moderation" in parts of the distribution transformer market, 5-Aug).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "GE Verona buying Trench - a close Arteche peer - for 13x 2028 EBITDA or 18x 2026 EBITDA" and "Q - What is read-across to Arteche?". The read-across is mixed, and the market read it as no support.

  • The positive: the memo (1-Nov) calls it "a valuation reference" and says it "may push Hitachi Energy and Siemens Energy, who now buy bushings and instrument transformers from a direct competitor, toward neutral suppliers: for Arteche, AGIT and its own lines"; it adds that "Bernstein calls that conflict overblown, so it is a tail, not a base".
  • The limit: Trench was priced on a projected 37% EBITDA margin; Arteche's FY2027 consensus margin is 18.5%. At €29.0, with the memo's enterprise value adjusted for the lower price, Arteche trades at about 12x FY2028 and 17x FY2026 consensus EBITDA: roughly Trench's deal multiples for half Trench's margin. The deal tells you the niche is valued; it does not say Arteche is cheap.
  • The price action: Arteche opened 3.2% higher on 29-Oct, the session after the deal, and no peer matched it; sellers faded it on 0.57x volume and Arteche has since fallen 8.8% in two sessions.

Who turned first: R&S Group Holding and Pfisterer months before Arteche; Alfen and the large names in October (Schneider Electric's Stock RS 78 to 18 in a month); Arteche last (94 to 64). Landis+Gyr is the exception (+15.8% since 30-Sep, Stock RS 69), for a cause not in the sources read.

What the peers imply for Arteche: the reassuring reading, "orders up, shares down" (Portfolio Fitness for Fighting, 23-Oct), still describes the large names, whose estimates rise. It no longer describes Arteche's last week: Arteche now moves like the cohort's small caps after their own de-rating began (Pfisterer, R&S Group Holding), on flat rather than falling estimates. Test: Pfisterer's third quarter (18-Nov), the closest read-across for order normalisation in the small-cap grid names.

4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's consensus has not moved for a month: Alfen's SP fell 21.5% from 22-Sep to 26-Oct on unchanged estimates, so the SP is ahead of the analysts, waiting for the 4-Nov update.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS a loss of €0.149, unchanged over 1 month, cut from a profit of €0.18 over 3 months; revenue €455.3m, flat over 1 month, +1.1% over 3; EBITDA €22.2m, +1.9% over 1 month, (12.5%) over 3
  • FY2027: EPS €0.354, flat over 1 month, +13.3% over 3; revenue €492.5m, flat, +0.7% over 3; EBITDA €28.7m, (0.3%) over 1 month, (6.6%) over 3
  • FY2028: EPS €0.499, flat, +3.4% over 3; revenue €534.0m, flat, +0.7% over 3; EBITDA €34.4m, (0.3%) over 1 month, (4.7%) over 3

The cuts are largest in the nearest year, and the recovery is placed in FY2027 and FY2028. The implied EBITDA margin is 4.9% in FY2026, 5.8% in FY2027 and 6.4% in FY2028; implied EBITDA growth is 29.5% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 19.8% from FY2027 to FY2028, on revenue growth of 8.2% and 8.4%. FY2026's 4.9% sits in the lower half of the company's guidance of a 4% to 7% adjusted EBITDA margin (reiterated 18-Aug, earnings review).

FY2027 EPS rose 13.3% in three months while FY2027 EBITDA fell 6.6%: the gain is below the EBITDA line (the memo flags depreciation), on 7 analysts; I give it little weight.

Against the memo's tests: its re-open test pairs a first-quarter 2027 adjusted EBITDA margin of 8% (2.2 points above FY2027 consensus) with third-quarter 2026 storage intake above €50m; its invalidation tests include third-quarter 2026 storage intake below €30m. Deutsche Bank's 6-Oct preview ("Phasing of storage business weighing on Q3") points to the low side of the storage test. Test: 4-Nov.

No Buy among Alfen's 10 rated analysts since the August month-end (0 Buys, 6 Holds, 4 Sells since 30-Sep), and no rating or PT change in the data file since 30-Sep. Net Upgrade Breadth splits: revenue +33% (3 of 9 raising, none cutting), PT +20% (3 of 10 raising, 1 cutting), EPS +14% (2 of 7 raising, 1 cutting), EBITDA (33%) (none raising, 3 of 9 cutting). All four readings are unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end. More revenue at a lower margin is the analysts' picture.

Where analysts are cutting: EBITDA and one PT, and the one cutter named in the sources read is Jefferies. Jefferies' David Kerstens cut Alfen from Buy to Hold on 21-Aug, cut his PT to €14 from €18 and cut his FY2026 EBITDA by 32% to €18.8m (a 4.0% margin, from 6.0%), "blaming €8m of temporary, overlapping personnel costs" of the reorganisation (earnings review). Healthy retest or broken trend? Xvivo against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: fails. It peaked on 28-Oct and has fallen since, (0.02%) over five sessions.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes, weakening. SEK 259.78 against SEK 244.01, with the 150D falling since 26-Oct.

Verdict: a broken trend, not a retest; all six red flags of a broken trend listed above (the largest fall after good news, wider swings, lower lows, more down days than up days, a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, the SP tracking a rising MA) are present, and four of the six trend-health tests fail. What would settle it otherwise: an up day of 5% or more on 1.5 times average volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)), then a close above the 20D MA (SEK 214.37).

4. Consensus by named FY

Xvivo's analysts have cut FY2027 three times in a month and are pushing earnings into FY2028. FY2027 consensus EPS fell 13.2% from 29-Sep to 28-Oct (SEK 6.04 to SEK 5.24) in three steps: 3.8% on 6 and 7-Oct, 5.3% on 22 and 23-Oct, and 4.7% on 28-Oct. FY2028 EPS fell 8.3% over the month (SEK 9.48 to SEK 8.69), and FY2026 EPS rose 4.2% (SEK 3.50 to SEK 3.64), the tax and financial-income lift from the third quarter that the Investment Analyst expected to flatter momentum (sell-side memo, 3-Nov). No estimate has changed since 28-Oct (data file, to 1-Nov).

The cuts fall on FY2027 profit far faster than on revenue.

  • FY2027 over one month: EPS (13.2%), EBITDA (10.9%) to SEK 303.8m, revenue (4.3%) to SEK 1,201m; over 12 months EPS (21.7%), revenue (7.9%)
  • The implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 25.3%, from 27.2% at the 30-Sep month-end and 28.0% at the May month-end, against the company's kept 2027 target of more than 30% (earnings review, Q3 2026)
  • Implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 has fallen from 73% at the 30-Sep month-end to 44%, while FY2027 to FY2028 has widened from 57% to 66%: analysts are moving the earnings onto FY2028, the year of the hoped-for United States heart launch
  • FY2028's implied EBITDA margin is trimmed too, to 30.6% from 31.7%, with revenue (0.9%) and EBITDA (4.5%) over the month

The named brokers' logic (Investment Analyst's earnings summary, 30-Oct): DNB Carnegie cut FY2027 EPS 4% to SEK 5.7 and FY2028 3% to SEK 8.6 for lower projected abdominal sales, while raising FY2026 10%; RedEye sized the largest customer's in-house lung platform at 5 to 6% of 2027 group sales.

The SP stopped tracking the cuts for one week, then fell anyway. The 28-Oct cut (FY2027 EPS 4.7%, average PT 2.3%) came on a day Xvivo rose 1.4% and closed near its high (0.96 of the day's range); the 2-Nov fall came with no estimate change. The Investment Analyst (30-Oct) set out two readings: the reset being done early and in the price, or the market cutting earnings and the multiple together, which shows as the SP tracking each cut. Test: whether Xvivo holds its price at the next round of cuts; the Investment Analyst expects FY2027 consensus to be cut at each of the next four reports, 25 to 35% in all (sell-side memo, 3-Nov), starting with the fourth-quarter report on 28-Jan-2027.

Buys rose from 4 of 5 to 6 of 6 at the October 2025 month-end, the month of Xvivo's previous low, and have not moved since. These ratings follow valuation, not momentum.

Xvivo's average PT is SEK 324.9 (28-Oct), 62.7% above the SP, and falling in steps with the estimates. The average PT fell from SEK 346.1 on 29-Sep to SEK 336.1 on 6-Oct, SEK 332.4 on 23-Oct and SEK 324.9 on 28-Oct, 6.1% in a month, while the SP fell 11.1% from SEK 224.6. Xvivo's Net Upgrade Breadth is negative on every measure, and all of the movement is cutting.

  • EPS: (100%) on 1-Nov, 0 of 6 raising and 6 cutting, from 0% at the 30-Sep month-end and (67%) at July and August
  • EBITDA: (100%), 7 of 7 cutting, from (29%) at 30-Sep
  • Revenue: (86%) at the 30-Oct month-end, 6 of 7 cutting, from 0% at 30-Sep and +83% at the May month-end
  • PT: (57%) at 30-Oct, 4 of 7 cutting, from (14%) at 30-Sep and +71% at the April and May month-ends

Where analysts are cutting, and why. Every analyst covering Xvivo's EPS and EBITDA cut in the month. The stated logic in the sources read: DNB Carnegie cut FY2027 and FY2028 for lower abdominal sales, the kidney volume held back by a manufacturing transfer due to finish by the start of 2027; RedEye cut its fair value for the largest United States customer's move to its own lung perfusion platform (LungBio, approved June 2026), which it sized at 5 to 6% of 2027 sales and answered by moving to a 90% discounted-cash-flow valuation (earnings review, Q3 2026; Investment Analyst's earnings summary, 30-Oct). The other cutting brokers are not named in the sources read.

Precedent: in 2026 Xvivo's SP turned before its breadth. Xvivo's SP turned up on 3-Mar-2026 and revenue breadth followed, from (50%) at February to +67% at April; relative price peaked on 21-Apr and breadth turned negative at June. If that pattern holds, Xvivo's next turn shows first in its SP and volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)), and breadth confirms one to two months later.

9. Peers

Xvivo is the only one of its five-stock cohort below its 50D and absolute 200D MAs, and the only one whose estimates are falling hard; the cohort trades on estimate direction, and Xvivo is on the wrong side of it.

Your four peer checks, for Xvivo's cohort (Ambu, Bonesupport, Vitrolife, Surgical Science), to 2-Nov:

  • Sell-side estimates: three peers' 3M one-year-forward EPS revisions are +6% to +11% (Bonesupport, Vitrolife, Surgical Science) and their revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +80% to +100%; Ambu's is (2%) and +20%. Xvivo's is (14%) and (86%).
  • SP technicals: all four peers are 11.7% to 27.4% above their 50D MAs and up 17.8% to 39.8% over the month; their Stock RS rose over the month to 49 to 98. Xvivo is 15.3% below its 50D MA and down 6.5%.
  • Newsflow: Bonesupport's third-quarter report beat on United States sales on 22-Oct (Redeye, 22-Oct, Finwire via Börsvärlden) and its SP rose 24.3%; Vitrolife reported the same morning and rose 5.0%. The causes of Surgical Science's and Ambu's October rises are not in the sources read.
  • Company guidance and communications: Xvivo kept its 2027 targets (EBITDA margin above 30%, EBIT margin above 20%) on 22-Oct while saying sales to its largest customer will decline, with no timeline; consensus now implies a 25.3% FY2027 EBITDA margin (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). The peers' guidance changes are not in the sources read beyond Bonesupport's beat.

Who turned first: Surgical Science (Stock RS from 4 three months ago to 92 a month ago), then Bonesupport, Vitrolife and Ambu in October, as Xvivo turned down. Xvivo's estimates were cut on 6 and 7-Oct, a fortnight before the peers' reports; the rotation within the cohort shows on 22 and 23-Oct (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "These stocks track their SS estimate momentum." Inside this cohort the data support you: the three peers with rising EPS estimates have the highest Stock RS (71 to 98) and Xvivo, with the steepest cuts, the lowest (11). The wider sector is less tidy: Ypsomed Holding's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is (70%) and its 3M EPS revision (3%), yet its Stock RS is 86, so the link is strongest for the small one-product growers. The Sector Digest (31-Oct) gives the mechanism: for a small grower "the multiple breaks on the first doubt about the path, on a miss or on a beat with a weaker outlook", and Xvivo's 22-Oct report was a beat with a weaker outlook for its largest customer.

On 28-Oct you asked why Bonesupport, Vitrolife and the others rose in October. The sources read give two causes: their estimates rose while Xvivo's fell, and their sector rose with Healthcare from mid-October (Sector RS from 23 on 2-Oct to 63).

The week to 2-Nov: Xvivo fell 1.2% while Vitrolife rose 5.2%, Surgical Science 3.1% and Ambu 1.6%; only Bonesupport fell, 8.2%, giving back part of its 22-Oct gain. On 2-Nov itself Bonesupport fell 4.2% and Vitrolife 0.8% while Ambu and Surgical Science rose; Xvivo's (2.6%) was 1.6 points worse than the cohort's average.

What the peers imply for Xvivo. A sector recovery is already under way and Xvivo is not in it, so the peers offer Xvivo no lift until its own estimates stop falling. For the rules, the peers matter through Sector RS, which keeps the test of Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 clear at 63; a reversal of October's sector rally would bring that test back by the 30-Nov month-end.

In July Theon's Stock RS rose from 30 (3-Jul) to 69 (24-Jul) while Industry RS stayed between 7 and 20, then Industry RS jumped from 13 (31-Jul) to 59 (7-Aug) and Theon rose 15.4% to its 14-Aug high. Then Theon's absolute 200D MA was rising and PT Net Upgrade Breadth was +56% at the June month-end and still positive, +10%, at the July month-end; now that MA is falling and the breadth is (25%) (items 1, 5 and 6).

  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The last stock pullback of this magnitude in Dec-25 was against much stronger RS backdrop for stock and sector + industry; total opposite now". Confirmed: at the 30-Dec-2025 low Theon's Stock RS was 59 to 72, Sector RS 62 to 64 and Industry RS 86; each is now 32 to 73 points lower (Stock RS 27, Sector RS 10 and Industry RS 13 on 2-Nov).
  • On 28-Oct you wrote: "RS for Industry, Sector and Stock have collapsed - STRONG SELL". The data agree; the one improvement, Stock RS from 16 to 32, was partly reversed on 2-Nov.

Would confirm the rotation: Defence's Industry RS above 30 at a Friday reading. Would refute it: Theon's Stock RS back below 22 (its 23-Oct reading). Hensoldt's Stock RS rose from 22 to 42 and Kongsberg Gruppen's from 33 to 72 over the month, faster than Theon's.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts keep raising Theon's EBITDA for every year and its FY2026 EPS, while cutting FY2027 and FY2028 EPS: the operating case is upgraded, the shareholder's line beyond this year is not. No estimate changed between the 30-Oct and the 1-Nov readings.

  • EPS: FY2026 €1.563, +2.6% in a month, +9.1% in 3 and +11.3% in 12; FY2027 €1.654, (1.6%) in a month, (1.6%) in 3 and (3.2%) in 12; FY2028 €1.971, (0.7%), (1.7%) and +12.2%.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 €161.8m, +1.7%, +3.3% and +10.9%; FY2027 €202.8m, +1.2%, +3.2% and +15.5%; FY2028 €242.5m, +1.7%, +3.8% and +32.6%. The later years rise faster than the nearer one: analysts are raising the operating trajectory, not pulling it forward.
  • Revenue: FY2026 flat at €600.6m (the ~€600m guidance of 7-Sep); FY2027 €743.1m, +0.4% in a month and +11.5% in 12; FY2028 €871.7m, +0.6% and +19.3%.
  • Implied growth and margin: FY2027 over FY2026, revenue +23.7%, EBITDA +25.3%, EPS +5.8%; FY2028 over FY2027, +17.3%, +19.6% and +19.2%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 26.9% to 27.3% and 27.8%. The gap between EPS growth and EBITDA growth arises in FY2027.

New this week, from the research filed on 30-Oct: part of the FY2026 EPS "upgrade" is an accounting artefact of Theon's 9.8% Exosens stake, which makes the FY2027 EPS growth look weaker than it is and FY2026 stronger. FactSet's broker detail (28-Oct) shows FY2026 EPS of €1.79 at ING and €1.95 at Marex, which include fair-value gains on the stake (€37.2m in the first half), against €1.28 at Deutsche Bank and €1.43 at UBS, which exclude them (Deutsche Bank, via AlphaSense research filed 30-Oct).

  • Hypothesis: the 6.3% (September, to the 30-Sep month-end) and 2.6% (October, to the 31-Oct month-end) rises in Theon's FY2026 EPS consensus are brokers adding Exosens gains, not operating upgrades. For it: FY2026 revenue did not move over the month; FY2026 EBITDA rose only 1.7%. Against it: the sources read do not name the brokers who moved on those days. Consequence: Exosens fell 6.6% over the month and 7.3% over the week to 2-Nov (chart file), which on those brokers' basis is a fourth-quarter fair-value loss, so FY2026 EPS can fall at the 9-Nov update without any operating miss.
  • The FY2027 EPS cut is real and explained: Deutsche Bank cut adjusted EPS 5% on 11-Sep for the interest on the €325m term loan; the Merio purchase (80%, completed 29-Oct) brings a 20% minority and amortisation; the HGH closing slipped to early 2027 (Deutsche Bank, 27-Jul). Each adds EBITDA before it adds EPS.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Holding here looks like hope. This looks classic Stage 3 IMHO. Exit unless we can prove strong upgrade scope". The data show no upgrade scope at the line the market prices, FY2027 EPS, and the FY2026 EPS rise is weaker than it looks. The scope that exists is operating: FY2028 EBITDA up 32.6% in 12 months, and a record of beating revenue guidance in FY2024 and FY2025 (research filed 30-Oct).

On 28-Oct you wrote: "Real shame. Why has the trade rolled over… Confusing at 15X P/E with guidance upgrades". Three causes in the sources read: Defence's multiple fell while its estimates held (item 1 (SP and MAs)); ING applied a leverage discount (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)); and the upgrades lifted EBITDA, not FY2027 EPS. Test: the 9-Nov third-quarter trading update; a book-to-bill of 1.0 or more (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's test) followed by FY2027 EPS revisions turning up would show upgrade scope reaching the shareholder.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Theon's analysts raise EBITDA and revenue while cutting EPS and PTs (unchanged since 30-Oct): less EPS per euro of EBITDA, and a lower price paid for it, not weaker demand.

  • FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth turned negative at the 30-Oct month-end, (14%) (2 raising, 3 cutting, of 7), after four month-ends at zero.
  • FY2027 EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth is +50% (4 raising, none cutting, of 8), from +30% at 30-Sep; revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +25% (2 of 8, none cutting), from +44% in July and August.
  • PT Net Upgrade Breadth is (25%) (none raising, 2 cutting, of 8), from (11%) at 30-Sep, +10% in July and August and +78% in May. It has fallen or held at every month-end since May.

The brokers cutting, and their stated logic:

  • ING (Tijs Hollestelle), 15-Sep: Buy kept; PT cut to €38 from €42.50 on a 2027 multiple of enterprise value to EBIT of 16.1 times, "citing investor sensitivity to leverage", benchmarked to land-defence peers whose multiple fell from 21.6 to 17.2 times between January and September 2026; Theon removed from ING's favourites on near-term macro headwinds, while calling the raised margin target "a highly positive surprise" (earnings review; research filed 29 to 31-Oct).
  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: ""ING put its PT cut (item 1 (SP and MAs)) down to the European defence sector as a whole, not to Theon's numbers, which rose (item 3 (Consensus by named FY); earnings review)" is instructive of the issues - what does the note say". ING's cut is a multiple cut on two counts, the land-defence peers' lower multiple and a leverage discount, with its forecasts raised: better operations, a lower price for them.
  • Deutsche Bank (Sriram Krishnan), 11-Sep: Buy and €39 PT kept, adjusted EPS cut 5% for the term loan's interest; Krishnan warned that integration delays at the French acquisitions could stop Theon reaching its raised margin target, and puts end-2026 leverage at about 3.4 times against the 3.5 times covenant.
  • The second PT cut and two of the three FY2027 EPS cuts are not named in the sources read.

Theon's ratings have not followed the SP down: 8 Buys, no others and no Sells at 30-Oct and 1-Nov, against 8, 1 and 0 at 30-Sep; one neutral analyst left the count. With every remaining analyst at Buy, ratings cannot signal a turn; PTs and breadth will.

Theon's average PT, €39.88 (unchanged since 22-Oct), is 34.5% above the 2-Nov close, against 44.6% at the 8-Oct low and 31.1% at the 30-Oct month-end. Both times in 12 months the gap reached about 35% (the November 2025 and June 2026 month-ends), Theon's SP rose by about a quarter over the next six to seven weeks; both times the PT breadth was positive (+20% and +56%), and now it is (25%).

Would confirm a turn: two PT raises with no new cut, taking PT Net Upgrade Breadth to zero or above, after the 9-Nov update. Would refute it: further FY2027 EPS or PT cuts after it.

9. Peers

Theon trails the defence electronics leaders in the rebound and fell with the whole cohort on 2-Nov: the 2-Nov fall was the cohort's, not Theon's alone.

  • From the 8-Oct low to 2-Nov: Kongsberg Gruppen +22.7%, Ovzon +18.3%, MilDef +15.9%, Hensoldt +12.0%, Theon +9.0%, Exail Technologies +0.7% (anchored by Thales's agreed €134 a share) and Exosens +0.6% (chart files). In the fall from 14-Aug to 8-Oct Theon's (29.0%) was the cohort's worst.
  • Since the 29-Oct close: Exosens (4.8%), Hensoldt (4.5%), MilDef (4.3%), Theon (3.2%), Ovzon (3.0%); only Kongsberg Gruppen rose (+1.4%). On 2-Nov alone Hensoldt fell 2.3%, Exosens 2.8% and MilDef 1.7%, all on light volume, while Defence was flat. So Theon fell less than its closest peers in the last two sessions.

Theon's four peer checks:

  • SP technicals: Kongsberg Gruppen (9.3% above), Hensoldt (5.0%), Exail Technologies (2.4%) and MilDef (36.2%) are above their absolute 200D MAs; Theon (5.9% below), Exosens (8.1% below) and Ovzon (18.8% below) are not. Hensoldt's and Kongsberg Gruppen's absolute 200D MAs are flat to rising (0.0% and +1.2% over 20 sessions); Theon's is falling (0.28%).
  • Sell-side estimates: Theon's 3-month EPS revision, (1%), is the cohort's second weakest, against MilDef's +24%, Exosens' +7%, Kongsberg Gruppen's +3% and Hensoldt's +2% (PMS Per-stock Dashboard peer panel).
  • Newsflow: Exosens reported third-quarter revenue up 23.5% on 27-Oct, "driven by stronger demand and faster-than-expected capacity ramp-up; fully on track to meet upgraded 2026 outlook" (Exosens press release, 27-Oct), and fell 4.8% that day on 2.25 times its 50D average volume (chart file): a beat sold on heavy volume. Hensoldt and Rheinmetall report on 5-Nov.
  • Company guidance: Exosens raised its FY2026 guidance on 17-Sep and reaffirmed it on 27-Oct; Theon raised its own on 7-Sep. Both raised, and both still lag the cohort.

Hypothesis: the market treats the night-vision pair, Theon and Exosens, as short-cycle soldier equipment rather than long-cycle defence electronics.

  • For it: Exosens is Theon's most correlated stock (average correlation 0.55) and tied to it by Theon's 9.8% stake and a 400,000-tube supply contract; both lag since 8-Oct despite raised guidance; brokers give long-cycle electronics a premium over short-cycle land equipment (Bernstein, 29-Sep); the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's counter-case calls soldier-equipment demand "a two-to-three-year replenishment surge that peaks in 2027 to 2029".
  • Against it: Exosens' estimates are rising fast (+7% in 3 months), so its lag is not an estimates problem; and Bernstein (28-Oct) argues armies are moving to one night-vision device per soldier, which makes demand durable.
  • What Exosens implies for Theon's 9-Nov update: the market sold a clean Exosens beat on 2.25 times volume; a Theon beat may meet the same reception, and an Exosens fall also lowers some brokers' FY2026 EPS for Theon (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • Test: if both night-vision names lag Hensoldt again on a good Defence day (5 and 9-Nov), the market is pricing the segment, not Theon's debt alone.

What the peers imply for Theon's sell tests: Theon's Stock RS (27) sits with Exosens (37) and Ovzon (22), not with Hensoldt (42), Kongsberg Gruppen (72) or MilDef (96); a cohort rally lifts Theon's Sector RS faster than its Stock RS, and a cohort fall like 2-Nov's can take Theon's relative price through its €29.01 line (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)) without any Theon news.

What the rules say for Theon now, and what decides the next step

  • The rebound has stalled under falling MAs. Theon's 12.6% rise from 8-Oct peaked intraday 1.9% below its falling absolute 200D MA and has given back 3.2%; the 2-Nov fall was the cohort's, on light volume, so a base of higher lows (€27.92, €28.64) holds inside a broken trend (items 1, 2 and 9).
  • The upgrade scope you asked for is not shown. FY2027 EPS is being cut, with Net Upgrade Breadth (14%); part of the FY2026 EPS rise is Exosens fair-value gains, which Exosens' 7.3% fall last week works against (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). The upgrades are at the EBITDA line, which the market is not paying for.
  • What the rules say: Theon is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone. Four give whole-holding sell signals at their latest readings: Industry RS 13 and the relative 200D MA (1.17%) at the Fri 30-Oct reading; Sector RS 21 with Stock RS 32, and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (25%) with Stock RS 32, at the 30-Oct month-end. Under your one-off in/out link Theon is sold when its replacement is bought, in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale; nothing else sells it at the Tue 3-Nov session. Your 2-Nov note lists Theon among the "Secondary" sell signals. Neither tranche (tranche 1, 2.00%; tranche 2, 2.05%) has a 30% fall triggered.
  • The nearest test, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (read 30-Nov): 23.3% below, from 21.6% a week ago, 21.0% four weeks ago and 18.6% thirteen weeks ago. A Theon close below €29.01 on 30-Nov (2.2% below the 2-Nov close at a flat market, which a Defence fall of about 1.7% would produce at Theon's 1.30 ratio to Defence) gives a sell signal; under your link it would be a fifth sell signal on Theon and would change nothing the rules do.
  • The next, a tranche 30% below its highest close, with Stock RS (27) below 70: both tranches' level is €26.82, 9.6% below the close, from 10.5% a week ago, 9.9% four weeks ago and 28.4% thirteen weeks ago (when the level was lower). A Defence fall of about 7.4% would reach it at Theon's 1.30 ratio to Defence (9.6% divided by 1.30). A close below €26.82 triggers both tranches, each sold at the Tuesday session after its release, even under your link. One fall sets off both, €29.01 first. On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "27 EUR is the 30% below where we bought level - very close to exit criteria". Confirmed at €26.82, but measured from the highest close since purchase, not the purchase price: 30% below the €38.32 close of 14-Aug for both tranches, 9.6% below the 2-Nov close.
  • What would take the standing signals away: relative price up about 81% by 6-Nov, 34% by 13-Nov or about 11.5%, held from 3-Nov, by 27-Nov turns the relative 200D MA up (computed on the dashboard's basis, the MA against its level 21 sessions earlier); Defence above 30 at a Friday ends the Industry RS signal (13 to 59 in one week in August); Sector RS 50 or Stock RS 70, and Stock RS 50 or PT breadth at zero, at 30-Nov end the other two.
  • The record: on today's rules Theon's Industry RS, Sector RS and relative-price tests gave sell signals around the 30-Jun month-end; a sale at the Tue 7-Jul session (€32.64) would have been 10.0% above today's close, though it would have missed the 17.4% rise to 14-Aug that came with Industry RS rising from 13 (3-Jul) to 73 (11-Aug).
  • Next readings and adds: Fri 6-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA; both will very probably still give sell signals. The rules block an add while any Sell Criterion gives a sell signal, with 2.63% of room below the 6.68% limit.
  • Where Theon stands for an add: none of the three stages (pulling back or basing; may be retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern; healthily retesting a rising MA or breaking out of one). The pullback has become a broken trend. The Master Dashboard (built 2-Nov) reads Healthy Retest "Possible (3 of 6 tests)" with no MA retested, Basing "Possible (1 of 4 tests)", Pulling Back "None (1 of 4 tests)" and both volatility contraction pattern tests "None (0 of 4 tests)". Neither add trigger is present (item 12 (adding capital)).
  • Dates: Hensoldt's and Rheinmetall's results, 5-Nov; the Friday reading, 6-Nov; Theon's third-quarter trading update, 9-Nov (research filed 30-Oct; the data file shows only FY2026 results, projected for 19-Mar-2027); the Bundestag budget vote, 27-Nov; the month-end readings, 30-Nov.
  • What would change this reading: a book-to-bill of 1.0 or more on 9-Nov with a close above €31.96 on heavy volume would start to repair the trend; a close below €28.64, then €27.92, would end the base and bring both lines into play.

Richard decides.

  • Verdict: a broken trend at the 20D to 150D MAs, with four Stage 3 red flags present and two partly; at the rising absolute 200D MA the SP is still testing, inside the 5% band your 28 to 31-Oct note gave for holding or testing. What settles it: a close above 361.68 on 1.5x the 50D average volume (a retest that held) or a close below 343.7 (a break of the last rising MA).

Avanza's heavy volume was concentrated in the seven sessions from 13 to 21-Oct, five of them distribution days, and has receded since; the decline as a whole carries more heavy-volume down days than any of Avanza's three earlier declines in 12 months.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The ratio of down-day to up-day volume this month was much more negative than during any other pullback over LTM". Partly confirmed. 4. Consensus by named FY

Avanza's consensus has not been cut for the third-quarter miss: FY2026 EPS is down 0.9% in a month, FY2027 is flat, and FY2028 is still rising; the SP has fallen 10.0% in the same month, ahead of the estimates.

  • FY2026 EPS 19.36: (0.9%) over 1 month, 0.0% over 3, +6.6% over 6. FY2026 revenue SEK 5,200m: (1.0%), (0.4%), +3.9%.
  • FY2027 EPS 21.06: (0.1%) over 1 month, +1.6% over 3, +7.5% over 6. FY2027 revenue SEK 5,743m: 0.0%, +1.1%, +5.1%.
  • FY2028 EPS 23.27: +1.2% over 1 month, +2.8% over 3, +8.7% over 6. FY2028 revenue SEK 6,322m: +0.8%, +1.8%, +5.9%.
  • Avanza is a bank and has no EBITDA consensus.

The later year is rising faster than the nearer ones: analysts have cut the year the miss lands in and raised the trajectory. Implied FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth has risen from 9.3% three months ago to 10.5%, while FY2026 to FY2027 growth is 8.8%. Hypothesis: the rate path (a Riksbank hike expected on 4-Nov, worth about SEK 160m a year of net interest income in the A&J Memo's base) is lifting the later years while activity cuts hit 2026. Against: the only post-results estimate changes documented in the earnings review are DNB Carnegie's, and it cut FY2028 to SEK 20.79 from 20.93 (earnings review).

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo: its base puts FY2027 EPS at SEK 20.2, 4.1% below today's consensus of 21.06, and it expects consensus cuts of 2% to 4% to FY2027 and 3% to 6% to FY2028 at the mid-January compilation and the 22-Jan results. Hypothesis: the SP has priced that cut first. For: the SP peaked on 28-Aug as FY2027 EPS stalled (up 0.2% in August, to SEK 20.77, after 4.3% in July). Against: FY2027 EPS then rose 1.4% in September, to SEK 21.07, while the SP fell 7.9% to its 24-Sep low, so the SP led the estimates down rather than following a stall. Against: only three of thirteen houses had published since the print (memo), so most models are pre-results. Test, by the 22-Jan results: a 2% cut takes FY2027 EPS to about 20.64.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "I quickly scanned the earnings news and thought it was fine. Yet the ER summary reads more negatively (e.g. PT cuts from at least 2 brokers). I am out of step with the market ATM". The earnings review supports the more negative reading: the profit miss was small (2%), but every recurring revenue line missed, and the A&J Memo judges about two-thirds of the fall in income per customer structural. The market has moved before the estimates: PTs and breadth have turned (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)) while EPS consensus has barely moved.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Avanza's analysts are cutting PTs and revenue while holding EPS: PT Net Upgrade Breadth fell from +24% at the 30-Sep month-end to (16%) at 30-Oct (5 raising, 8 cutting, of 19), and revenue breadth from +22% to (6%) (6 raising, 7 cutting, of 16).

  • EPS breadth for FY2027 fell from +90% (31-Aug) and +33% (30-Sep) to 0% at the 31-Oct month-end (7 raising, 7 cutting, of 19).
  • The average PT fell 2.0%, from 421.0 (13-Oct) to 412.6 (28-Oct). The divergence: PTs and breadth have turned while average EPS is flat, so the cuts are to revenue per customer and the multiple, not yet to the earnings total.

The brokers cutting, and those holding, with their logic (earnings review unless stated):

  • DNB Carnegie (Buy): PT to SEK 425 from 430 (19-Oct); FY2026 EPS to SEK 18.90 from 19.04, FY2027 to 19.29 from 19.34, FY2028 to 20.79 from 20.93. Its logic: the brokerage and currency shortfall came from softer retail sentiment and more lower-priced Private Banking and Pro clients, which it treats as cyclical.
  • Nordea (Buy): PT to SEK 435 from 465 (Placera, 19-Oct); its logic is not in the sources read.
  • Jefferies (Underperform, SEK 335, unchanged): the miss reinforced its view that lower income per customer, competitive pricing, the Danish entry and technology spending make sustainable EPS weaker than the growth case assumes.
  • Deutsche Bank (Buy, SEK 440) held: the asset engine outweighs yield compression.
  • The other six PT cuts and the seven revenue cuts in the counts are not named in the sources read.

Hypothesis: Avanza's ratings follow the SP. For: Buys rose from 2 of 14 (October 2024 month-end) to 11 of 21 (30-Sep-2026) as the SP rose, with jumps in January 2025 and June 2026. Against: Buys fell in May 2025 as the SP rose. Since the results Buys have fallen to 10 of 19, with coverage down from 21; no rating change is named in the sources read.

Avanza's average PT (412.6) is 17.3% above its SP (351.6), up from 16.4% on 27-Oct; the gap was widest at the February (19.5%) and April 2026 month-ends, each within about 3% of an SP low.

Precedent: Avanza's PT breadth was also negative at the January, February and May 2026 month-ends, each near an SP low (the closing paragraph gives what followed). What differs now: this time the PT cuts follow a results miss, and the A&J Memo expects two more rounds of estimate cuts. Test, after 4-Nov: PT changes after the Riksbank decision; the memo calls a November hold "the one event that would turn the whole cohort's revisions negative at once".

Middle Innings Core Investment · 2.0% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.0%, bought 26-Jun-2026) · readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Bally's Intralot missed the gambling relief rally: flat in a week when its industry and its UK peers rose. Both are small (0.4 and 0.2 times its usual moves), but both are negative.

  • The trend: (1.8%) over 3 months (Stoxx Europe 600 (1.2%)) and +6.7% over 12 months. The SP has spent a year between €0.83 and €1.20.
  • The legs since the August high: €1.176 (14-Aug) to €1.059 (18-Sep), (9.9%); to €1.140 (1-Oct), +7.6%; to €1.080 (8-Oct), (5.3%); to €1.130 (21-Oct), +4.6%; to €1.120 (2-Nov), (0.9%), the latest leg, in which every close since 23-Oct has sat between €1.117 and €1.122.
