Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with my UK Budget memo of 28-Oct and the AlphaSense research reports filed on 31-Oct and 2-Nov; they hold no company news since the 1-Sep first-half results.

  • 28-Oct, the UK Budget: Machine Games Duty to 25% from April 2027 on William Hill's shop machines (about £9m to £12m a year on our estimate, under 2% of the combined group's EBITDA), and a change to remote gaming duty's free-play rules from April 2027, which bears on the bonus cuts Bally's Intralot used to offset 65% of its second-quarter duty cost (Bally's Intralot monitoring note, 28-Oct). The 29-Oct reaction was 0.0% on 1.31x (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • 20-Oct: Flutter announced up to 1,400 UK and Ireland redundancies to offset the duty (press release, via the AlphaSense research report of 2-Nov).
  • 23-Oct: Evoke's third-quarter update kept completion of Bally's Intralot's purchase for late fourth quarter 2026 or early first quarter 2027 (SCCG Management).
  • Which legs are Bally's Intralot's own, not its industry's: the (0.9%) leg from 21-Oct to 2-Nov is its own, because the industry and the peers rose over it; the cause is not in the sources read. Hypothesis: the pending issue of new shares to Evoke's holders caps the SP while the deal spread narrows (item 9 (Peers)).

The MAs: the long MAs rise, the medium ones fall, and the SP sits on all of them. On 2-Nov: 100D €1.123 (falling 0.6% over 21 sessions), 20D €1.112 (rising 2.1%), 150D €1.109 (rising 2.9%), 50D €1.106 (falling 0.8%), absolute 200D €1.070 (rising 0.75%). The SP is 0.3% below the 100D MA and 0.7% to 1.3% above the 20D, 150D and 50D MAs; it crossed the 100D MA three times between 21 and 27-Oct on volume of 0.19x to 1.18x, so none of the crossings carried conviction. At the earlier turns the absolute 200D MA held: on 23-Jul (0.7% above) and 18-Sep (0.2% above).

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "150D MA now above the 200D MA so the MAs are stacked into proper Stage 2… Positive". Partly: the 150D MA is 3.6% above the absolute 200D MA and both are rising, but the 50D MA sits below the 150D and is falling, and so is the 100D. Your trend-health tests read:

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes, +0.75% over 21 sessions.
  • The 150D above the 200D: passes, €1.109 against €1.070.
  • The 50D above the 150D and 200D: partly: above the 200D, 0.3% below the 150D.
  • The SP above each: partly: above the 20D, 50D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs, 0.3% below the 100D.
  • Near its 52W high, away from its low: partly: 6.4% below the 23-Jun closing high (€1.197), 34.7% above the 30-Mar low (€0.831).
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: more down days than up days, present (10 against 8 over 20 sessions; 31 against 28 over 63); lower lows, partly (18-Sep's €1.059 below 23-Jul's €1.064, then a higher low on 8-Oct); the SP tracking flat MAs instead of bouncing off a rising one, present; the largest one-day or one-week fall since the March advance began, absent (the largest are (3.2%) on 28-Apr and (7.2%) in the week to 16-Jul); wider swings, absent (the range is narrowing); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, absent.
  • Verdict: neither a healthy retest nor a broken trend, but a sideways base with two Stage 3 flags present and a sell signal from three Sell Criteria. What settles it: a close above €1.140 on 1.5x the 50D average or more, or below €1.080 on heavy volume.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Bally's Intralot's chart file rounds prices to €0.01 (about 0.9%), so close-in-range readings near 0 or 1 are coarse; closes and changes below use the data file's unrounded closes. The 50D average holds two days of mechanically inflated volume (31-Aug; 18-Sep, an index day), which lower each ratio by about 10%; the volume script's ratio without those two days is in brackets.

Bally's Intralot's volume is drying up in a tight range: neither buyers nor sellers are pressing.

  • Heavy-volume days in 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more): two, both up days and both before 16-Oct. On 29-Sep the SP rose 2.7% to €1.130 on 5.23M shares (€5.91m, 1.94x; 2.06x), closing at the day's high after a 0.9% gap down; on 15-Oct it rose 2.1% to €1.114 on 6.61M shares (€7.34m, 2.36x; 2.54x), closing at 0.75 of the range, with Consumer discretionary up 1.6% that day. No cause for either day is in the sources read.
  • Distribution days: one in 25 sessions (13-Oct, (1.0%) on 1.44x; 1.63x), down from 4 at 2-Oct on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's count as September's selling left the window; none since.
  • Up-day against down-day volume: the PMS Per-stock Dashboard reads 1.21 over 20 sessions and 1.74 over 5 (the volume script's 0.76 is the 20D-to-50D volume ratio, mislabelled as an up/down ratio); on my chart-file count, up days averaged 1.16 times down days' volume over 20 sessions, 8 up days against 10 down.
  • The volume trend: the 20D average volume is 0.85 times the 50D on the chart file (the PMS Per-stock Dashboard reads 0.76), against 0.93 on 26-Oct and 0.86 on 27-Oct; 12-Oct (0.18x), 27-Oct (0.19x), 5-Oct (0.20x) and 22-Oct (0.23x) were the four lightest sessions of 25. The 10-session closing range is €1.117 to €1.130, 1.2%, inside a 20-session range of 4.6% (€1.080 to €1.130) and a 63-session range of 11.0%: a volatility contraction without a breakout.

The first session after the UK Budget was heavy and went nowhere. On 29-Oct, Bally's Intralot's first session after the Budget (Athens was shut for Ohi Day on 28-Oct), it was unchanged at €1.117 on 3.40M shares (€3.81m, 1.31x), while Entain had risen 3.6%, Evoke 3.7% and Lottomatica 3.4% on 28-Oct. Hypothesis: sellers used the gambling relief to supply stock at about €1.12, where every close since 23-Oct has sat (€1.117 to €1.122). Against it: 1.31x is not heavy by the 1.5x rule, and the close did not fall.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Generally, over L6M, down days have much higher volume than up days - a fragility indicator IMHO". Partly confirmed. Over the 126 sessions from 7-May to 2-Nov, on the unrounded closes, Bally's Intralot's down days averaged 1.08 times the volume of its up days, and there were 67 down days against 53 up; heavy-volume days split 8 up and 7 down. The chart file's €0.01 rounding turns 49 of those 126 moves into unchanged days and reverses the reading (up days at 1.10 times down days, heavy days 8 against 3), so it cannot settle the point. The two declines since June were one-sided: 16 down days against 4 up from 23-Jun to 23-Jul (up/down volume 0.17), and 16 against 7 from 14-Aug to 18-Sep (0.22). The fragility is real: down days are more frequent and slightly heavier, though not "much higher" in volume, and each decline's selling outlasted the buying.

Precedent: the past year's declines ended quietly, except November 2025's 17.3% Budget-day climax on 5.26x. The lows of 3-Mar, 27-Mar and 23-Jul turned on rebounds of 3.8% to 8.4% on 0.7x to 0.8x the 50D average. The rebound from 18-Sep has run at total up/down volume 1.58, against 3.74 for the July rebound that still failed at €1.176 (on unrounded closes; the rounded chart file gives 1.80 and 13.81), and has made only 5.7% in 30 sessions.

Relative price has slipped back under its relative 200D MA and never confirmed the June SP high. Bally's Intralot's relative price closed 0.2% below its relative 200D MA on 2-Nov, its second close below since it crossed above on 15-Oct (the first was 29-Oct); it is 13.1% below its 52W relative high of 24-Nov-2025, and stood 16.7% below that high on 23-Jun when the SP made its 52W closing high (€1.197).

Confirms a turn up: a close above €1.140 (the 1-Oct high) on 1.5x the 50D average or more. Confirms renewed selling: a close below €1.080 (the 8-Oct low), or two distribution days in ten sessions.

3. RS

The rotation has started to lift Consumer discretionary and Gambling, and Bally's Intralot's Stock RS fell as it did: the stock is being left behind, not carried.

  • Stock RS read 45 on 2-Nov, down 7 points from 52 at the 30-Oct month-end and back below the 50 line, where it read 49 on 29-Oct and 46 on 16-Oct. It is down 1 point over the month (46 on 2-Oct), up 8 over 3 months (37) and up 5 over 12 months (40 at 31-Oct-2025), and has stayed below the 70 line at every reading since 1-Jul.
  • Sector RS for Gambling rose from 12 on 29-Oct to 19 on 2-Nov, still far below the 50 line it crossed in the first half of October (58 at 30-Sep).
  • Industry RS for Consumer discretionary read 33 on 2-Nov, its first reading above the 30 line since 4-Sep (40), after 26 at every Friday from 9 to 30-Oct; Consumer discretionary rose 2.9% in the week, against the Stoxx Europe 600's 1.4%.
  • The order of the turn: on 2-Nov Industry RS (26 to 33) and Sector RS (16 to 19) rose together and Stock RS fell (52 to 45). In September Stock RS and Sector RS moved together without Industry RS: Stock RS jumped from 27 to 49 and Sector RS from 11 to 50 on 25-Sep while Industry RS stayed at 13.
  • The peers in the same rotation: Evoke's Stock RS rose from 76 a month ago to 89, Lottomatica's from 54 to 73 and Betsson's from 18 to 32, while the parent, Bally's Corporation, fell from 70 to 25. Hypothesis: the money returning to Gambling after the 28-Oct Budget is going to the UK names priced for the worst and to the acquisition target, not to the levered acquirer. Test: Stock RS at Friday 6-Nov against Evoke's and Lottomatica's.

4. Consensus by named FY

Bally's Intralot's consensus is one or two analysts deep, and the 72% EPS fall of 30-Sep is a change of contributor, not a cut.

  • EPS: FY2026 €0.02, FY2027 €0.03 and FY2028 €0.04, each from one analyst, unchanged since 30-Sep. On 30-Sep they replaced €0.072, €0.086 and €0.097 (31-Jul), falls of 72%, 65% and 59%. An AlphaSense research report filed on 2-Nov ("Ballys Intralot Guidance and Consensus Forecast Analysis") attributes the three figures to Barclays (22-Sep) and judges the drop "primarily driven by thin analyst coverage rather than a sudden business failure"; Deutsche Bank's model carries a finance charge of about €120m a year on €1.7bn of gross debt at about 7.9%, which is why any broker's EPS sits so far below EBITDA. Before the switch, the like-for-like trend was down: FY2027 EPS fell 14% from February to June 2026.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 €408.5m (one analyst), down 2.7% over 3 months and 3.2% below the company's FY2026 guidance of about €422m; Deutsche Bank sits at €390m, 7.6% below guidance, on the Ohio and Morocco contract expiries (AlphaSense research report, 2-Nov). FY2027 (€452.2m) and FY2028 (€473.4m) have had no contributing analyst since the August month-end.
  • Revenue and the trajectory: no analyst since the August month-end, when FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 revenue had been cut 1.9%, 3.1% and 4.2% over three months, the later years harder, while FY2027 and FY2028 EBITDA held; the implied EBITDA margin rose to 37.5% for FY2027 and 38.3% for FY2028. What it suggests: analysts trust the cost programme (€41m of Bally's International Interactive synergies) more than the top line under 40% Remote Gaming Duty.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "MAJOR NEGATIVE - Just had a guidance downgrade; UK-gambling-related". The sources read do not show a cut to group guidance: FY2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance of about €422m was reaffirmed at the first-quarter results (May 2026) and still stands (AlphaSense research report, 2-Nov). The nearest to a downgrade is the first-half results call of 1-Sep, where the chief executive put FY2026 Interactive adjusted EBITDA at about €280m if UK market consolidation fails, against about €295m under the original consolidation scenario, a €15m downside (earnings review). The one-year delay to the plan came earlier, conceded on the third-quarter 2025 call of 28-Nov-2025, not on 1-Sep (AlphaSense research report, 2-Nov). The sources read show no guidance change since 1-Sep. Test: the third-quarter results on 27-Nov (projected), where the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo sets an alarm on a further EPS cut of 2% or more.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst moved Bally's Intralot's rating, PT or estimates in October: the sell-side is silent, which leaves the Sell Criteria to the SP.

  • Ratings: 2 Buys and nothing else at the 31-Oct month-end, as at 30-Sep. Over 24 months every change in the count came with a change in coverage (1 to 3 analysts), not with the 20.4% fall to March 2026 or the 44.6% rise to June: the ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum.
  • Average PT: €1.405, unchanged since 29-Sep (€1.41 at the July and August month-ends), 25.4% above the close. The gap was 46% at the March 2026 month-end and 15.7% at the May month-end (€1.35 against €1.167), the narrowest in the record back to June 2024, just before the SP topped in June.
  • Net Upgrade Breadth: PT 0% (two analysts), EPS 0% (one), revenue and EBITDA no reading; the last non-zero readings came at the March 2026 month-end, at the SP's low.
  • Where analysts are cutting: none in October. The most cautious stated logic in the sources read is Deutsche Bank's (initiation, 13-Jan-2026): FY2026 EBITDA of €390m on the Ohio and Morocco contract expiries, €120m of finance charges, and net leverage not reaching the 2.5x target until FY2029.
  • The link to the Sell Criterion that reads SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50: with Stock RS now 45, one PT cut by either of the two analysts before 30-Nov would give PT breadth of (50%) and a sell signal at that month-end.

9. Peers

The online peers (Evoke, Flutter, Entain and the Italian Lottomatica) rallied 6% to 11% in the week of the Budget; Bally's Intralot, with 64.8% of its first-half revenue in the UK, was flat. The peer table on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard carries the figures; these are the points they make.

  • SP technicals: in the five sessions to 2-Nov Evoke rose 10.9%, Flutter 9.3% (6.1% on 2-Nov alone), Entain 7.1% and Lottomatica 6.4%, while Bally's Intralot rose 0.2% ((0.2%) on 2-Nov on 0.92x). Flutter and Entain remain 17.4% and 11.6% below their absolute 200D MAs: a relief rally inside a downtrend. Lottomatica is the one cohort peer in an uptrend, 8.3% above its absolute 200D MA with Stock RS 73.
  • Newsflow: the 28-Oct Budget (item 1 (SP and MAs)) lifted gambling on the day (my UK Budget memo, 28-Oct), but its free-play change lands on the bonus cuts Bally's Intralot relies on more than on the peers' cost cuts, such as Flutter's of 20-Oct.
  • Company guidance and communications: Flutter plans to mitigate 40% to 50% of the duty rise (Sector Digest, 10-Oct) against Bally's Intralot's 65% in the second quarter (earnings review); no peer has changed guidance since Betsson's third-quarter report (22-Oct) in the sources read.
  • Sell-side estimates: only Betsson has rising estimates (3-month EPS +3%, revenue +6%, revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +100%); Flutter ((52%)), Entain ((61%)) and Evoke ((50%)) are being cut on revenue breadth. The peers' rally is therefore not estimate-led either.

Evoke's deal spread halved in a week, which says the market is pricing completion into the target, not value into the acquirer. Bally's Intralot is buying Evoke for 0.537 new Bally's Intralot shares per Evoke share, worth 52p at €1.12 (SCCG Management, 23-Oct). Evoke closed at 47.3p on 2-Nov, about 9% below that value, against 43.0p and about 17% on 27-Oct, on the 23-Oct exchange rate. Hypothesis: the Budget removed the risk of a Machine Games Duty shock to William Hill's shops (the deal was struck at a 20% rate), so the completion risk fell. Against it: Evoke may simply have rallied with its UK peers (Entain +7.1%); and a narrow spread brings nearer the day Evoke's holders receive about 11.5% of the enlarged group in new Bally's Intralot shares, which they can sell. Test: the spread at the Gibraltar court hearing and Bally's Intralot's volume in the sessions after completion.

What the peers imply: the sector's turn is under way without Bally's Intralot, whose daily moves correlate only weakly with any stock (CrediaBank, 0.23): the pattern of a stock with its own overhang (leverage and the Evoke share issue), not one waiting for its industry.

What the rules say for Bally's Intralot now, and what decides the next step

  • Finding 1, the rotation passed it by: Consumer discretionary's Industry RS rose from 26 to 33 and the UK online peers rose 6% to 11% in the Budget week, while Bally's Intralot was flat and its Stock RS fell from 52 to 45 (items 1, 3 and 9).
  • Finding 2, the base is tightening without buyers: a 1.2% ten-session range, 20D average volume at 0.85 times its 50D average (0.76 on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard), no heavy-volume day since 15-Oct and relative price back below its relative 200D MA (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • Finding 3, the sell-side is silent and thin: one or two analysts, no change in October, and an EPS consensus that fell 72% on 30-Sep only because Barclays replaced older estimates (items 4, 5 and 6).
  • Tranche 1 (2.0%, bought 26-Jun-2026): sell signal. Three Sell Criteria gave a sell signal at their 30-Oct readings (Industry RS below 30; the relative 200D MA falling over the month; Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70), so the whole holding sells when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link, in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale. It is not among the sales at the Tuesday 3-Nov session.
  • Industry RS below 30 (Friday 6-Nov): Industry RS read 33 on 2-Nov, up 7 points in one session after eight Fridays at 26 or below; holding 30 or more on 6-Nov ends this sell signal, but not the sale.
  • The relative 200D MA (Friday reads): at an unchanged relative price the month's change is (0.27%) at 6-Nov, (0.10%) at 13-Nov and +0.17% at 20-Nov, as weak January and February 2026 readings leave the window; a 3% relative rise clears it at 13-Nov, a 3% relative fall delays it to 27-Nov.
  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (30-Nov): Sector RS 19 would need to rise above 50, or Stock RS 45 above 70: neither is close, so this sell signal holds the sale in place to the 30-Nov month-end whatever the Friday tests do.
  • The nearest new sell test, SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 (30-Nov): Stock RS is 45 today, 7 points lower in a session; one PT cut by either of the two analysts before 30-Nov, most likely around the 27-Nov results, gives a fourth sell signal.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (30-Nov): 13.1% today; the 24-Nov-2025 relative high leaves the window before 30-Nov, cutting the distance to 10.8% at today's relative price, so a sell signal needs an SP of about €0.94 at a flat market, 16% lower.
  • How they interact: a fall of about 16% to €0.94 would add the relative-price test; tranche 1's 30% fall level (€0.8261) lies 26.2% below the €1.120 close, so the relative-price test is reached first.
  • The record: the last time Stock RS turned down from 52 (14-Aug), it reached 27 by 18-Sep as the SP fell 9.9% to its absolute 200D MA.
  • Where Bally's Intralot stands for an add: the first stage, pulling back or basing; it is basing. The Master Dashboard's tests read the third stage, a healthy retest of the 50D MA (6 of 6 tests, SP 1.28% above it), but that MA is falling (0.9% over 21 sessions) and below the 150D MA, so it is not the rising MA your add trigger requires. The Master Dashboard's basing tests read 2 of 4, and its volatility contraction tests 0 of 4. The rules block an add while a Sell Criterion gives a sell signal, and no add trigger is present.
  • Dates and what would change this reading: Friday 6-Nov (Industry RS; the relative 200D MA); third-quarter results on 27-Nov (projected), where the memo's alarms are UK online growth below 8% and a further EPS cut of 2% or more; the 30-Nov month-end; Evoke's completion. A close above €1.140 on heavy volume with Stock RS back above 50 would make the base a breakout; a close below €1.080 would make it a breakdown.

Richard decides.

CVS Group

Middle Innings Core Investment · 2.5% of capital in 2 tranches (tranche 1, 1.0%, bought 22-Jul-2026; tranche 2, 1.5%, bought 4-Aug-2026) · readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

On 2-Nov CVS Group closed above its 7-Sep high (1,328p) for the first time, at 1,360p, its highest close since 25-Feb-2026 and 4.0% below its 52W closing high (1,416p, 18-Feb). The lower high flagged in the 28-Oct note has been cleared; the 12-month range of 1,114p to 1,416p has not.

  • Over 12 months CVS Group is up 6.9%; over 3 months 6.2%, against Healthcare's 7.6% and the Stoxx Europe 600's (1.2%); over 1 month 19.3%, against 8.6% and 2.9%.
  • Of CVS Group's 18.3% rise over 20 sessions, 15.5 points were its own once the market's part, at CVS Group's beta of 1.12, is taken out; that own part is 2.5 times its usual 20-session move. Of the week's 4.3% rise, 2.8 points were its own, within its usual weekly move.

The legs of the move, with the industry and peers beside each:

  • 7-Sep to 2-Oct, 1,328p to 1,140p, (14.2%): CVS Group's own fall, on its 24-Sep results ((6.2%) that day on 2.77x its 50D average volume, against Healthcare (0.5%)). Pets at Home fell 1.3% and Vimian rose 3.2% over the same days.
  • 2 to 20-Oct, 1,140p to 1,323p, +16.1%: CVS Group's own move, leading Healthcare (12 to 20-Oct +11.8% against Healthcare's +2.5%, 28-Oct note); Healthcare's Industry RS followed from 23-Oct.
  • 20 to 22-Oct, 1,323p to 1,299p, (1.8%): a pause under the 7-Sep high, with a distribution day on 21-Oct while Healthcare rose 0.5%; a second came on 26-Oct, at 1,304p, while Healthcare rose 0.8%.
  • 22-Oct to 2-Nov, 1,299p to 1,360p, +4.7%: in the week to 2-Nov CVS Group rose 4.3% against Healthcare's 1.2%, Pets at Home's 5.8% and Vimian's 3.5%, while the hospital cohort fell (Diagnostyka (4.2%), Terveystalo (3.2%)). On 2-Nov alone CVS Group rose 2.5% while Healthcare fell 0.1% and its cohort 0.6%.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with CVS Group's announcements on Investegate (read 3-Nov). No CVS Group announcement explains the 2-Nov rise; the cause is not in the sources read.

  • 23-Oct: annual report posted; annual general meeting on 25-Nov-2026 (CVS Group). Last year's meeting, on 18-Nov-2025, carried a trading update.
  • 26 and 30-Oct: the weekly buyback statement and total voting rights (CVS Group); item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high) gives the buyback's share of volume.
  • Ex-dividend on 5-Nov (CVS Group, 30-Sep, as recorded in the 28-Oct note; amount not in the sources read). The chief executive's succession is still open (no successor named in the sources read).

CVS Group's MAs have turned up from the short end: the 5D to 20D, 100D and 150D MAs are rising, the 50D and the absolute 200D MA are still falling, and the 150D MA is still below the absolute 200D MA.

  • The SP is 7.1% above the 50D MA (1,270.2p, down 0.60% over 20 sessions), 10.0% above the 150D (1,236.3p, up 0.52%) and 8.6% above the absolute 200D (1,252.6p, down 0.35%, last rising over a month on 12-Oct).
  • The SP regained all its MAs by 16-Oct on ordinary volume, reversing the 24-Sep break of four of them on 2.77x. At the earlier turn: the July to September advance also regained the long MAs and then failed at 1,328p with the 150D MA below the absolute 200D MA, as now.

On 22-Sep you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO this looks ripe for positive upwards breakout", with two negatives: "the 150D MA is below the 200D MA" and "150D MA is declining". The answer on 2-Nov: the breakout above 1,328p has come, six weeks later and after a 14.2% fall on the results. The second negative has gone (the 150D MA is rising); the first is narrowing (1.3% below the absolute 200D MA, from 2.2% on 19-Oct).

Against your trend-health tests CVS Group passes four of six: the absolute 200D MA rising fails (down 0.35% over 20 sessions); the 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA fails (1.3% below); the 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA passes (2.7% and 1.4% above); the SP above each MA passes; near its 52W high passes (4.0% below 1,416p); away from its 52W low passes (22.1% above 1,114p, 30-Mar).

Verdict: an advance inside a 12-month range that has not yet become a Stage 2 uptrend, because the long MAs are flat to falling. What would settle it: on my calculation, if CVS Group holds 1,360p the absolute 200D MA turns up over 20 sessions by about 13-Nov and the 150D MA crosses above it by about 24-Nov, passing all six tests; at 1,300p the cross still comes by about 25-Nov, as high closes leave the 150D window, but the absolute 200D MA turns up only in January. A close above 1,416p would leave the 12-month range.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Multiples are the volume script's (shares against the 50D average; the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep move none by more than 0.01x). For 2-Nov the dashboard's value-traded basis gives 1.55x and the key-days table 1.43x, against the script's 1.41x.

CVS Group's heavy-volume days have turned from selling to two-way trade to buying: all three from 29-Sep to 2-Oct were down days, the four from 20 to 30-Oct split two and two, and the last two (30-Oct, and 2-Nov on the dashboard's basis) were up days.

  • 29-Sep to 2-Oct: (1.0%) on 2.86x (838,798 shares, £10.4m), closing at the day's low; (1.7%) on 3.84x (1,205,027 shares, £14.7m) on the 30-Sep quarter-end, closing at 0.10 of the range after a 3.2% gap up; (1.7%) on 1.57x (483,336 shares, £5.5m) on 2-Oct, the low.   • 20 to 30-Oct: +2.6% on 1.85x (584,744 shares, £7.7m) on 20-Oct, closing at 0.93; (1.5%) on 1.68x (540,351 shares, £7.0m) on 21-Oct, closing at 0.04; (1.0%) on 1.88x (622,248 shares, £8.1m) on 26-Oct, closing at 0.30 after a 1.0% gap up; +0.9% on 1.52x (526,703 shares, £7.0m) on 30-Oct, closing at 0.65.
  • 2-Nov: +2.5% on 1.41x (498,858 shares, £6.8m), closing at 0.78 of a 1,321p to 1,371p range after a 0.2% gap up.

CVS Group's distribution days fell from 7 to 5 in 25 sessions in a week, and three of the five leave the window by Fri 6-Nov. The five are 29-Sep, 30-Sep, 2-Oct, 21-Oct and 26-Oct; accumulation days also number five (5, 14 and 20-Oct, 30-Oct, 2-Nov). The two October distribution days both came at 1,300p to 1,323p, under the 7-Sep high; the 30-Oct and 2-Nov accumulation days came as CVS Group went through it.

Up-day volume leads over 5 sessions but trails over 20, and the October advance is CVS Group's third-thinnest of seven advances of 8% or more in 12 months.

  • The dashboard's up/down volume ratio is 1.52 over 5 sessions and 0.86 over 20 (0.63 on 26-Oct); the 20D average is 1.15 times the 50D.
  • From 1,140p (2-Oct) to 1,360p (2-Nov), +19.3% in 21 sessions, CVS Group had 15 up days and 6 down days, but the average up day traded 0.89 times the average down day.
  • Precedent: the two thinner advances ended in falls of 14.2% (July to September 2026, 0.70) and 20.6% (July to October 2025, 0.85); the four others ran at 1.07 to 2.01. Hypothesis: the advance rests more on sellers withdrawing than on heavy buying.

Volume rose through the advance: on the dashboard's value basis CVS Group averaged about 0.95x its 50D average from 5 to 16-Oct and about 1.27x from 19-Oct to 2-Nov, with buying and selling both heavier. It regained its 20D and 150D MAs on 14-Oct (1.33x, an accumulation day) and its 50D, 100D and absolute 200D MAs on 16-Oct (1.08x, closing at the day's high); it dipped below the falling 50D MA during 19-Oct (low 1,265p against 1,266.5p, 0.91x) and closed above it, and has not traded back to any MA since.

  • The buyback's part: CVS Group bought 150,000 shares in the week to 23-Oct (CVS Group, 26-Oct), 8.1% of that week's volume on my arithmetic. Shares in issue fell a further 104,615 by 30-Oct (CVS Group, total voting rights), about 5% of that week's volume. About £41.6m of the up to £50m programme is spent (3,332,850 shares at 1,248.1p), leaving about £7m, three to four weeks at the recent pace.
  • How declines ended: the 17-Nov-2025 low was followed by a 3.3x day on the annual general meeting trading update; the 2-Oct-2026 low by a 1.49x accumulation day on 5-Oct and then six sessions of 0.48x to 1.14x, sellers withdrawing with no event.

CVS Group's relative price is 11.2% below its 52W high, against 4.0% for the SP: relative price has not confirmed the SP's high. The 1,360p close is the SP's highest since 25-Feb-2026; relative price (747.2) is only its highest since 19-Mar-2026, as the market has risen since February, and its 52W high (841.7, 17-Feb-2026) stays in the window until February 2027. Relative price has closed above its relative 200D MA since 22-Oct, held it on 26 and 29-Oct (0.1% and 0.03% above), and is now 3.4% above it.

Peers' volume: on 2-Nov Pets at Home rose 4.4% on 0.55x its 50D average and Vimian 0.9% on 0.45x, so CVS Group's buying that day was heavier than its pet-care peers'; the cohort's October heavy days came with small or opposite moves.

Confirms the breakout: a second close above 1,328p on 1.5x or more and no new distribution day this week. Confirms a failure: a close back below 1,328p on heavy volume, the pattern of 21 and 26-Oct.

3. RS

All three of CVS Group's RS readings rose together in the weeks to 23 and 30-Oct and again on 2-Nov, and CVS Group's Stock RS of 60 is its highest since 20-Feb-2026.

  • Stock RS: CVS Group's Stock RS is 60 on 2-Nov, from 58 at the 30-Oct month-end, 53 on 23-Oct, 32 on 16-Oct and 25 on 9-Oct; it was 45 three months ago (4-Aug) and 60 twelve months ago (31-Oct-2025). It is 10 points above the line of 50 and 10 points below the line of 70.
  • Sector RS: the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes is 67, from 66 at 30-Oct, 19 on 16-Oct and 32 at 30-Sep; it is 17 points above its line of 50.
  • Industry RS: Healthcare's Industry RS is 86, unchanged since 30-Oct and the highest in 13 months of readings, from 46 on 16-Oct and 20 at the 31-Jul month-end; it is 56 points above its line of 30.

The order of the turn: CVS Group first, from 25 on 9-Oct to 32 on 16-Oct while its Sector RS fell to 19, then Healthcare and the sector together from 23-Oct with no CVS Group news. The Healthcare rotation has carried CVS Group since 23-Oct, but CVS Group's 2-Nov rise of 2.5%, against Healthcare's (0.1%) that day, was its own.

The precedent is a warning with a difference. CVS Group's last run of Stock RS mostly at 60 or more (19-Dec-2025 to 20-Feb-2026, with a dip to 51 at the 30-Jan month-end) ended in a 21.3% fall to 30-Mar and a Stock RS of 31; but then its Sector RS was 27 to 56 and Healthcare's Industry RS 26 to 46, while now Sector RS and Industry RS are both high.

What would confirm it: Stock RS above 60 and Healthcare's Industry RS above 70 on Fri 6-Nov. What would refute it: Stock RS falling back below 50, as it fell 17 points in the week to 25-Sep.

4. Consensus by named FY

CVS Group's FY2027 is its year to June 2027; figures are FactSet's consensus on 1-Nov-2026, in pence and £m.

CVS Group rose 19.3% in the month to 2-Nov with its estimates flat: the SP led and the estimates did not follow.

  • FY2027 EPS is 95.2p: +0.7% over 1 month, (0.4%) over 3 months, +0.3% over 6 months and (0.5%) over 12 months; it was 107.1p in October 2024, so it has fallen 11.1% in 24 months, all of it before March 2026.
  • FY2027 revenue (£750.6m) and EBITDA (£150.6m) moved 0.5% or less over 1 and 3 months; they stand 5.3% and 6.4% above FY2026's actuals (earnings review).

Analysts have cut the later years faster than FY2027 over three months, lowering the trajectory, but the cutting stopped in October.

  • FY2028 EPS is 103.3p: +0.2% over 1 month, (1.4%) over 3 months, (2.1%) over 12. FY2029 EPS is 111.3p: +0.1% over 1 month, (4.8%) over 3 months, (2.8%) over 6. FY2029 EBITDA is £165.5m, (2.0%) over 3 months and (4.9%) over 6.
  • Implied EPS growth is 8.5% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 7.7% from FY2028 to FY2029; revenue grows 4.5% and 4.0%; EBITDA 5.0% and 4.6%. EPS growing nearly twice as fast as EBITDA fits the buyback, the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's "capital-allocation case" (1-Oct).
  • The implied EBITDA margin, 20.1% to 20.3% for FY2027 to FY2029 against 19.9% in FY2026, shows analysts expect almost no margin gain.

The link to the memo's tests: the memo's modal case is UK like-for-like growth of 2.5% for the first half of FY2027, with an alarm below 1.5%, an upgrade trigger at 3% or more and invalidation below 0%. The first evidence is the trading update CVS Group gave at last year's annual general meeting (18-Nov-2025); this year's meeting is on 25-Nov (CVS Group, 23-Oct). Test: like-for-like growth at or above 3% on 25-Nov would give analysts a reason to follow the SP; below 1.5% would leave a 19.3% rise without estimate support.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

The SP has run towards the PTs: CVS Group's average PT of 1,626p is 19.6% above the 2-Nov close, narrower than at any month-end since August 2023. At the 30-Oct month-end the gap was 22.5%; the narrowest month-end gaps before it were 21.0% (May 2025) and 13.3% to 18.1% in January to August 2023; the gap was 26.0% at the August 2026 month-end and about 44% at the 2-Oct low (1,640p against 1,140p).

  • Precedent: after the 21.0% gap of May 2025 CVS Group traded flat, then rose to 1,423.9p on the Competition and Markets Authority's provisional decision in October 2025: news, not the gap, set the direction. A second precedent: after month-end gaps of 13% to 18% in 2023, CVS Group fell from 2,119.6p at the August 2023 month-end to 974.9p at the March 2024 month-end; a narrow gap has preceded both a rise and a large fall.
  • The average PT fell from 1,640p to 1,626p on 12-Oct. After the 24-Sep results Deutsche Bank kept Buy and 1,700p and Jefferies Hold and 1,400p (earnings review), 2.9% above today's close.

Ratings follow valuation more than momentum: CVS Group's Buys rose after falls and slipped after rises. Buys rose from 7 to 8 at the March 2025 month-end near 1,004p, slipped to 7 at the February 2026 high, and rose to 9 at the July 2026 month-end after the 23-Jun low. The count is 9 Buys and 2 others of 11, unchanged since the September month-end; coverage has risen from 9 to 11 in 24 months. If the pattern holds, the next rating change after a 19.3% rise is more likely a downgrade than an upgrade.

Net Upgrade Breadth splits: analysts raise revenue and cut PTs and EPS.

  • Revenue: +33% at the 30-Oct month-end (3 of 9 raising, none cutting), from +22% at 30-Sep and (12%) at July and August, the highest in the 14 month-end readings since September 2025.
  • PT: (18%) at 30-Oct (none raising, 2 of 11 cutting), from 0% at 30-Sep and +20% at July and August, the lowest since the February 2025 month-end ((22%)).
  • EPS (FY2027): (10%) at 31-Oct (1 raising, 2 cutting, of 10), from (40%) at 30-Sep; EPS breadth has been negative or zero at 11 of the last 12 month-ends. EBITDA (FY2027): +11% (1 of 9 raising).
  • Who is cutting, and why: the two analysts cutting PTs and the two cutting FY2027 EPS are not named in the sources read, and their stated logic is not in the sources read (the earnings review names only Deutsche Bank and Jefferies, neither cutting; AlphaSense was unreachable). Hypothesis, from the earnings review: revenue rises with acquisitions (Australia, a two-site UK practice for £15.0m) while EPS and PTs are cut for slower UK like-for-like growth (about 1.5% in the second half of FY2026) and the open chief executive succession.

The link to the sell tests: PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero is already one half of the test "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50"; only CVS Group's Stock RS (60) keeps it from a sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end.

9. Peers

CVS Group trades with the pet-care stocks and with UK-listed stocks, not with its hospital and clinic cohort: its rebound since 2-Oct matches Pets at Home's and Vimian's, both at 52W highs on 2-Nov, and also Big Yellow's and Experian's.

  • On my calculation, CVS Group's daily moves over 63 sessions correlate 0.39 with Pets at Home and 0.38 with Vimian, against 0.10 to 0.19 with Spire Healthcare, Diagnostyka and Aevis Victoria and (0.03) with Terveystalo; neither pet-care stock is on the page.
  • On the same basis (daily SP moves, 63 sessions to 2-Nov), CVS Group correlates more closely with three UK-listed stocks outside healthcare: Big Yellow 0.49, Burford Capital 0.48 and AG Barr 0.45. Since the 2-Oct low Big Yellow is +19.4% and Experian +23.0%, as far as CVS Group's +19.3%, so the rebound fits a UK-listed move as well as a pet-care one.
  • Since the 2-Oct low CVS Group is +19.3%, Pets at Home +20.3% and Vimian +17.9%. On 2-Nov Pets at Home rose 4.4% and Vimian 0.9%, both closing at 52W highs; CVS Group closed 4.0% below its own. The cause of Pets at Home's 2-Nov rise is not in the sources read.
  • Who turned first: in the 7-Sep to 2-Oct decline CVS Group fell 14.2% while Pets at Home fell 1.3% and Vimian rose 3.2%. The decline was CVS Group's own, on its 24-Sep results; the rebound is shared with the pet-care group. Hypothesis: investors are buying veterinary and pet-care exposure as a group, and CVS Group, the laggard after its results, has caught up. Rival hypothesis: a UK-listed move: from 7-Sep to 2-Oct AG Barr fell 10.1%, Experian 12.5% and Burford Capital 15.9% as CVS Group fell 14.2%, though the 24-Sep results day ((6.2%) against Healthcare's (0.5%)) was CVS Group's own. Test: whether CVS Group holds its gains if Pets at Home or Vimian falls back, and whether it moves with the UK-listed stocks when they diverge from the pet-care stocks.

The hospital and clinic cohort is split, and its strong members are pausing, which matters for the Sector RS test at 30-Nov.

  • SP technicals: Medicover (Stock RS 87, +10.2% over 1 month) and Spire Healthcare (81, 0.4% below its 52W high, priced on its offer) are strong; Diagnostyka (14) and Terveystalo (5) are below their absolute 200D MAs. In the wider sector, Attendo fell 4.7% in the week and 8.4% in the month (Stock RS 89 a month ago, 75 now), and Ambea 0.8% in the week.
  • Sell-side estimates: 3-month EPS revisions run from Spire Healthcare's +35% to Terveystalo's (17%), and Medicover's revenue revision is (9%) with breadth (55%); CVS Group's (1%) EPS and +1% revenue sit between.
  • Newsflow and guidance: Terveystalo cut its 2026 adjusted operating profit guidance to €110m to €120m from €120m to €140m on 20-Oct (Sector Digest, 28-Oct) and fell 5.1% that day as CVS Group rose 2.6%. Spire Healthcare's board recommended a 250p cash offer (Sector Digest, 22-Oct). CVS Group has given no guidance since its 24-Sep results.
  • What it implies: the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes (67) rests on Medicover, Spire Healthcare and the Nordic care operators; Attendo's fall is the first sign of the strong members weakening. A sector reversal would bring the Sector RS test (below 50 with Stock RS below 70) back at 30-Nov without any move in CVS Group.

On 2-Nov the UK-listed stocks whose daily moves track CVS Group's all rose (AG Barr +2.2%, Experian +1.2%, Big Yellow +1.4%, Burford Capital +5.7%, against the Stoxx Europe 600's +0.4%): part of CVS Group's 2.5% was a UK-listed day.

What the rules say for CVS Group now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • A breakout not yet confirmed: CVS Group's 2-Nov close of 1,360p cleared its 7-Sep high on 1.41x its 50D average volume (1.55x on the dashboard's value basis), with no company news and moving with Pets at Home, Vimian and the UK-listed stocks that track it (item 9, Peers); the 28-Oct note's 1.5x test is met on one basis only.
  • A sale on a lagging reading while the rest improves: the rules sell CVS Group on its falling relative 200D MA, which at a flat relative price stays a sell signal until about the Fri 15-Jan-2027 reading, while its RS readings, relative price and distribution-day count have all moved away from their lines.
  • Price ahead of estimates: FY2027 EPS rose 0.7% in the month CVS Group rose 19.3%, PT Net Upgrade Breadth is (18%) and the average PT only 19.6% above the SP; the 25-Nov annual general meeting is the first test.

The whole holding: no MA SL applies to a Middle Innings Core Investment. The relative 200D MA fell 1.54% over the month to the Fri 30-Oct reading, a sell signal; under your one-off in/out link both tranches (1.0% and 1.5%) are sold when CVS Group's replacement is bought, in the APM's order of sale.

The nearest sell tests and their pace:

  • The 200D MA of relative price (sell signal now): its monthly fall was 2.05% at 5-Aug, 1.09% at 5-Oct, 1.62% at 26-Oct and 1.47% at 2-Nov. On my calculation, at a flat market ending it needs 1,557p (+14.5%) by Fri 20-Nov or 1,505p (+10.6%) by Fri 27-Nov; at a flat relative price it turns up about 14-Jan-2027, a week earlier than in the 30-Oct note.
  • SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 (30-Nov): PT breadth is (18%), so a Stock RS of 49 or lower, 11 points below today's 60, gives a second sell signal.
  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (30-Nov): with Stock RS at 60, the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes (67) decides it; it fell 40 points in the week to 25-Sep.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (30-Nov): at a flat market a close below about 1,149p, 15.5% lower.
  • How they interact: a repeat of September's fall (14.2% in 19 sessions, Stock RS 54 to 26) at a flat market would take the SP to about 1,167p: a Stock RS below 50 would give a second sell signal (with PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero), relative price would sit about 23.8% below its 52W high, short of the 25% line, and the Sector RS test would add a third only if the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes also fell below 50, as it did in September.

The record: both tranches were bought with the relative 200D MA test already met (negative since 18-Feb-2026); CVS Group's last run of Stock RS at 60 or more ended in a 21.3% fall (item 3 (RS), RS).

The next reading day and the add:

  • Fri 6-Nov: reads Industry RS (86) and the relative 200D MA, whose sell signal stands at any plausible price; the 5-Nov ex-dividend date lowers the SP by the dividend.
  • Adds: the rules block an add, because a Sell Criterion gives a sell signal.
  • Where CVS Group stands for an add: none of the three. It is not pulling back or basing, not retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern, and not healthily retesting or breaking out of one; it is advancing, 7.1% above a falling 50D MA. The Master Dashboard agrees (pulling back 2 of 4 tests, basing 2 of 4, healthy retest 2 of 6, volatility contraction patterns 0 of 4).
  • Dates: buyback about £7m left (three to four weeks); annual general meeting 25-Nov; 30-Nov month-end; Competition and Markets Authority price lists 22-Dec-2026; first-half results 4-Mar-2027 (projected).
  • For your ruling: On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "It means HC might be catching a bid now… Prioritise…! ASAP!", and listed CVS among the "Secondary" sell signals. CVS Group is the portfolio's only Healthcare holding moving with that bid; where it falls in the order of sale is yours to set.
  • What would change this reading: a second close above 1,328p on 1.5x volume, then like-for-like growth of 3% or more on 25-Nov, would strengthen the case for placing CVS Group last in the order of sale; a close below 1,299p (the 22-Oct low) on heavy volume would mark the breakout as failed.

Richard decides.

Helios Towers

Helios Towers has closed below its 20D, 50D, 100D and 150D MAs and has twice traded below its absolute 200D MA without closing below it.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Helios Towers against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes. +2.64% over 21 sessions, +0.50% over five.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D MA: passes. 209.18p against 202.79p, 3.1% above.
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D MA: partly. 2.9% above the absolute 200D MA, 0.3% below the 150D.
  • The SP above each: partly. 204.8p is above only the absolute 200D MA (+1.0%); it is 1.8% below the 50D, 0.5% below the 100D, 2.1% below the 150D and 4.0% below the 20D.
  • Near its 52W high: fails. 16.5% below the 245.39p closing high; relative price 21.5% below its own.
  • Away from its low: passes. 35.1% above the 151.6p 52W closing low.
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began (20-Jul): partly present. The five sessions to 20-Oct ((6.0%)) were the largest; 22-Oct ((3.8%)) the second-largest day, after 31-Jul ((4.4%)). Neither followed good news.
  • Wider swings: absent. The average daily range was 2.66% over the last 10 sessions, against 3.10% in the 10 before and 2.85% over 60.
  • Lower lows: absent on closes. 204.0p (30-Oct) is above 195.3p (15-Sep); Monday's 200.6p intraday low is the lowest since 18-Sep.
  • More down days than up days: present. Eight down, five up since 13-Oct.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: present. The break above the late-June closes came on 0.44x (7-Oct); the 13-Oct high reversed on 2.54x on 14-Oct.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: present until Monday. From 27 to 30-Oct the lows slid along the absolute 200D MA (1.6% to (0.3%) from it); Monday's heavy-volume recovery from below it is the first sign of a bounce.
  • Verdict: not a broken trend, not yet a healthy retest. The absolute 200D MA is rising, the closing lows are higher and the selling was light; three topping flags are present and the bounce is one modest day old. Settled by Thursday's update and the volume after it: a second up day on 1.5x or more, closing in its top third above about 209p, confirms a healthy retest; a close below the absolute 200D MA, then below 195.3p, makes it a broken trend.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: two mechanical-volume days, 31-Aug and 18-Sep, sit in Helios Towers' 50D average; the ratios below (a day's volume as a multiple of the 50D average) leave them out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's own figure in brackets.

Monday 2-Nov was Helios Towers' first heavy-volume day in 13 sessions, and it was a test of the absolute 200D MA that buyers won, narrowly: a gap up sold into, a break below the MA bought back.

  • Helios Towers traded 6.1M shares (£12.4m) on 2-Nov, 2.51x (2.42x) its 50D average and its heaviest day since the 14-Oct distribution day.
  • The shape of the day: Helios Towers opened 1.5% higher at 207.0p, which was the day's high; sold down to 200.6p, 1.1% below the rising absolute 200D MA (202.79p) and its lowest intraday price since 18-Sep; and closed at 204.8p, +0.4%, at 0.66 of the day's range, just short of the top third.
  • The heavy volume was Helios Towers' own: Cellnex traded at 1.0x its 50D average, Inwit at 0.7x and Rai Way at 0.9x, while Airtel Africa rebounded 3.2% on 0.85x.
  • Two readings: buyers absorbing supply at the absolute 200D MA ahead of Thursday (the close back above it; an accumulation day); or holders selling into the gap up (the open was the high; +0.4% on 2.5x). The cause is not in the sources read. Confirmed by a follow-through up day above 209p (the 50D and 150D MAs) on above-average volume; refuted by a close below 202.8p, the absolute 200D MA.

Monday's day resembles the first day of Helios Towers' November 2025 turn, which needed a second, stronger day to confirm it.

  • On 21-Nov-2025, the session after the 151.8p low, Helios Towers rose 0.5% on 2.5x, closing at 0.72 of its range; it confirmed on 25-Nov (+1.7% on 3.2x) and rose 32.7% to 26-Feb. The 23-Mar-2026 low ended with +1.7% on 2.7x the next day, closing at 0.96 of its range; the 20-Jul low with +5.2% on 0.9x.
  • Monday's +0.4% on 2.5x, the session after the 204.0p closing low, matches the first half of that pattern; the second half has not come.

The October decline was light, and Monday tipped its volume balance to buyers. From 15 to 30-Oct Helios Towers had no heavy-volume day in 12 sessions (0.85x its 50D average on average without the mechanical-volume days; 0.79x on the dashboard's basis). Over the decline from 13-Oct, average up-day volume is 1.11 times average down-day volume including Monday (0.83 to 30-Oct; both figures count the flat close of 29-Oct as an up day, as the volume script does), against 0.52 in the February-to-March 2026 decline, but 0.97 in the 46-session May-to-July decline, which light selling did not stop.

Helios Towers' distribution days sit at the top of the advance, not in the decline: three in 25 sessions (30-Sep, 9-Oct, 14-Oct), falling to two on 4-Nov when 30-Sep leaves the window, unless a new one comes. Its accumulation days are 29-Sep, 12-Oct and 2-Nov. Up/down volume is 1.04 over 20 sessions and 2.35 over 5 (the dashboard's figures), the five-session figure carried by Monday alone.

Helios Towers' relative price confirmed the SP's low: it is 21.5% below its 52W high (14-May) and at its lowest since 15-Sep, while the SP is 16.5% below its own high. Relative price slipped on Monday although the SP rose, because the market rose as much. It crossed below its relative 200D MA on 22-Oct and is 4.5% below it; that relative 200D MA is still rising (+1.70% over the month), more slowly each week (1.82% at 26-Oct).

For Helios Towers, a turn is confirmed by a second up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more, closing in its top third and above about 209p; the decline continues on a close below the absolute 200D MA on above-average volume, the first such close since 14-Apr-2025.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts cut Helios Towers' EPS for all three years after the 30-Jul results while holding revenue and EBITDA, and nothing has moved since: Thursday's update meets estimates that have not been raised for two months.

  • FY2026 EPS ($0.0892) is down 5.7% over 3 months and 19.1% over 12; FY2027 EPS ($0.1384) down 5.2% and 7.9%; FY2028 EPS ($0.1669) down 7.4% over 3 months. All three cuts came at the 31-Aug month-end; each is within 0.4% since.
  • FY2027 revenue ($1,051m) is up 5.2% over 6 months and flat over 3; FY2027 EBITDA ($596.7m) up 4.4% and flat. FY2028 revenue ($1,120m) and EBITDA ($646.7m) are up 4.2% and 3.9% over 6 months.
  • Implied growth: FY2026 to FY2027, revenue +10.9%, EBITDA +12.7%, EPS +55%; FY2027 to FY2028, revenue +6.6%, EBITDA +8.4%, EPS +20.6%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 55.9% to 56.8% and 57.7%: analysts model operating leverage, then let only part of it reach EPS.
  • Why EPS fell while EBITDA held: Deutsche Bank (29-Oct, "Towering ahead - thoughts before the quarter") writes that "bottom-line projections face pressure because of non-cash foreign exchange losses and rising financing expenses". That is the counter-case in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Oct): the growth does not reach the shareholder. The memo's re-rating branch needs brokers' EPS raised alongside EBITDA at two prints.
  • The SP leads, not the estimates: revenue consensus followed the 14.3% jump of 7-May; the August EPS cut came during the 20-Jul to 13-Oct rise, so October's fall is not estimate-led.
  • Data limit: month-end consensus is stored only to 31-Aug, so "flat since" may be carried forward. Test: EPS against EBITDA changes after 5-Nov.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Helios Towers' sell-side has gone quiet rather than negative: no analyst raised or cut EPS, EBITDA or revenue in the latest readings, one has cut a PT, and all six still rate it Buy.

  • Ratings: six Buys of six; every analyst has rated Helios Towers Buy at every month-end since May 2025, through the 23% fall to July, so its ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum. Coverage fell from 7 to 6 in August. Deutsche Bank kept its Buy (29-Oct) and Morgan Stanley its Overweight (30-Oct). With no one left to upgrade, the first downgrade would be news.
  • Net Upgrade Breadth: EPS, EBITDA and revenue 0% (none of six moving), against +67%, +83% and +67% at the 30-Sep month-end; PT (17%), one cut of six, against +50%. The drop came in two steps: on 13-Oct, the day of the 225.6p SP high, PT Net Upgrade Breadth fell from +50% to (17%) and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth from +67% to +17%; on 21-Oct revenue Net Upgrade Breadth reached 0%. It cannot be the 30-Jul results revisions leaving the 100-day count, because 30-Jul is only 93 days before 31-Oct; earlier raises have left the count with no new raise replacing them, and no analyst has cut anything beyond the one PT. Why the raises stopped as the SP peaked is not in the sources read.
  • Where analysts are cutting: one PT. Helios Towers' average PT slipped from 290.8p (29-Sep) to 287.3p (22-Oct) and reads 288.2p (28-Oct). Who cut, and why, is not in the sources read; the named targets in the research filed are unchanged (Jefferies base case 300p and upside case 350p, 19-Oct; Deutsche Bank 285p, 21-Jul). The earlier cuts were to EPS, at the August month-end, for the reason Deutsche Bank gives in item 4 (Consensus by named FY).

Hypothesis: a wide gap marks a buying point (about 22% at the September 2025 and January 2026 month-ends, before rises of 15% and 16%). Against: Helios Towers' PTs ratchet up after SP jumps and do not fall with it, so the gap partly reflects stale targets.

Newsflow: three customer-aligned tower options are building in Helios Towers' markets. The Key Question research filed on 1 and 2-Nov names Esengo Towers, Vodacom and Orange's joint venture formed in April 2026 to build about 1,000 rural towers in the DRC for about $179m; Indus Towers building in Zambia and Uganda with Bharti Airtel as anchor (Emkay Research, 29-Oct); and MTN's acquisition of IHS Towers in 2026, an operator taking its tower landlord in-house. Each bears on how much of the operators' capex becomes Helios Towers tenancies. Test: any comment on competition or captive builds in Thursday's update.

Outside the cohort, the links are weak (0.21 to 0.24, all London-listed); on Monday Ashmore, the emerging-markets manager, rose 1.7% on 3.5x beside Helios Towers' 2.5x: a hint of an emerging-markets bid, not a link to rely on.

What the peers imply: Helios Towers' next move depends on its own 5-Nov update and on Airtel Africa's spending, not on the European towers; its Sector RS, and so one of its Sell Criteria, depends on the European towers.

  • The finding that matters most: Thursday's update (5-Nov) can fire a fundamental exit. The memo's Invalidation test is organic tenancy additions below 600 in the third or fourth quarter of 2026.   • What the rules say for each tranche today: hold all three. Helios Towers is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone; none gave a sell signal at its 30-Oct reading, and no tranche is near its 30% fall.
  • The nearest sell tests:
    • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70: missed by one Stock RS point at 30-Oct; Stock RS fell 2 points since, 8 in a week, 18 since 16-Oct. Unless Helios Towers beats the market by enough to lift Stock RS to 70 by 30-Nov, with Sector RS still below 50, the month-end reading gives a sell signal.
    • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high: 21.5% below (17.1% at 30-Sep, 19.5% at 26-Oct). At a flat market a close at or below 195.78p on 30-Nov, 4.4% below 204.8p and about one usual weekly move, gives a sell signal.
    • The relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month: +1.70%; at an unchanged relative price about +1.5% on Fri 6-Nov and first flat at the Fri 4-Dec reading. A 5% relative fall from Tuesday brings that to Fri 27-Nov.
    • How they interact: one bad reaction to Thursday's update, about 5% against the market, would give all three a sell signal by the 30-Nov month-end: relative price past its line, Stock RS further below 70, and the relative 200D MA turning flat on 27-Nov. Under your one-off in/out link any of these sells Helios Towers only when its replacement is bought.

Richard decides.

IMCD's SP has recovered its whole October pullback and closed at €104.45 on 2-Nov, 0.2% below its €104.7 closing high of 14-Oct, three sessions before its nine-month results on Thu 5-Nov.

  • Over 12 months IMCD's SP is up 19.8% (from €87.22 on 5-Nov-2025); over 3 months 6.0% (from €98.52 on 5-Aug), against (1.1%) for its industry, Industrials and capital goods, and (1.2%) for the Stoxx Europe 600; over the month 9.7%, against (1.4%) for its industry and 2.9% for the Stoxx Europe 600

  • 26-Oct to 2-Nov, €100.15 to €104.45, +4.3%: mostly IMCD's own. The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.4% and the industry 1.5%; Brenntag rose 1.1% and Azelis 2.0%; beta (0.58) explains 0.8 points, leaving 3.5 points IMCD's own, 0.7 times its usual weekly move (5.2%)

The cause is not in the sources read; the timing, three sessions before the results, fits buying ahead of them

Basis: two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and 18-Sep) inside the 50D average lower each ratio by about 3%; the ratios below leave them out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets.

  • The five sessions of the rise (27-Oct to 2-Nov, +4.3%) averaged 0.95x the 50D average volume; the eight sessions of the pullback (15 to 26-Oct, (4.3%)) averaged 1.18x, or 1.03x without 15-Oct
  • 2-Nov, the strongest day of the rise: +2.2% on 104,010 shares (€10.9m), 0.85x (0.82x), closing at 98% of the day's range. It is not an accumulation day, which needs 1.25x or more on an up day (your 28 to 31-Oct definition)

Last week's hypothesis, that IMCD's Stock RS fall was a base effect from the late-July results gains leaving the 3-month window, holds: Stock RS stopped falling once those gains had left, and rose 2 points as the SP rose 2.2% on 2-Nov.

4. Consensus by named FY

IMCD's consensus has not moved for five weeks on any line or year, into the results; the SP's 32.5% advance since 30-Jun has run far ahead of the estimates.

  • EPS over 1, 3, 6 and 12 months: FY2026 (€5.63) 0%, +1.5%, +5.7%, (6.1%); FY2027 (€6.07) 0%, +0.7%, +5.1%, (8.3%); FY2028 (€6.62) 0%, +0.9%, +5.7%, (15.4%). Revenue and EBITDA follow the same pattern
  • Over six months all three years rose at the same pace: analysts lifted the level after the first-half results, not the trajectory. Over twelve months FY2028 was cut most ((15.4%) for EPS against (6.1%) for FY2026): the trajectory was cut and has not been restored

Consensus EPS grows faster than EBITDA in every year, and the research filed this week names a risk to that gap. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.7% for EPS, 5.2% for EBITDA and 4.4% for revenue; from FY2027 to FY2028, 9.2%, 5.9% and 5.2%. The implied EBITDA margin barely rises (11.4%, 11.5%, 11.6%). EPS outgrowing EBITDA by 2.5 to 3.3 points a year needs lower costs below the operating line. The AlphaSense key-question research of 2-Nov records that refinancing IMCD's €300m 2.125% notes due March 2027 at current market rates will raise its annual interest charges, a cost not yet explicitly modelled in the sell-side reports it read (Jefferies: net finance costs €80.2m in 2025, from €45.1m).

On the operating line, the brokers sit above consensus. Jefferies puts FY2027 operating profit (EBITA) at €596.7m against consensus of €570.8m, on 5% organic revenue growth, and Deutsche Bank's FY2026 operating profit (EBITA) forecast (€560m) was 5% above consensus (research of 2-Nov, citing both). Deutsche Bank and Morgan Stanley warn that consensus may be extrapolating an unusually favourable pricing environment while underlying industrial demand remains subdued, and Morgan Stanley (30-Oct) adds that demand is highly unpredictable for distributors (research of 2-Nov).

The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Oct) makes 5-Nov its decisive test: nine-month organic gross profit growth below 0%, a group gross margin below 24.5%, or a conversion margin below 42% in the second half moves its view to neutral and park. The second quarter printed +7.3%, 25.2% and 44.9% (earnings review, 10-Aug). For the case holding: Azelis's third quarter (22-Oct) printed organic gross profit +8.5% with "strong pricing" (Deutsche Bank, 22-Oct), and Morgan Stanley (20-Oct) records technical chemical prices rebounding in September. Against it: the research of 2-Nov finds specialty distributors such as IMCD follow Brenntag's pricing turns with a one-quarter lag, and that in past cycles "rapid price de-escalation in Europe has squeezed margins"; IMCD's gross margin in the third quarter of 2025 was 24.3% (Investing.com, 6-Nov-2025), below the memo's bar; and IMCD's fourth quarters of 2023 and 2025 missed consensus (fourth-quarter 2025 operating profit 8% below), so the 5-Nov comments on October trading matter as much as the quarter.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

IMCD's average PT (€116.0) is now only 11.1% above the SP, the narrowest gap since the September 2024 month-end (6.5%). The last time the gap was this narrow, at the April 2026 month-end (12.2%), IMCD made its 4-May top and fell 21.9% to 30-Jun. The average PT fell 35% from March 2025 to March 2026, rose 8.4% to August, and has not moved since. The latest PT actions in the sources read are all of 29-Jul (earnings review): Deutsche Bank €123 from €122 (Buy); Jefferies €122 (Buy); ING €108 (Buy); Morgan Stanley €105 from €95 (Equal-weight). IMCD's SP is now within 0.5% of Morgan Stanley's PT and 3.4% of ING's.

IMCD's ratings follow the gap to the PT more than the SP's momentum. Buys peaked at 14 at the September and October 2025 month-ends, with the PT 65% and 56% above the SP; fell to 9 of 14 by April 2026 as the gap closed to 12%; and a Sell appeared in May after the 4-May high. Since July the count has held at 11 Buys, 3 others and 1 Sell, from 15 analysts. Hypothesis: with the gap at 11%, the next rating changes are more likely to be downgrades on valuation than upgrades, unless 5-Nov lifts the PTs. Test: PT changes in the week after 5-Nov.

Net Upgrade Breadth has run down to zero or near it on every line without a wave of cuts: raises are ageing out of the 100-day window.

  • FY2027 EPS: +7% (2 raising, 1 cutting, of 15), from +40% at 30-Sep
  • FY2027 EBITDA: 0% (1 raising, 1 cutting, of 13), from +54%
  • FY2027 revenue: 0% (1 raising, 1 cutting, of 14), from +43%
  • PT: +7% (1 raising, none cutting, of 15), from +47%. Buys flat at 11

Where analysts are cutting: one analyst has cut each of FY2027 EPS, EBITDA and revenue within the 100-day window. The sources read do not name the broker or give the logic, and none of the sources read records a PT cut since 29-Jul. The cautious view stated in the sources is Morgan Stanley's (Equal-weight, PT €105): a large part of the recovery already in the share price (29-Jul, earnings review) and demand highly unpredictable (30-Oct); with Deutsche Bank, it warns that consensus may be extrapolating an unusually favourable pricing environment (research of 2-Nov). Deutsche Bank, above consensus, warned on 29-Jul that a Middle East de-escalation would reverse the pricing tailwind and bring downgrades to FY2026 to FY2028 consensus (research of 2-Nov).

Precedent: IMCD's PT breadth faded once before during an advance and recovered after results: +38% at the June month-end, +13% at July's while the SP rose 20%, then +47% at August's, after the 29-Jul results. One more revenue cut before a raise takes revenue Net Upgrade Breadth below zero, half of the test that reads it with Stock RS below 50.

9. Peers

IMCD is the strongest of the three chemical distributors on price and MAs, and is pulling away from Brenntag, its price twin, into its results.

  • Technicals: IMCD is 0.2% below its 52W closing high; Brenntag 2.6% below its 16-Oct high, above its rising 20D and 50D MAs and its rising absolute 200D MA; Azelis 11.7% below its 14-Oct high and below its 20D and 50D MAs, both falling over 5 sessions, though above its rising absolute 200D MA. Over the week to 2-Nov IMCD rose 4.3%, Azelis 2.0% and Brenntag 1.1%
  • Who turned first: IMCD and Azelis peaked on 14-Oct, Brenntag on 16-Oct. Azelis broke on its own results (21 to 23-Oct); IMCD and Brenntag did not follow. Brenntag still tracks IMCD most closely (0.73 over 63 sessions), but IMCD gained about 3 points on it this week
  • Sell-side estimates: IMCD sits between the two. Azelis has the rising estimates (3-month EPS revision +6%, revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +21%); Brenntag's are flat (0%) with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (23%). Stock RS: Azelis 72 (77 a month ago), Brenntag 69 (64), IMCD 69 (70)
  • Newsflow: Azelis's third quarter (22-Oct): organic revenue +8.2%, organic gross profit +8.5%, organic operating profit +14%, but free cash flow €67m (down 52.5%), leverage 3.46 times and a €350m goodwill impairment in the Americas; Morgan Stanley titled it "3Q26 growth robust, small beat. FY26 guide unchanged" (22-Oct). Brenntag reports on 11-Nov (Jefferies' preview, 28-Sep: "What Goes Up Must Come Down")
  • Guidance and company communications: Azelis remains confident of positive organic operating profit growth for 2026; Brenntag raised its 2026 operating EBITDA guidance to €1.35bn to €1.45bn with its second quarter (August). IMCD gives no figures (research of 2-Nov)

Hypothesis: the market separated the three on cash conversion and balance sheet, and reads Azelis's growth across to IMCD. For it: IMCD's first-half free cash flow rose 29% to €222m with leverage steady at 2.8 times (earnings review), against Azelis's 52.5% fall and 3.46 times; IMCD held its 20D MA on 22 and 23-Oct as Azelis broke. Against it: no source read says so, and Jefferies' Brenntag preview argues the pricing gains reverse. Test: IMCD's own results day, 5-Nov, then Brenntag's on 11-Nov.

The wider sector is splitting: over the month Stock RS fell at Diploma (86 to 67), Addtech (60 to 26) and Rexel (55 to 39) and rose at Bunzl (61 to 80) and Beijer Ref (51 to 73).

What the peers imply for IMCD: Azelis's +8.5% organic gross profit points to IMCD's staying above zero on 5-Nov, the memo's first test; the gross margin and conversion margin tests are IMCD's own. Azelis's SP still fell 13.4% in its results fortnight on cash flow, so a growth print is not enough on its own. A Brenntag fall on 11-Nov would land inside any test of tranche 1 that 5-Nov starts.

What the rules say for IMCD now, and what decides the next step

What matters most:

  • The pullback held: IMCD retested its rising 20D MA for six sessions without a close below it, the first pullback of this advance to do so, and is back within 0.2% of its high; but the rise came on lighter volume than the fall, and relative price is 4.8% below its high (items 1 and 2)
  • The estimates have not followed: consensus unchanged for five weeks, Net Upgrade Breadth at zero, and the average PT only 11.1% above the SP, the narrowest gap since September 2024; the last gap near this (12.2%, April 2026) preceded a 21.9% fall (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), and items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth))
  • The industry is the weak layer: Industry RS 40, down 39 points in four weeks, 10 points above its line (item 3 (RS))

Tranche 1 (2.0%, bought 19-Aug), under the 10-day stop-loss rule: clear. IMCD's €104.45 close is 3.0% above the 20D MA (€101.36), and the stop-loss (€95.27) is 8.8% below the close, with no test running.   • What moves it away: a results-day rise like July's (+5.3%) puts the SP about 7% above the 20D MA

Richard decides.

12. Adding capital to IMCD: the rules and your add triggers (readings of 2026-11-02)

  • The rules: no sell signal from the six Sell Criteria {green}
    • Where it stands: No sell signal from any of IMCD's six Sell Criteria at the 30-Oct readings.
    • What would change it: IMCD's Industry RS below 30 at Fri 6-Nov; it is 40 after falling 39 points in four weeks.

1. SP and MAs

Volex is retesting its rising absolute 200D MA from just above it: its 22 to 29-Oct bounce (+10.9%) gave back 3.9% in two sessions, leaving a higher low but no lasting reclaim of the 150D or 50D MA.

  • Volex closed at 567p on 2-Nov, 18.9% below its 699p high of 6-Oct; its SP change is (17.1%) over the month, +5.6% over 3 months and +49.8% over 12 months. Since 28-May it has swung inside a 497p to 699p range: two highs 0.4% apart (28-May, 6-Oct), two declines of (28.6%) and (23.9%).
  • Volex's legs: 22 to 29-Oct, 532p to 590p, +10.9% on five up days; 30-Oct, 590p to 562p, (4.7%); 2-Nov, 562p to 567p, +0.9%. After removing the market's move at Volex's beta of 1.59, Volex's own five-session move is +1.5%, 0.2 times its usual weekly move of 6.0%.

Volex's 22 to 29-Oct bounce and its 30-Oct fall were both moves of its AI data-centre group; of the October legs only the 19-Oct fall was Volex's own.

  • 22 to 29-Oct: Volex rose 10.9%, against Huber + Suhner 8.1%, Oxford Instruments 5.3%, Industrials and capital goods 3.1% and the Stoxx Europe 600 1.8% (chart files, Market chart); Volex's bounce was its group's largest, as its 6 to 22-Oct fall of 23.9% had been.
  • 30-Oct: Volex fell 4.7% with Huber + Suhner (4.5%), Oxford Instruments (4.5%) and AQ Group (3.5%), while Technology fell 2.1%, Industrials and capital goods 1.3% and the Stoxx Europe 600 0.4%. Volex's beta of 1.59 applied to the Stoxx Europe 600's 0.4% fall explains about (0.6%) of the 4.7% fall; the rest matches its AI-exposed peers, and no Volex announcement after 19-Oct is in the sources read.
  • Hypothesis: Volex now trades as the highest-beta member of an AI data-centre basket (item 9, Peers), whose factor sets its daily direction except on Volex's own news days. Test: Volex's results-day move against Huber + Suhner and Oxford Instruments that day.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with the AlphaSense key-question reports filed for Volex from 29-Oct to 3-Nov (the 19-Oct fall; the H1 FY2027 disclosures; guidance; the market's narrative).

  • 19-Oct, Volex's first-half trading update: FY2027 underlying operating profit "towards the upper end of current market expectations"; about $10m of revenue pulled into the first half by customers' extra deliveries during a US site consolidation; second-half revenue broadly similar to the first; data-centre revenue below the second half of FY2026 (AlphaSense key-question report of 29-Oct, citing the release). Volex fell 11.6%.
  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Fell 12% on 19-Oct. Huge! Trading update. Why did it fall? The update reads well to me". The research filed since gives three reasons: profit-taking after a crowded run (Zeus, 19-Oct; the 29-Oct report's best-supported answer); data-centre revenue falling from a second half of FY2026 that carried about $20m to $30m of one-off share won while competitors were short of supply (AlphaSense report of 2-Nov); and a flat second half that, net of the $10m pulled forward, implies a slower FY2028 start. The update read well on profit; the market sold the data-centre sequence, the ground of the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's counter-case.
  • 2-Nov, Bernstein: Luxshare is ramping its own 224G copper interconnect (AlphaSense report of 3-Nov), a competitor in Volex's data-centre line.
  • Volex's H1 FY2027 results: 12-Nov in the research filed from 31-Oct to 3-Nov, 5-Nov in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, none in the data file; both fall before the 30-Nov month-end readings.

The MAs: Volex sits between its rising absolute 200D MA (555.5p, with the close 2.1% above it) and a cluster from 580.6p to 617.0p, and its 20D MA crossed below its 50D MA on 2-Nov.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Volex against your trend-health tests On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Retesting it?" The tests below answer it for Volex, ending in a verdict.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus keeps rising while its SP falls, and the later years rise as fast as the nearer one: analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521: +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1%, +6.8% (first estimate 30-Apr).

  • Volex's FY2027 EBITDA, $192.6m: +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%; FY2028, $208.8m: +1.8%, +10.4%, +23.5%; FY2029, $225.0m: +1.7%, +9.4%.
  • Implied growth: Volex's FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth is 11.1% (11.7% three months ago, 10.2% six months ago), FY2028 to FY2029 10.3%; revenue growth 7.2% and 6.9%.
  • Implied margin and the source of the upgrades: Volex's FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.6%, from 13.2% three and 12.6% six months ago; with EPS +15.0%, EBITDA +10.5% and revenue +6.8% over 3 months, the upgrades are margin first and below the EBITDA line second.
  • October's step: most of the month's +2.9% came on 17 to 21-Oct: Jefferies raised FY2027 EPS 6% to 52.06c and FY2028 4% to 57.64c; Zeus to 53.5c and 58.7c (19-Oct, AlphaSense report of 31-Oct).

Volex's SP led its estimates up from July to 6-Oct; since then the SP has fallen 18.9% while FY2028 EPS rose about 2.5%, so the whole fall is the multiple. Hypothesis: the market is discounting a FY2028 data-centre risk that consensus does not show: a flat second half and "lumpy project activity normalising before the next ramp-up in FY2028" (Zeus, 19-Oct, in the AlphaSense report of 3-Nov). Against it: the update said data-centre revenue stays well ahead of the prior year, and 7 of 7 analysts raised. Tests at the results: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's own tests: Complex Industrial Technology still growing; the largest customer at or below 18.7% of revenue (alarm above 20%); cash conversion back above 70%; a first-half group margin above 11%.

What the rules say for Volex now, and what decides the next step

  • Sellers have gone, buyers have not come: Volex's down legs came on falling volume (3.28M, 0.95M, 0.70M shares), but seven sessions after the 22-Oct low no up day has reached 1.5 times the 50D average; the March and July lows were confirmed within six.
  • The sell-side and the chart still point opposite ways: 7 of 7 analysts are raising Volex's FY2027 EPS (+15.0% in three months) and the average PT is 29.0% above the SP, against Technical Momentum D, a 20D MA below the 50D MA since 2-Nov and Industry RS at its data-file low of 40.
  • Volex's results decide it: 12-Nov in the research filed (5-Nov in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo); on other days Volex moves with its AI data-centre basket.
  • The three tranches today: Volex is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone; none gives a sell signal, so the rules hold tranches 1 (2.80%), 2 (1.62%) and 3 (1.10%).
  • The nearest sell test, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (read at the month-end): Volex's relative price is 21.5% below, against 23.3% a week earlier, 3.7% four weeks earlier and 28.2% thirteen weeks earlier. At a flat market a Volex close of 541.5p, 4.5% below 567p and less than one usual weekly move, gives a sell signal. Read next on Mon 30-Nov, a sell signal would sell the whole holding (5.52%) at the Tue 1-Dec session.
  • The industry test, Industry RS below 30 (read each Friday): 40 on 2-Nov, from 46 at the 23 and 30-Oct readings, 79 on 9-Oct and 59 on 31-Jul; it has never read below 40 in the data file (from 30-Sep-2025), Volex's SP cannot move it, and it is read next on Fri 6-Nov.
  • The sector-and-stock test (month-end): Volex's Stock RS (75) is 5 points from its half (65 at 536p on 23-Oct); its Sector RS (69) must fall 20 points, below its data-file low of 50.
  • How the tests interact: one results-day fall like 19-Oct's (11.6%), to about 501p, held to the month-end would pass the relative-price line by 7.5%, probably take Volex's Stock RS below 70, and bring the relative 200D MA test within reach by 27-Nov; the 30% tranche-fall test would still need a close below 489.3p. 1. SP and MAs

Sdiptech's SP is retesting its rising 50D MA from above, with higher lows intact, after its first pullback from the 14-Oct high. Sdiptech closed at 262.4 on Mon 2-Nov, up 3.7% on the week, 2.2% on the month, 8.3% over 3 months and 38.0% over 12 months; it is 3.0% below its 14-Oct closing high (270.6) and 57.1% above its 9-Feb low (167.0). Over the month its industry (Industrials and capital goods) fell 1.4%; the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 2.9%.

The legs since the 14-Oct high:

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file.

Leverage rose to 2.95x net debt to adjusted EBITDA, from 2.80x, against a 3.0x ceiling (the interim report of 23-Oct and RedEye, 29-Oct, as quoted in the AlphaSense research filed on 31-Oct).

The MAs: all rising, stacked in order, and the SP above each. On 2-Nov the 50D MA is 254.07 (up 2.4% in 20 sessions), the 100D 248.09, the 150D 243.57 and the absolute 200D MA 228.53 (up 3.2%). Healthy retest or broken trend? Sdiptech against your trend-health tests

  • The 150D above the 200D: passes; 243.57 against 228.53.
