2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Volume ratios are the volume script's, without the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's differ by 0.02x at most, and both give a 20-session up/down ratio of 1.12.

Sdiptech's bounce off its 50D MA since 26-Oct has come on falling volume: sellers have not returned, and buyers have not had to chase. Mon 2-Nov's 2.5% rise came on 38,551 shares (SEK 10.1m, 0.48x), closing 89% up a range that first dipped 0.6% below the 50D MA. On Fri 30-Oct, when its industry fell 1.3%, Sdiptech fell 0.4% on 18,783 shares (SEK 4.8m, 0.23x), its lightest session in 25. Hypothesis: the supply that met Sdiptech around its report is spent for now; the memo (31-Oct) records that "three holders have sold roughly 11 points of capital into this rally" and calls the overhang "largely spent". Against: light volume is not demand; the test is the next heavy-volume day.

Sdiptech's nine heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) in 25 sessions all fall between 29-Sep and 27-Oct.

  • Accumulation: 29-Sep (+1.0%, 3.61x, 201,297 shares, SEK 50.3m, close 91% up its range); 30-Sep (+1.8%, 1.50x, SEK 21.7m); 14-Oct (+5.9%, 1.88x, SEK 37.9m, a 1.3% gap up, close 97% up); 23-Oct, the report (+4.6%, 3.06x, 240,370 shares, SEK 63.5m, close 90% up after an intraday low of 246.2, below the 50D and 100D MAs); 27-Oct (+2.4%, 1.90x, SEK 40.7m, closing at its high).
  • Distribution: 1-Oct ((0.3%), 5.47x, 345,557 shares, SEK 87.6m); 7-Oct ((1.4%), 4.35x, SEK 77.1m, close 20% up its range); 15-Oct ((1.9%), 1.96x, SEK 39.9m); 26-Oct ((4.2%), 1.50x, SEK 30.6m, closing at its low while its industry rose 1.4%). The heaviest two came on days its industry fell 1.3% and 2.3%.
  • The count is falling: the dashboard counts 4 distribution days in 25 sessions, from 6 on 26-Oct and 5 on 19-Oct.

Up-day volume now leads over 5 and 20 sessions; over the whole advance from 24-Jul it does not. The 20-session up/down ratio is 1.12, from 0.86 on 27-Oct; the dashboard's 5-session ratio is 2.24. The advance from 24-Jul (+13.1%) has a ratio of 0.98, against 1.27 and 1.33 for the February and May advances: the SP has risen through steady selling rather than on rising demand.

At the MAs: the rising 50D MA has been undercut five times since 8-Oct (on 0.86x, 0.45x, 3.06x, 1.90x and 0.48x) and never lost on a close. The 20D MA has been crossed six times on closes since 22-Oct, all inside the 4.6% band between 252.4 and 264.0: the short-term trend is undecided while the medium-term support holds.

Precedent: the decline that matches best ended quietly. After the 24-Jul low (232.0) Sdiptech's first ten sessions traded 0.4x to 1.2x, with no heavy day, and the SP rose 16.6% to 14-Oct; the 9-Feb low ended loudly instead (+13.2% on 6.1x). The 14 to 22-Oct pullback was sold on falling volume (1.96x to 0.45x), and its retest has been light: it follows the July pattern so far.

Relative price confirmed the 14-Oct SP high and is recovering towards it: 5.0% below its 52W high on 2-Nov, from 7.2% on 26-Oct, and 8.9% above its rising relative 200D MA.

Peers on Sdiptech's heavy days: no cohort peer traded heavily with it on 1, 7, 14 or 27-Oct; on 23-Oct Swedish industrials outside the cohort did (Munters 3.2x, Alimak 2.8x, Bravida 1.9x), a sign that Swedish small and mid-cap flows drive its heaviest days as much as its news.

Confirms the advance resumes: a close above 264.0, then 270.6, on 1.5x the 50D average (about 120,000 shares, SEK 31m). Confirms a top: a close below the 50D MA (254.07) and 252.4 on 1.5x or more.

3. RS

In your 28 to 31-Oct Notion note you wrote: "RS positive for stock and sector and industry". On the 2-Nov readings that holds for Sdiptech's Stock RS and Sector RS; it no longer holds for its Industry RS, which is the nearest of Sdiptech's sell tests.

  • Stock RS: 86 on 2-Nov, from 84 at the 30-Oct month-end, 85 a month ago, 75 three months ago and 12 a year ago (31-Oct-2025). It has read 70 or more at every reading since 25-Sep and is 16 points above the 70 line the Sell Criteria read with Sector RS.
  • Sector RS (Diversified industrial groups): 76, up from 72 at the month-end, after a fall from 89 (30-Sep and 16-Oct, its 13-month high in the data file) as Lifco, thyssenkrupp and Hill & Smith fell (item 9 (Peers)). It is 26 points above the line of 50.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 40 on 2-Nov, from 46 at the 30-Oct month-end and 79 on 2 and 9-Oct. Industry RS ranks 16 industries, so it moves in steps of about 6.7 points (40, 33, 26): a fall of two more places gives a reading below 30 on Fri 6-Nov.

The order of the turn is industry first, sector second, stock not at all: Sdiptech is being carried against a rotation, not with it. Industry RS turned after 9-Oct, Sector RS after 16-Oct; Sdiptech's Stock RS dipped once, to 80 on 29-Oct after the 26-Oct fall, and has risen since. Among its cohort, only Sdiptech's Stock RS is higher than a month ago: Indus Holding's fell from 97 to 87 and Storskogen's from 73 to 60. Hypothesis: Sdiptech's rising revenue consensus (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)) holds its Stock RS up against its groups; against, a 12-month composite lags a turn.

Precedent: Industry RS has read 40 before, and no lower, in the data file's 13 months. It read 40 on three Fridays (13, 20 and 27-Mar-2026) and at the 31-Mar month-end, then recovered to 73 by 24-Apr; over the same weeks Sdiptech's Stock RS rose from 38 to 56 and its SP was in its 45.6% advance from the 9-Feb low.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts are raising Sdiptech's later years at least as fast as the nearer one, and revenue faster than EBITDA, and EBITDA faster than EPS: a rising trajectory with less of each krona of sales reaching earnings.

  • Revenue: Sdiptech's FY2027 revenue consensus rose 4.0% in October to SEK 5,584m (5.6% over 3 months, 9.4% over 6), and FY2028 rose 4.3% to SEK 5,956m (5.9% and 9.6%). About 1.2 points of FY2027's 4.0% rise came before the report (6 and 13-Oct), 1.5 points on 25 and 26-Oct, after it, and 1.3 points by the month-end, when a seventh analyst joined the count.
  • EBITDA: FY2027 up 2.1% in the month to SEK 1,407m (3.3% over 3 months), FY2028 up 2.2% to SEK 1,506m.
  • EPS: FY2027 up 0.9% in the month to SEK 17.58 (6.6% over 3 months), FY2028 up 1.8% to SEK 19.61 (7.2%). FY2026 EPS fell 6.9% in the month, to SEK 13.31, after rising 8.1% on 30-Sep.
  • Implied margin: FY2027 EBITDA against revenue was 25.7% at the 30-Sep month-end and 25.2% at the 31-Oct month-end; FY2028 25.3%. Analysts took about half a point off Sdiptech's FY2027 margin in the month of its report.
  • Implied growth: FY2027 to FY2028 is 11.5% for EPS (10.6% a month ago), 6.7% for revenue and 7.0% for EBITDA. FY2028 EPS rising faster than FY2027 is analysts raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward.

The links. The revenue-over-margin pattern matches the report (23-Oct): organic sales up 13% against DNB Carnegie's 6%, but the adjusted EBITA margin 20.0% against a 20.9% consensus, with Supply Chain & Transportation at 18.5% from 21.0% on Mecno's rail-project costs and Hilltip's low-margin chassis deliveries (earnings review). The memo (31-Oct) reads it as mix that ends in the fourth quarter of 2026; RedEye as possibly structural cost pressure. The FY2026 EPS swing (+8.1%, then (6.9%)) is larger than the 2% DNB Carnegie took off its 2026 EPS for the SEK 13m bond-redemption charge. Hypothesis: it is a change in the mix of brokers in the average; the memo calls FactSet's EPS "a median of two brokers on two different EPS definitions". Sdiptech's SP led its estimates: it rose 12.4% from 15-Sep to 14-Oct, while FY2027 revenue consensus rose 1.2% from 29-Sep to 14-Oct.

The memo's test. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (31-Oct) gates the next leg on one number on 10-Feb-2027: fourth-quarter cash conversion "above 100% and leverage at 2.6x to 2.7x unlocks the engine; below 90% and 2.8x pauses it for two quarters". Test: FY2027 EBITDA rising with revenue at the November month-end would show the margin cut was a one-off.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

In your 28 to 31-Oct Notion note you wrote: "SS positive across dimensions". Positive in direction on revenue, EBITDA and PT; on EPS and ratings it has turned mixed, and that is new this month.

  • Ratings: five Buys and one other rating at the 31-Oct month-end and on 1-Nov, the first non-Buy on Sdiptech in the 24 months of the data file, as coverage rose from five to six analysts. The broker and its logic are not in the sources read.
  • EPS: three analysts raised FY2027 EPS and one cut it at the 31-Oct month-end (Net Upgrade Breadth +33%, from +80% at 30-Sep). The cutting analyst is not named in the sources read; it is not DNB Carnegie, which raised its later-year adjusted EPS by 5% for lower finance costs (earnings review) to SEK 19.5 for FY2027 (its 26-Oct note, as quoted in the AlphaSense research filed on 31-Oct).
  • Revenue, EBITDA, PT: revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +67% (four of six raising, none cutting), EBITDA +71% (five of seven), PT +33% (two of six). The 100-day counts the Sell Criteria read give revenue +71% and PT +43%, with no analyst cutting.

Where analysts are cutting: DNB Carnegie's PT, on the sector's multiple, not on Sdiptech's numbers. DNB Carnegie's Carl Korsheden reiterated Buy and trimmed his PT to SEK 340 from 342 on 26-Oct, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples" (earnings review). Against it, RedEye published "Sdiptech (Q3 review): Sales beat, Base Case raised to SEK315" on 29-Oct (the title, from the AlphaSense research filed on 31-Oct; the note was not read), from SEK 300 in July. The Sector Digest (30-Oct) reads the pattern as "a lower multiple for Swedish serial acquirers appears to cap PTs (hypothesis)".

Ratings carry little signal on Sdiptech; the PT does the work. Every analyst rated Sdiptech a Buy through its 35% fall from 258.2 (11-Nov-2024) to its 9-Feb-2026 low of 167.0 and its rise since, and the average PT followed the SP: down 35% from 398.8 (August 2024) to 258.4 (January 2026), up 21% since to 312.7 on 26-Oct.

Sdiptech's average PT has acted as a ceiling about 12% to 16% above its SP, and the gap has reopened. The gap was 12.6% at the 14-Oct high (PT 304.8, SP 270.6), the third time in 2026 the SP came that close before a 6.6% to 7.8% pullback and a PT rise. The gap is 19.2% on 2-Nov (PT 312.7, SP 262.4). Test: a close above about 272 (a gap of 15% between the PT of 312.7 and the SP, the basis used above) without a further PT rise would break the ceiling pattern.

Precedent: Sdiptech's SP has turned with breadth: EPS breadth went from (100%) to +100% between the January and April 2026 month-ends while the SP rose 45.6% from its 9-Feb low. PT breadth has thinned since June (+100%, +83%, +83%, +50%, +33%) with no PT cut in the dashboard's counts; DNB Carnegie's SEK 2 trim on 26-Oct (earnings review) is not counted. Test: PT or EPS breadth at or below zero at the November month-end would be the first broker turn against the SP since January.

9. Peers

Sdiptech leads its cohort (diversified serial acquirers in a reset) on estimates, technicals and newsflow; the cohort's other leader, Indus Holding, turned first and reports on 12-Nov.

  • Sell-side estimates: Sdiptech's 3-month EPS revision is +7% with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +67%; Indus Holding's 3-month EPS revision is +10% with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth at 0%, Storskogen's (1%) with +60%, and Volati's (4%) with (100%). Lifco, the sector's largest Swedish serial acquirer, is (4%) and (45%) after DNB Carnegie cut its FY2027 and FY2028 EPS 5% to 6% and its PT to SEK 338 "due to customer losses" (earnings review).
  • SP technicals: Indus Holding fell 11.3% in the month to 2-Nov and sits 4.3% below its 50D MA, its Stock RS down from 97 to 87; Storskogen is 1.8% above its 50D MA, its Stock RS down from 73 to 60. Sdiptech is 3.3% above its 50D MA with Stock RS rising.
  • Newsflow: Lifco fell 12.0% on 23-Oct on 7.2x its 50D volume, on organic growth of 1.6% and a customer loss (RedEye, 29-Oct: "Margins hold, but a customer leaves"); Storskogen reported sales up 4% and its margin up to 9.7% on 23-Oct and moved (0.1%). Sdiptech's 13% organic growth was the strongest of these prints.
  • Company guidance and communications: neither Sdiptech nor Lifco gives numerical guidance; Sdiptech reaffirmed its building blocks (organic adjusted EBITA growth of 5% to 10% a year, SEK 100m of acquired run-rate EBITA in 2026) on 23-Oct without raising them (memo, 31-Oct).

Sdiptech's report-week swings were shared with Swedish serial acquirers outside its cohort, not with its cohort. On 23, 26 and 27-Oct Sdiptech moved +4.6%, (4.2%) and +2.4%; Röko, in its sector, moved +12.7% (12.2x its 50D volume, its report day), (9.4%) (3.7x) and +5.2%, and Lifco (2.3%) on 2.8x on 26-Oct, while its cohort and industry rose that day. DNB Carnegie's Röko review (26-Oct): "Excellent print, but further rerating unlikely". Hypothesis: 26-Oct was a day of selling Swedish serial acquirers after strong reports, the same multiple concern DNB Carnegie cited in trimming Sdiptech's PT; this replaces the 28-Oct note's reading that 26-Oct was the first selling "about Sdiptech". Against: it is one session.

Since 22-Oct, the group Sdiptech trades with is Swedish small and mid-cap industrials, not its cohort. Sdiptech is up 4.0% since its 22-Oct low; Storskogen +2.2%, Indus Holding (1.6%), Volati (4.0%) and Lifco (12.8%), against Röko +7.7% and Munters +12.6%. Munters, the closest daily tracker outside the cohort (a weak 0.27 correlation), rose on 23 and 27-Oct and 2-Nov with Sdiptech.

What the peers imply for Sdiptech: its exposure is to its industry (Industry RS 40, item 3 (RS)) and to a Swedish rates and multiples factor, not to its cohort. Tests: Indus Holding's 12-Nov report for the cohort's estimates; Sdiptech against Röko, Lifco and Munters after the Riksbank's 4-Nov decision.

What the rules say for Sdiptech now, and what decides the next step

  • Sdiptech's own trend is healthy so far: all five trend-health tests pass and the lows are rising, although one Stage 3 sign (wider swings) is present and four are partly present; the rising 50D MA has held on every close since 28-Sep, on falling volume (items 1 (SP and MAs) and 2 (Volume)).
  • The risk to the holding is its industry, not the stock: Industry RS fell to 40 on 2-Nov, its joint lowest in 13 months, while Sdiptech's Stock RS rose to 86 (item 3 (RS)).
  • The sell-side is positive but thinner: revenue upgrades continue, but a first non-Buy in 24 months, one FY2027 EPS cut and DNB Carnegie's PT trim on peer multiples are new this month (items 4 (Consensus by named FY) and 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)).

Each tranche today: hold all four. Sdiptech is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone, and none gives a sell signal: tranche 1 (3.00%, 8-Jun-2026), tranche 2 (1.05%, 6-Jul-2026), tranche 3 (1.00%, 17-Jul-2026), tranche 4 (1.00%, 30-Oct-2026).

The nearest sell test: Industry RS below 30, read on Fri 6-Nov.

  • Pace: Industry RS is 40 on 2-Nov, from 46 on 23-Oct (one week earlier), 79 on 2-Oct (about four weeks) and 53 on 3-Aug (thirteen weeks).

Richard decides.

D'Ieteren

Early Innings Probing Bet · 2.5% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.5%, bought 12-Aug-2026) · readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

D'Ieteren's SP has moved sideways for nine months inside a narrowing range, and on Mon 2-Nov it closed at €177.6, above the €175.9 ceiling of 16 and 21-Oct and at its highest close since the €180.4 of 4-Sep, but on below-average volume and on its industry's best day.

  • Over 12 months (31-Oct-2025 to 2-Nov-2026) D'Ieteren's SP is up 13.5%, level with the Stoxx Europe 600 (+13.6%) and ahead of Consumer discretionary (+4.6%). Over 3 months it is down 3.6%, against about (1%) for the Stoxx Europe 600 and for Consumer discretionary alike.
  • D'Ieteren's swing highs since its €195.44 high of 10-Feb have come lower, at €186.06 (17-Apr), €184.3 (5-Aug) and €180.4 (4-Sep), while its lows held at €153.86 (30-Mar), €158.9 (8-Jun) and €160.0 (24-Sep). The 2-Nov close is 1.6% below the 4-Sep high, the next lower high to clear.
  • Over the week to 2-Nov D'Ieteren rose 1.5% against 2.9% for Consumer discretionary; after taking out the part of the move explained by the market, its own move was (0.2%), 0.1 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with peers' news from the key question research on operating leverage filed 31-Oct.

The legs since the 24-Sep low, side by side (closes; the industry is Consumer discretionary, equal-weighted):

  • 24-Sep to 16-Oct, +9.9% to €175.9 (industry +3.5%, Stoxx Europe 600 +1.3%): D'Ieteren's own leg. It rose with the parts and rental half of its sector (Inter Cars +10.6%, ZIGUP +7.1%, Sixt +4.7%) while the car-retail and leasing half fell (Inchcape (11.4%), Ayvens (10.2%)). Its heaviest day was 8-Oct (+2.2% on 2.72x the 50D average), when Bloomberg News reported that Belron had picked four banks for a listing (Bloomberg Línea, 8-Oct, in the 28-Oct note).
  • 21 to 28-Oct, (3.1%) to €170.4 (industry (1.0%), Stoxx Europe 600 (0.1%)): mostly D'Ieteren's own, alongside the car retailers. Inchcape fell 7.0% and Bilia 3.5% after Inchcape's 22-Oct margin warning (item 9, Peers), while Sixt and Inter Cars rose. Most of D'Ieteren's fall came on 27-Oct ((1.8%), closing at the day's low on 0.84x, against an industry up 0.1%), the day the data file shows D'Ieteren's FY2027 EPS consensus down 1.7% and its average PT down 1.1% (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus by named FY). Hypothesis: the 27-Oct fall was the estimate change; no source read names the analyst.
  • 28-Oct to 2-Nov, +4.2% to €177.6 (industry +2.8%, Stoxx Europe 600 +0.8%): mostly the industry's leg. The broken half of D'Ieteren's sector rebounded hardest (Auto1 +5.7%, Ayvens +4.5%, Sixt +3.6%). On 2-Nov D'Ieteren rose 2.7% against 2.1% for Consumer discretionary and 0.4% for the Stoxx Europe 600; no company news on 2-Nov is in the sources read.

The MAs: D'Ieteren's 20D to absolute 200D MAs sit within 1.8% of one another (€170.37 to €173.40), and on 2-Nov the SP closed above all of them for the first time since 26-Oct.

  • From highest to lowest, the MAs stand in this order: the absolute 200D, the 100D, the 20D, the 150D and the 50D, close to the reverse of a Stage 2 (advancing) order. Over 20 sessions the 20D rose 3.4% and the 100D, 150D and absolute 200D 0.5% to 0.8%; tranche 1's 50D MA fell 1.6% and stopped falling on 30-Oct (€170.35).
  • The crossings came in this sequence: below the 100D and absolute 200D on 27-Oct (0.84x), below the 50D and 150D on 28-Oct (0.51x), back above the 50D on 29-Oct (0.56x), the 100D and 150D on 30-Oct (0.65x) and the absolute 200D on 2-Nov (0.91x). Every crossing, down and up, came on below-average volume.
  • At earlier turns: from the 4-Sep high D'Ieteren lost the 50D the next session and the 100D, 150D and absolute 200D on 10-Sep (4.44x, after the half-year results); from the 24-Sep low it regained the 20D first (8-Oct, 2.72x) and the other four on 13-Oct.
  • On 22-Sep you wrote in your Notion note: "150D MA was falling from a month ago". It has turned: D'Ieteren's 150D MA has risen 0.5% over 20 sessions and 0.3% over 5, though it remains 1.0% below the absolute 200D MA.

Healthy retest or broken trend? D'Ieteren against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly. +0.7% over 20 sessions, flat over 5 ((0.05%)).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: fails. 1.0% below.
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: fails. Below both.
  • The SP above each MA: passes. 2.4% above the absolute 200D, 4.2% above the 50D.
  • Near its 52W high: partly. 9.1% below the €195.44 close of 10-Feb.
  • Away from its 52W low: passes. 24.5% above the €142.7 close of 25-Nov-2025.
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: partly. The largest day, 27-Oct ((1.8%), 0.84x), and five sessions, (3.1%) to 28-Oct, are small, on light volume, and not after good news.
  • Wider swings: absent. D'Ieteren's average daily range was 1.6% of the close over the last 10 sessions, against 3.4% from 10 to 24-Sep. On 22-Sep you wrote in your Notion note: "SP gotten very, very scatty/erratic (more volatile?) over L5-7 trading days". That has passed: the range has halved.
  • Lower lows: absent. The 28-Oct close is 6.5% above the 24-Sep low.

  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: the first half is in place. The 2-Nov close above €175.9 came on 0.91 times the 50D average volume; a heavy-volume close back below it would complete the flag.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly. D'Ieteren has crossed its absolute 200D MA five times since 13-Oct (13, 14, 16, 27-Oct and 2-Nov).
  • Verdict: neither, yet: a sideways base inside a falling relative trend, whose 21 to 28-Oct pullback closed just below the 50D and 150D MAs on 28-Oct (by 0.1% and 0.5%), on the lightest volume in 25 sessions, and regained them within two sessions. With two MA-order tests failing, there is no Stage 2 (advancing) trend to retest. What would settle it: a close above the €180.4 high of 4-Sep on 1.5x the 50D average or more would make it a breakout from the base; a close below €170.4 (the 28-Oct low, beside the 50D MA) on above-average volume would make 2-Nov a failed breakout.

Basis: the 31-Aug index-review day and the 18-Sep option-expiry and index-review day inside the 50D average lower D'Ieteren's volume ratios by about 4%; the ratios below leave them out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's in brackets.

That sits between D'Ieteren's June to August advance (+16.0%, 1.02, no heavy day), which gave back 13.2%, and its March to April advance (+20.9%, 1.61), which gave back 14.6%. Inference: the advance has more buying behind it than June to August, but not the volume of a breakout.

Precedent: the last breakout attempt, 4-Sep (+3.4% to €180.4 on 1.32x, dashboard), reversed the next session (see "The record" in the section "What the rules say for D'Ieteren now, and what decides the next step"). After declines, the 24-Sep low drew its first heavy up day eight sessions later (6-Oct), while the 25-Nov-2025 and 30-Mar-2026 lows drew theirs the next session.

Relative price has not confirmed the SP's new high. Its highs keep falling, 101.1 (5-Aug), 98.8 (4-Sep), 98.1 (22-Oct), and on 2-Nov it stood 0.5% below the 22-Oct high while the SP made its highest close since 4-Sep. Peers' volume on D'Ieteren's heavy days: on 8-Oct D'Ieteren's rise was its own (Ayvens (3.4%) on 1.6x, Auto1 (1.2%) on 1.7x, Sixt (0.6%) on 2.5x; only Inchcape, +2.2% on 2.3x, and Inter Cars, +3.2% on 0.8x, rose); on 15-Oct its sector bought and D'Ieteren lagged (Sixt +2.2% on 3.3x, Inchcape +3.4% on 1.6x, Auto1 +4.6%).

3. RS

D'Ieteren's three RS readings turned up together this week, the sector first, the stock with it and the industry on 2-Nov, but all three remain below their sell-test lines.

  • D'Ieteren's Stock RS read 42 on 2-Nov, from 31 on 29-Oct and 35 at the 30-Oct month-end; 38 a month ago, 35 three months ago and 27 twelve months ago. It has been below 50 at every reading since 11-Sep (12-month high 68, 6-Mar).

  • D'Ieteren's Industry RS (Consumer discretionary) read 33 on 2-Nov, the first reading above 30 since Fri 4-Sep (40), after 26 at every Friday from 9 to 30-Oct.

The order of the turn: a rotation carrying the stock. Sector RS turned first (29 to 38 on 30-Oct), Stock RS with it (31 to 35), and Industry RS rose 7 points on 2-Nov, when Consumer discretionary rose 2.1%. D'Ieteren lagged its industry over the week (+1.5% against +2.9%) and its relative price made no new high (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)), so the turn is the group's more than the stock's.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's RS rallies this autumn have faded. Stock RS reached 47 on 9-Oct, the Belron-report week, and fell back to 31 by 29-Oct while the SP held flat; the last time Industry RS stood above 30, at 40 on 4-Sep, it fell to 13 within two weeks. Against: Stock RS rose from its two earlier lows into the +37.0% and +16.0% advances: it bottomed at 14 on 12-Dec-2025, after the 25-Nov-2025 SP low, and reached 61 by 23-Jan-2026; it stood at 27 to 31 from 15-May to the 8-Jun low and reached 56 by 1-Jul-2026.

Would confirm the turn: Stock RS and Sector RS both above 50 at a Friday reading. Would refute it: Industry RS back below 30 at Fri 6-Nov; Consumer discretionary's Industry RS moves in steps of about 7 points (its readings this year are 13, 20, 26, 33, 40 and 46), so one step down, from 33 to 26, would do it.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts cut D'Ieteren's EPS for every FY again in October, by about 2%, after the September cuts, and left revenue and EBITDA unchanged; D'Ieteren's SP rose 9.0% in the same month, so the SP led and the estimates did not follow.

  • EPS over 1, 3 and 12 months: FY2026 (year to Dec-26) €12.75, (2.4%), (4.8%), (13.8%); FY2027 €15.09, (1.7%), (3.5%), (12.5%); FY2028 €17.38, (1.9%), (3.8%), (7.2%).
  • Revenue and EBITDA are unchanged since 30-Sep; their 3-month cuts ((1.5%) to (2.4%) on revenue, (3.3%) to (5.9%) on EBITDA) all came after the 9-Sep half-year results.

The trajectory: analysts cut the nearer year fastest and are pushing the recovery out, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 18.4%, from 16.7% three months ago; from FY2027 to FY2028 it is 15.2%, from 15.5%. The implied EBITDA margin has fallen in every year: FY2026 7.9% (8.2% three months ago), FY2027 8.3% (8.5%), FY2028 8.5% (8.6%).

D'Ieteren's October EPS cut came in steps in the daily FY2027 series: (1.0%) on 2-Oct, back up 1.0% on 16-Oct, then (1.7%) on 27-Oct, the day D'Ieteren fell 1.8% (item 1 (SP and MAs)). On 27-Oct the count of analysts with a PT also rose from 13 to 14. Hypothesis: if one analyst joined that day, its PT works out at about €201 and its FY2027 EPS (if also new) at about €11.7, 24% below the others. That would make the 27-Oct step a new low estimate or a reported-EPS basis (€8.04 reported against €12.95 adjusted in FY2025, the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo) rather than a cut. No source read names the analyst.

The links: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's alarm "sell-side cuts of 2 percent or more with underperformance" is half present: FY2026 EPS fell 2.4% in a month, but D'Ieteren beat the Stoxx Europe 600 by 6 points over the month. The research filed 31-Oct points to more D'Ieteren Automotive cuts for FY2027: the 344-role restructuring's savings "will likely not impact earnings until mid-2027", and with 2026 the last year of 100% tax deductibility for corporate electric vehicles in Belgium, fleet demand "will likely taper from 2027 onward" (Jefferies, 15-Oct). Test: the November month-end consensus; FY2026 results and FY2027 guidance on 10-Mar-2027.

D'Ieteren's analysts rate it Buy unanimously while every estimate breadth is negative: the ratings and PTs value Belron; the estimates track D'Ieteren Automotive.

  • Ratings: 14 Buys and no Hold or Sell at the 31-Oct month-end, from 12 at 31-Aug (24 months ago: 6 Buys and 1 other rating). Every analyst has rated D'Ieteren Buy at every month-end since January 2026, through the 21.3% and 13.2% falls: the ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum and carry no signal.
  • Average PT €234.7 (27-Oct), (2.0%) in October from €239.5. Its gap to the SP narrowed from 46.9% at the 30-Sep month-end to 32.2% on 2-Nov as the SP rose. In 2026 the gap has run from 12.6% at the 10-Feb high to 51.4% at the 30-Mar low while the average PT stayed between €220 and €240: the gap moves with the SP alone.
  • Latest PT changes: one analyst cut on 16-Oct, taking the average to €237.3 from €239.5 (about €28 off one PT); the 27-Oct step to €234.7 came as the count rose to 14 (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). No source read names either analyst; the latest named changes were raises on a higher Belron value, KBC Securities to €250 (16-Sep) and ING to €263 (28-Sep) (28-Oct note).

Net Upgrade Breadth at the 31-Oct month-end: every estimate breadth is negative, the PT breadth positive. FY2027 EPS (21%), with 1 analyst raising and 4 cutting, of 14, from (25%) at 30-Sep and +9% at 31-Aug; EBITDA (62%) (0 raising, 5 cutting, of 8), from (75%); revenue (56%) (0 raising, 5 cutting, of 9), from (67%); PT +36% (6 raising, 1 cutting, of 14), from +46%; Buys 14 of 14. Where analysts are cutting: on D'Ieteren Automotive. Deutsche Bank (Kerstens) cut PBT forecasts by 1% after the 9-Sep results "to reflect the heavier-than-expected drag from Automotive retail operations", and brokers now model the Automotive margin at 2.1% against 3.5% or more before (earnings review, 18-Sep). October's cutters are not named in the sources read.

Precedent: the same split ran at D'Ieteren's March and April 2026 month-ends (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%)), across the 20.9% rally to 17-Apr; D'Ieteren gave back 14.6% by 8-Jun. Test: PT changes when Belron's terms, or a delay, are reported; PT cuts without Belron news would mean the Belron value itself is being cut.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's.

D'Ieteren's sector is still split, parts distribution and rental holding up, car retail and leasing broken, and D'Ieteren has traded with each half in turn: with the parts and rental half from 24-Sep to 16-Oct, with the car retailers from 21 to 28-Oct, and with the broken half's rebound this week (item 1 (SP and MAs)).

  • Sell-side estimates: D'Ieteren's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((56%)) is the weakest in its sector, below Ayvens ((50%)), Auto1 ((45%)) and Inchcape ((33%)); the parts and rental half is positive (Sixt +38%, Inter Cars +20%, ZIGUP +17%, Bilia +14%). On 3-month EPS revisions D'Ieteren ((4%)) sits with Auto1 ((3%)) and Inchcape ((8%)), against Inter Cars (+9%).
  • SP technicals: over the week to 2-Nov Auto1 (+5.9%), Sixt (+5.7%) and Ayvens (+5.4%) led; D'Ieteren (+1.5%) beat only ZIGUP (+1.3%) and Inchcape ((2.0%)), which is 20.2% below its absolute 200D MA. Sixt's Stock RS rose from 39 to 66 in a month, the sector's clearest turn; Bilia's fell from 68 to 50 and Inchcape's from 36 to 6.
  • Newsflow (research filed 31-Oct): Inchcape cut its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance to about 5.5% on 22-Oct (Jefferies, 22-Oct); Bilia's service business made 73% of its third-quarter operating earnings (23-Oct); LKQ, a rival of D'Ieteren's Parts Holding Europe, lost about $43m of European EBITDA to a self-inflicted German systems migration (Jefferies, 29-Oct).
  • Company guidance and communications: the warnings are in new-car retail (Inchcape), D'Ieteren Automotive's business; service and parts, where Belron and Parts Holding Europe sit, are steady.

Who turned first: Sixt's Stock RS turned up in October, before its sector's; Inchcape's broke after its 22-Oct warning. D'Ieteren barely moved on that day ((0.4%)) but fell with Inchcape and Bilia over the week, the 27-Oct estimate change being the other candidate cause (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). The stocks most correlated with D'Ieteren outside its cohort are weak links (0.26 or less) and explain no move.

What the peers imply: D'Ieteren's Sector RS (41) reaches 50 by the 30-Nov month-end only if the broken half keeps recovering; three sector stocks at Stock RS 12 or below hold it down. A D'Ieteren fall alongside Inchcape, Ayvens and Auto1 without Belron news would show that its car arm is setting the price; a rise with Sixt and Inter Cars on D'Ieteren's own volume would show that Belron and parts are.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts have raised Hilton Food's nearer years faster than FY2028: the recovery is being pulled forward, not lifted to a higher trajectory. Hilton Food's financial year ends in December; figures are in sterling.

  • EPS, FY2026: 52.2p; +1.5% over 1M, +6.9% over 3M, +9.7% over 6M; (16.7%) over 12M, after the November 2025 profit warning.
  • EPS, FY2027: 56.9p; +1.0%, +6.0%, +8.1%; (18.1%) over 12M.
  • EPS, FY2028: 62.1p; (0.7%) over 1M, +2.9% over 3M, +3.9% over 6M; (18.6%) over 12M.
  • Implied EPS growth: FY2027 over FY2026 +9.0% and FY2028 over FY2027 +9.1%, from +9.8% and +12.5% at the July month-end. Analysts have kept the FY2027 step and shaved the FY2028 step by about a quarter.
  • Revenue over 3M: FY2026 +6.1% (£4,606m), FY2027 +5.4%, FY2028 +4.3%. EBITDA over 3M: FY2026 +2.7% (£145.7m), FY2027 +2.4%, FY2028 +0.7%.
  • Implied EBITDA margin, falling: FY2026 3.16%, FY2027 3.26%, FY2028 3.30%, against 3.27%, 3.35% and 3.42% three months ago. Revenue rises faster than EBITDA because raw-material inflation passes through Hilton Food's cost-plus contracts at cost (earnings review); EPS rises faster than EBITDA, so the upgrades come from below EBITDA (the Dalco sale removing a loss-making business, which lost £2.0m at the adjusted operating level in the first half of 2026 per Deutsche Bank, and on which Shore Capital raised its estimate, both in the AlphaSense research of 2-Nov; and lower finance costs).

Every Hilton Food estimate change since 30-Sep came on one day, 8-Oct, and nothing has moved since. On 8-Oct FY2027 EPS rose 1.0%, revenue 1.3% and EBITDA 0.7%, and the average PT 6.4%, on the day the number of analysts counted fell by one (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)). No estimate changed from 9-Oct to 1-Nov.

Hilton Food's SP has run far ahead of its estimates: since the 23-Jun low the SP has risen 44.4%, and FY2027 EPS has risen 7.4% since the June month-end; over 3 months 21.1% against 6.0%. On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". The estimates support that judgement: the re-rating, not the earnings, has carried the SP since July, and FY2028 is being trimmed. The AlphaSense research of 2-Nov judges the FY2026 guidance (adjusted pre-tax profit £66m to £71m) "likely to be met or modestly raised", which would be a leg of delivery at the January 2027 trading update, not before. Test: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's cleared branch (the Canada launch in January 2027 on time; mid-single-digit core growth in FY2027, West core meat volumes flat to +1%, against +0.6% in the first half).

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings follow its SP: the Buys came back after the advance, as they peaked at the 2025 top.

  • Hilton Food had 5 Buys and 1 other at the May 2025 month-end, the month of its 905.1p closing high, before a 49.6% fall; Buys fell to 3 of 5 after the November 2025 profit warning and to 2 of 4 by July 2026 as coverage fell from 7 analysts to 4. Buys rose to 4 of 5 at 30-Sep, after the results, and 4 of 4 at 31-Oct, when the analyst with another rating left the count. These ratings follow momentum, not valuation: none was raised at the 29-Jan low.

Hilton Food's SP is closing on its average PT again: the gap is 8.3%, from 11.2% on 8-Oct.

  • Hilton Food's average PT has been 795p since 8-Oct (747p on 29-Sep; 705p at the August month-end; 667p at June). The gap between the average PT and the SP was 49% at the 29-Jan low (679p against 455.7p), 17% at the May 2025 high (1,061p against 905.1p) and 3.9% at the 30-Sep month-end, the narrowest since at least August 2024.
  • The 8-Oct step looks like a change in the analysts counted (5 to 4 that day), not a raise; no 8-Oct PT raise is in the sources read.
  • Named PTs: Deutsche Bank 850p from 815p, Buy (3-Sep, earnings review); Berenberg 810p from 790p (7-Sep, Hargreaves Lansdown, as read for the 28-Oct note). Shore Capital raised its FY2026 adjusted pre-tax profit estimate to £66.0m from £62.5m (AlphaSense research, 2-Nov).

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure but fading, and one analyst is cutting FY2027 EPS.

  • At the 31-Oct month-end: FY2027 EPS +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5); FY2027 EBITDA +60% (3 raising, none cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, none cutting); PT +50% (2 raising, none cutting, of 4).
  • At the 30-Sep month-end the same measures read +50%, +83%, +50% and +60%. The falls are September's raises leaving the 100-day count, not new cuts; with no estimate change since 8-Oct, the breadth will keep drifting toward zero until a new event brings new raises.
  • Where an analyst is cutting: one of Hilton Food's five analysts has cut FY2027 EPS, a cut carried in the count since the 30-Sep month-end (4 raising and 1 cutting then). The sources read do not name the broker or its logic. The only stated reasons for lower EPS in the sources are below EBITDA: Deutsche Bank's post-results forecasts "incorporating updated foreign exchange rates and borrowing costs" (AlphaSense research, 2-Nov), consistent with FY2028 EPS slipping 0.7% in a month while FY2028 EBITDA is flat (hypothesis).

Precedent: Hilton Food's estimate breadth turned before its SP last time; now the SP leads and the breadth fades. EBITDA and PT breadth turned positive at the April 2026 month-end (+33% and +20%), eleven weeks before Hilton Food crossed its absolute 200D MA on 15-Jul. Test: new raises after the January 2027 trading update, or the first PT cut since the March 2026 month-end.

9. Peers

Hilton Food led its cohort up from July; in October the staples rotation lifted the laggards faster, and Hilton Food was the cohort's slowest riser over the month (Greencore +22.8%, Cranswick +8.6%, Bell Food Group +7.4%, LDC +6.3%, Hilton Food +5.1%, to 2-Nov).

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Greencore and Bell have strong technical momentum vs. Cranswick; it's the lower-quality names "catching a bid" vs. the slow and steady names (risky, but working ATM)". Still true of Greencore and Bell Food Group, but over the last month the slow and steady names have caught a bid too: Cranswick's Stock RS rose from 33 to 48 and LDC's from 38 to 51, and every cohort stock closed above its 50D MA on 2-Nov.

Your four peer checks:

  • Sell-side estimates: Bell Food Group's 3M EPS revision is +10%, Hilton Food's +8%, Greencore's +3%, Cranswick's 0% and LDC's (1%). Hilton Food has the cohort's highest revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (+40%); LDC's is (25%), the cohort's only negative.
  • SP technicals: Hilton Food and Bell Food Group are at 52W closing highs; Greencore is 2.1% below its high but 14.6% above its 50D MA, the most stretched; LDC is 11.4% below its high. Hilton Food and Bell Food Group are the leaders consolidating, Greencore the extended mover.
  • Newsflow: Greencore raised its FY2026 adjusted operating profit guidance to about £248m on 13-Oct (Just Food and Hargreaves Lansdown, as read for the 28-Oct note); Berenberg (22-Sep) and Peel Hunt (12-Oct) upgraded Cranswick to Buy. The causes of Greencore's heavy volume and LDC's 4.4% rise on 2-Nov are not in the sources read.
  • Company guidance and communications: Greencore's is the only guidance change in the cohort in the sources read since Hilton Food's own of 3-Sep; Greencore's full results on 1-Dec are the next read-across before Hilton Food's January update.

Who turned first: Hilton Food (absolute 200D MA crossed 15-Jul), then Bell Food Group (13-Aug), then Greencore in October on its upgrade, then Cranswick and LDC with the rotation and without estimate support. Hilton Food now trades with the cohort on staples days (2-Nov) and on Greencore's news days (13 and 14-Oct). Your note's sector narrative, "retailers outsourcing more to companies like Hilton and Greencore", is the Sector Digest's strongest theme, rated Big (23-Oct).

On 28 to 31-Oct you asked: "Q — Risk from Tesco and CEE?". From the AlphaSense research of 2-Nov:

  • Tesco, a slow risk to volume and a faster one to margin. Tesco removed Hilton Food's Scottish beef volumes in the first half of 2026 by moving to an all-Scottish supply chain; the renewal date and terms of the Tesco agreement are not disclosed. The research judges renewal on stable terms likely, as switching costs are high, but Tesco's savings programme "could force Hilton to pass back more efficiency savings than expected". The A&J Memo's Alarm is any announced Tesco in-sourcing or re-tender.
  • Central and Eastern Europe, a growth leg more than a risk. Central Europe fresh prepared food volumes rose 26% in the first half on supply to Żabka, the Polish convenience chain, and management is scoping up to £30m of Polish capacity, decided around year-end and spent from 2027 (earnings review; Shore Capital). The risk the sources name is the cash cost of building Poland and Canada at once; dependence on one customer, Żabka, is my inference.

What the peers imply: if the rotation holds, the laggards' catch-up supports Hilton Food's Sector RS; if it fades (the 6-Nov Friday reading), the precedent is 1-Oct, when the cohort fell 1.6% and Hilton Food 2.4%: Hilton Food is not shielded from a staples sell-off.

What the rules say for Hilton Food now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • A breakout without volume or news: Hilton Food closed at a 52W high on 2-Nov after a five-week base on its lowest volume of the year, but on 0.64 times the 50D average and on a cohort-wide up day (items 1 and 2).
  • The SP has outrun the estimates: the SP is up 44.4% from 23-Jun against FY2027 EPS +7.4% since the June month-end; no estimate has moved since 8-Oct and FY2028 is being trimmed (items 4, 5 and 6). Your "another fundamental leg of delivery" has no dated company event before December.
  • The group is now behind the stock: Consumer staples' and Food production's RS are above their lines, and Hilton Food's laggard peers are catching up (items 3 and 9).

Tranches 1 (1.3%) and 2 (2.0%) are clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule. Both use the 50D MA (693.9p) and its stop-loss (652.2p, the MA less 6%): a fall of 5.5% from the 734.0p close starts a test, one of 11.1% confirms an exit at once. The 8-Jul-2026 confirmation was cleared under your 2-Nov-2026 ruling (a confirmation whose price had since closed back above the MA is cleared), and both tranches are judged afresh from 2-Nov. On dividend-corrected prices the MA is 684.8p and the stop-loss 643.7p; the 2-Nov note's For your ruling on which MA the rule reads stands.

The nearest sell test is the 50D MA, about one usual weekly move (4.9%) below.

  • Pace: the close's margin above the 50D MA has narrowed from 13.7% on 4-Aug to 7.3% on 5-Oct, 5.8% on 26-Oct and 4.0% on 30-Oct, and is 5.8% on 2-Nov. At a flat SP the rising MA reaches about 703p by Fri 6-Nov (a 4.3% fall to start a test) and 721p by 30-Nov (1.7%).
  • What the rule then does: Hilton Food's down days run on light volume, so the volume test would be slow to confirm; an exit would more likely come from three bottom-half or three lower closes at or below the MA, or a close at or below the stop-loss. A confirmed exit sells both tranches at the following Tuesday session. No other test is within a usual weekly move: the relative-price test needs a fall of about 24%, to about 557p, by 30-Nov.
  • The record: Hilton Food last closed below its 50D MA on 10-Jul, two sessions after the 8-Jul confirmation your ruling cleared; the SP then rose 36% to 3-Sep. The September pullback held 11.9% above the MA.

Fri 6-Nov-2026 reads Consumer staples' Industry RS (66; a fall of 37 points, to below 30, would be needed) and the relative 200D MA (rising 3.12% over the month): information only for a Probing Bet. It is also the second of the two Fridays that enter Hilton Food in the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection (passed on 30-Oct).

The rules block an add: Hilton Food holds 2 tranches, a Probing Bet's maximum, at 3.3% of capital. On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote: "Only 3.3% of AUM — the position has been too small. Should've been willing to add on the Stage 2 trigger". The rules' route to an add is reclassification to a Middle Innings Core Investment: the six Sell Criteria alone would then decide a sale, adds would be allowed to 6.68%, and tranche 2 (2.0% at cost) would be split into 1.67% plus a 0.33% remainder, which the Position Management System offers to make.

  • For your ruling: whether to reclassify Hilton Food after the 6-Nov Pool reading.

Where Hilton Food stands for an add: the second stage, may be retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern (here, forming one); the 2-Nov breakout attempt does not yet qualify for the third, breaking out of a volatility contraction pattern. The Master Dashboard's tests point to the first stage, pulling back or basing (basing plausible, 3 of 4; volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4). I move it one stage on because the closing lows rose four times on the year's lowest volume; not to the third, because the break came on 0.64 times the 50D average. Your positive-retest trigger is partly present (the 2-Nov dip below the rising 20D, bought back on light volume); the broadening of the case is partly present (technicals and peers rising; guidance unchanged since 3-Sep, estimates since 8-Oct).

Dates: Greencore's results on 1-Dec; the Saudi joint venture (fourth quarter) and the Polish capacity decision (around year-end); December 2026, the A&J Memo's Alarm date for an FDA decision on Foppen; January 2027, the trading update and Canada launch; FY2026 results, 7-Apr-2027 (projected). No contact with Hilton Food's investor relations, which your note asked for ("Speak to IR ASAP"), is recorded in the sources read.

What would change this reading: a close above 734.0p on more than about 198,000 shares (the third stage, breaking out of a volatility contraction pattern); a close back below 726.4p on 1.25 times volume or more (a failed breakout, back to the first stage); a close at or below the 50D MA (a test, and adds suspended).

Richard decides.

Carlsberg broke out on 2-Nov: its SP closed at DKK 1,005.5, above the DKK 981.8 closing high of 12-Feb, the ceiling near which its July (DKK 964.8, 30-Jul) and October (DKK 980.6, 22-Oct) advances also stopped, a 52W high and its highest close since 23-May-2023. Carlsberg's 5-session rise of 3.9% is 0.8 times its usual weekly move.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's breakout has the volume of a shakeout and a reversal, not yet of a breakout. The heaviest selling of the advance (27 and 29-Oct) was reversed within a session by its heaviest buying (30-Oct), but the 2-Nov close above DKK 981.8 came on only 1.24x the 50D average volume.

  • 15-Oct, accumulation: +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x), 321,386 shares, DKK 299.9m, close at 0.89 of the day's range, gap +1.6%; mostly the group's (brewers +2.6%).

  • 30-Oct, accumulation: +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), 567,548 shares, DKK 551.1m. Carlsberg gapped up 2.2% to open at DKK 955.0, the day's low, traded to DKK 982.0 (above the 22-Oct closing high of DKK 980.6 and the 12-Feb ceiling of DKK 981.8 intraday) and closed at DKK 971.0, at 0.59 of the range. It is Carlsberg's heaviest volume since the 29-May index-review day and its heaviest up-day volume since 25-Nov-2024 (chart file).
  • Below the line: 29-Oct (3.0%) on 1.30x (DKK 273.4m), a 2.0% gap down and a close on the low; 2-Nov +3.6% on 1.24x (DKK 294.8m), a close on the high.

Distribution and accumulation in 25 sessions: the volume script flags 2 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct) against 5 accumulation days (7, 14, 15, 28 and 30-Oct); the dashboard counts 1 distribution day, from 4 at 5-Oct. The only fresh selling clustered around the statement.

Up-day against down-day volume: 1.32 over 5 sessions and 1.07 over 20 sessions (dashboard; 1.07 without mechanical-volume days). Over the whole advance from 18-Sep (+19.6%, 31 sessions) it is 1.06, with 2 heavy up days and 1 heavy down day. That is up from 0.97 at 27-Oct, but still thinner than Carlsberg's September 2025 to February 2026 advance (1.26, 9 heavy up days) and its March to May 2026 advance (1.16, 3), and only a little above its June to July 2026 advance (1.02, 1), which gave way to the 12.9% fall.

Volume at the MAs: on 29-Oct Carlsberg's low (DKK 934.2) held 1.5% above the rising 20D MA (DKK 920.0) on 1.30x, and the next session reversed on 2.43x. The volume trend (20D average against the 50D average) is 1.23x and rising through the move.

Precedent: a heavy-volume high is not proof. Carlsberg's 12-Feb closing high (DKK 981.8) came on 1.65x, and 13-Feb fell 1.8% on 1.64x before the 20.0% fall to 24-Mar. What differs now: the heavy selling came before the high and was reversed on heavier buying, the reverse of February's order. Of the three completed declines in 12 months, only the 24-Mar low ended with heavy-volume up days within ten sessions; the June and September lows turned on 1.3x at most.

Carlsberg's relative price did not confirm the new SP high. On 2-Nov Carlsberg's SP closed at a 52W high (and its highest close since 23-May-2023), but its relative price against the Stoxx Europe 600 stood 6.3% below its own 52W high of 12-Feb. It is 8.4% above its relative 200D MA, which it crossed on 15-Oct.

Peers' volume: on 30-Oct Heineken traded 1.5x its 50D average, AB InBev 1.3x and Royal Unibrew 1.2x; on 2-Nov 1.19x, 0.85x and 1.47x. Carlsberg's buying was heavier than any peer's on 30-Oct; on 2-Nov Royal Unibrew's 1.47x was heavier than Carlsberg's 1.24x, so Carlsberg's breakout day was not the heaviest among its brewers.

Confirms the breakout: a Carlsberg close above DKK 1,005.5 on 1.5x the 50D average or more, closing in its top third, or a pullback towards DKK 981.8 on below-average volume that holds. Confirms a failed breakout: a close back below DKK 981.8, then below the 29-Oct low of DKK 934.2, on above-average volume.

3. RS

Carlsberg's Stock RS of 77 on 2-Nov is its highest reading since the series began on 30-Sep-2025, above the 74 that marked February's top. Each RS is a percentile rank from 0 to 99.

  • Trend: Carlsberg's Stock RS was 48 at the Fri 2-Oct reading, 73 at Fri 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct (the statement day), 70 at the Fri 30-Oct month-end and 77 on 2-Nov. It was 68 at the 31-Jul month-end and 22 at the 31-Oct-2025 month-end.
  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Stock RS was ~40 and now up to ~67 over L1M. How on earth can it move that much? Seems surprising. Check that it is correct". The readings are consistent with the data file. Carlsberg's relative price against the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 12.3% from 2-Oct (484.4) to 23-Oct (544.0) while its Stock RS rose 25 points (48 to 73), about 2.0 points per 1% of relative price. That rate is close to the 2.4 to 2.5 points per 1% seen from 23-Oct to 2-Nov, so a 25-point move in three weeks follows from a 12% outperformance of the market; the rank's method itself was not re-derived.
  • Beverages' Sector RS is 81 (51 at 30-Sep, 85 at 23-Oct, 70 on 29-Oct, 76 at 30-Oct); Consumer staples' Industry RS is 66 (40 at 30-Sep, 73 at 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct, 66 at 30-Oct). Both stand far above the lines the Sell Criteria read (Sector RS 50, Industry RS 30).
  • The order of the turn: Carlsberg's Stock RS and Beverages' Sector RS turned together at the Fri 25-Sep reading (41 to 51, and 36 to 48); Consumer staples' Industry RS followed from 9-Oct (46, from 33 at 2-Oct). Beverages' Sector RS overtook Carlsberg's Stock RS by 16-Oct (78 against 63), and Consumer staples' Industry RS drew level with it at 73 on 23-Oct. Since 29-Oct all three have risen together, but Carlsberg's Stock RS rose 18 points in two sessions against 11 for Beverages' Sector RS and 7 for Consumer staples' Industry RS: on these two days Carlsberg led its group rather than being carried by it.
  • The Industry Digest's test of 30-Oct for a durable rotation rather than de-grossing (Consumer staples' Industry RS above 50 at the 30-Oct and 6-Nov Friday readings) passed its first reading at 66.

Precedent: Carlsberg's Stock RS has twice topped in the high 60s and 70s, then fallen to the low 40s. It was 74 at the Fri 13-Feb reading, then 42 by 20-Mar after a 20.0% SP fall; it was 68 at the 31-Jul month-end, then 41 by 18-Sep after a 12.9% fall. The hypothesis of last week's note that the 70s are the top of Carlsberg's range is weakened, not refuted: Stock RS of 77 is a new high and the SP has cleared DKK 981.8, but relative price is still 6.3% below its February high. Refuted if Carlsberg's Stock RS holds 70 or more at the Fri 6-Nov and Fri 13-Nov readings with the SP above DKK 981.8.

Peers' Stock RS: AB InBev 70 (58 a month ago), Heineken 69 (41), Royal Unibrew 25 (11). Carlsberg now leads the cohort on Stock RS.

4. Consensus by named FY

Carlsberg's consensus turned up after the 29-Oct statement, but the rise is translation currency, not the business: estimates moved less than 1% while the SP rose 7.6% from the statement-day close.

  • The post-statement change (daily series of 28-Oct against 1-Nov, FY2027): EPS +0.6% (DKK 69.72 to 70.12), revenue +0.7%, EBITDA +0.5%. The brokers' stated reason is currency: Bernstein raised FY2027 EPS 1% "due to positive currency effects" (30-Oct); Deutsche Bank raised EPS 0.6% for FY2026 and 2.5% for FY2027 and FY2028 on foreign exchange (29-Oct); Rothschild & Co Redburn left FY2026 unchanged because packaging cost rises were offset by translation gains (29-Oct; earnings review). Carlsberg itself raised its translation tailwind to DKK 100m, from neutral, while reiterating 4% to 6% organic operating profit growth (statement, 28-Oct; earnings review).
  • The chart's 1, 3 and 6-month changes: FY2026 EPS DKK 65.09, +0.2%, +0.5% and +0.8%; FY2027 EPS DKK 70.12, +0.4%, (0.6%) and (1.0%); FY2028 EPS DKK 76.47, +0.5%, +0.2% and (0.3%). FY2026 EBITDA is flat over the month and (1.7%) over three months.
  • The trajectory: implied EPS growth of 7.7% (FY2026 to FY2027) and 9.1% (FY2027 to FY2028), on revenue growth of 4.4% and 3.6% and an EBITDA margin rising from 21.0% to 21.3% and 21.7%: a synergy and insourcing case more than a volume case.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "MAJOR PROBLEM - Consensus EPS has been steadily falling through 2026. Same for EBITDA and Revenue. EBITDA VERY much so." Partly confirmed: the fall ran from 31-Mar, not through the whole of 2026, and has paused since. Over 2026 to date Carlsberg's FY2026 EPS is up 3.7% (DKK 62.80 at 31-Dec-2025 to DKK 65.09) and FY2027 EPS up 1.0% (DKK 69.40 to DKK 70.12). Over the same period FY2028 EPS is down 2.5% (DKK 78.44 to DKK 76.47), FY2027 EBITDA down 1.0% (DKK 20,824m to DKK 20,609m) and FY2028 revenue down 2.0%, while FY2027 revenue is up 0.3%. Within the year, Carlsberg's FY2027 EPS fell 2.1% from its 31-Mar high (DKK 71.12) to 31-Aug (DKK 69.65); FY2028 EPS fell 3.4% from 31-Dec-2025 (DKK 78.44) to 31-Aug (DKK 75.76); FY2027 EBITDA fell 2.1% from 31-Mar (DKK 20,959m) to 30-Sep (DKK 20,512m), most of it in August and September. Since those lows FY2027 EPS is up 0.7% and FY2027 EBITDA 0.5%, almost all of it currency.

And: "What is driving the SS downgrades?" The sources read name four: China volume, (6%) in the second quarter and (10%) in the third (Morgan Stanley, 29-Oct); beer volume, (3.5%) for the group in the third quarter (Bernstein, 29-Oct); soft-drink mix diluting gross margin (Jefferies, 29-Oct); and 2027 packaging inflation (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 29-Oct).

The link: the SP led, and the estimates have not followed beyond currency. Carlsberg's SP rose 17.3% over the month from the 2-Oct close (17.5% over 20 sessions from 5-Oct, as in item 1 (SP and MAs)) while FY2027 EPS rose 0.4% from 30-Sep. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's FY2027 EPS test (modal DKK 69 to 71, alarm below DKK 66) is met at DKK 70.12. Test: whether FY2027 and FY2028 EPS keep rising once the currency changes are in, through to the FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's SP has reached its average PT: DKK 1,005.5 against DKK 1,043.5, a gap of 3.8%, the narrowest at any reading since at least January 2023. The next narrowest month-end gap was 5.8% (May 2025), after which the SP fell 21.3% to its low of 30-Sep-2025, four months later; at 10.4% (February 2026) and 10.7% (July 2026) it fell 17.5% to the 24-Mar low and 12.1% to the 18-Sep low. Hypothesis: the average PT acts as a ceiling unless PTs rise. Against it: the post-statement PT changes are up, and three of the lower PTs date from September and early October.

The latest PT changes, newest first (earnings review; the earlier ones from last week's note):

  • After the statement: Bernstein, Outperform, PT DKK 1,550 from DKK 1,535 (30-Oct); Deutsche Bank, Buy, PT up 3.4% (29-Oct; level not in the sources read); Rothschild & Co Redburn, Buy, DKK 1,100, and Morgan Stanley, Overweight, DKK 1,075, both unchanged (29-Oct).
  • DNB Carnegie, Buy, PT DKK 995 from DKK 990 (15-Oct); the Sector Digest records DNB Carnegie's Daniel Andersen keeping a trading sell on China volume (9-Oct), so the house holds two views.
  • The PTs now below the SP: Barclays DKK 930 (Equal Weight, 12-Oct), Jefferies DKK 950 (Hold, 13-Sep) and HSBC DKK 980 (Hold); Carlsberg's SP of DKK 1,005.5 is 2.6% to 8.1% above them.

Ratings: the last Sell has gone and the Buys have not grown. At 31-Oct 20 analysts rated Carlsberg a Buy, 8 a Hold and none a Sell (20, 6 and 1 at 30-Sep; 21, 5 and 1 at 31-Aug). The Sell that went is Barclays' (DKK 775, 10-Sep), upgraded to Equal Weight on 12-Oct. The Buy share fell from 78% (31-Aug) to 71%. Ratings again move against the SP (the Buy share falls while the SP rises), as they did from September 2024 to June 2025.

Net Upgrade Breadth: positive on the Sell Criterion's count, mixed on the monthly chart.

  • The 30-Oct month-end count (100-day window) puts revenue breadth at +48% (13 raising, 1 cutting, of 25) and PT breadth at +16% (10 raising, 6 cutting, of 25), from (17%) and (22%) at 30-Sep.
  • The monthly chart at 31-Oct: EPS 0% (11 raising, 11 cutting, of 26), from (32%) at 30-Sep; EBITDA +32% (12 and 5, of 22), from (29%). Its revenue (8%) and PT (21%) are the pre-statement figures of 26-Oct and do not match the 30-Oct count, a fault in the dashboard's monthly chart data.

Where analysts are cutting: Rothschild & Co Redburn cut its FY2027 organic revenue growth by 50 basis points to 3.0% (29-Oct) on "persistent consumer weakness in China that is likely to continue through the first half of 2027". Bernstein trimmed FY2026 EPS 0.1% (30-Oct). The six PT cutters in the 30-Oct count are not named in the sources read beyond HSBC (DKK 1,000 to 980) and Jefferies (DKK 1,100 to 950, 13-Sep, on growth doubts).

On 28-Oct you wrote: "MAJOR PROBLEM - Very clear. The entire set of NUB is negative". No longer so at the 30-Oct month-end: revenue and PT breadth turned positive on the Sell Criterion's count after the statement, and EPS and EBITDA breadth reached 0% and +32%. The raises are mostly currency, so the breadth is real but shallow.

9. Peers

Carlsberg and Heineken lead the brewers to new 52W highs together; AB InBev, with the best estimates, lags on price; Royal Unibrew shares the daily moves but not the trend.

  • SP technicals: on 2-Nov Carlsberg and Heineken both closed at 52W highs; AB InBev closed 2.4% below its 22-Oct high; Royal Unibrew closed 27.9% below its 23-Feb high and below its absolute 200D MA. From the 29-Oct close to 2-Nov Carlsberg rose 7.6%, Royal Unibrew 5.0%, AB InBev and Heineken 4.7% each, and Diageo 6.1%.
  • Who turned first: Carlsberg (its 1-Oct higher low), then the group from 9-Oct; since 30-Oct Carlsberg has led.
  • Sell-side estimates: over three months AB InBev's EPS rose 3% and its revenue 3%, Heineken's EPS 1%, Royal Unibrew's 0%, Carlsberg's (1%). Revenue Net Upgrade Breadth on the monthly chart: Heineken +21%, Royal Unibrew +13%, Carlsberg (8%), AB InBev (12%). Carlsberg leads on price and trails on estimates.
  • Newsflow and guidance: Heineken (28-Oct) reported organic revenue +4.6% and double-digit China growth, and reiterated its guidance; AB InBev kept its guidance, with China volume (17%) in the earnings review (Rothschild & Co Redburn) but (11%) in the Sector Digest. On 29-Oct the market sold the brewers that only kept guidance (Carlsberg (3.0%), AB InBev (3.2%), Campari (6.6%)) while Heineken held (Sector Digest, 30-Oct).

On 28-Oct you wrote: "Heineken reported strong growth in China. That is negative for Carlsberg, given the reported weakness in China". Partly supported. For: Heineken grew double digits in China in the quarter in which Carlsberg's China volume fell 10%, so Heineken gained share. Against: Carlsberg's (10%) is in line with Chongqing Brewery's market and better than AB InBev's, and Carlsberg says wholesaler inventory was back to prior-year levels by end-September (earnings review). The market's verdict since 30-Oct is that China is an industry problem, not a Carlsberg one: Carlsberg has beaten Heineken by 2.9 points since the statement-day close.

And: "Isn't this what Jefferies have been saying? China weakness for Carlsberg, down to sell/hold?" Not China: Jefferies' 13-Sep downgrade to Hold ("Growth Losing Its Fizz") rested on a 0.3% embedded volume rate and an unproven 4% to 6% growth algorithm (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 1-Oct); on 29-Oct its concern was soft-drink mix and 2027 packaging costs. China is DNB Carnegie's and Morgan Stanley's concern.

What the peers imply for Carlsberg: the breakout is shared with Heineken, which has the stronger China print and better revenue breadth, so the group's move, not only Carlsberg's, sets Beverages' Sector RS and Carlsberg's Stock RS into the 30-Nov month-end. A Carlsberg breakout that Heineken confirms is stronger than one Carlsberg makes alone; AB InBev's failure to regain its 22-Oct high is the cohort's weak point.

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: Carlsberg has broken out, but on price, not on volume or relative strength against the market. The SP closed above its DKK 981.8 ceiling at a 52W high with Stock RS at a series-high 77, while relative price stayed 6.3% below its February high and breakout-day volume was 1.24x its 50D average.
  • Second: the estimates have not followed beyond currency, and the SP has reached its average PT. FY2027 EPS rose 0.6% after the statement, on translation gains; the average PT is 3.8% above the SP, the narrowest gap since at least January 2023.
  • Third: China crossed the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's alarm, and the market looked through it. The memo's alarm is second-half China volume down more than 3%; the third quarter was (10%). The memo's invalidation is 2027 down again, not yet tested.
  • The rules today: no sell signal from any of Carlsberg's six Sell Criteria; no tranche is due for sale; the holding is held. The 30-Sep sell signal from the Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS test ended at the 30-Oct reading.
  • The nearest sell test, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end): Carlsberg's Stock RS is 77, 8 points from below 70; it moved +4 points in 1 week (73 at 23-Oct), +29 in 4 weeks (48 at 2-Oct) and +9 in 13 weeks (68 at 31-Jul). Beverages' Sector RS is 81, 32 points from below 50; it moved (4), +31 and +13 points over the same weeks. Carlsberg's Stock RS moved about 2.5 points per 1% of relative price on 29 and 30-Oct; at that rate about 3% of underperformance of the Stoxx Europe 600 takes Carlsberg's Stock RS below 70, but a sell signal also needs Beverages' Sector RS to fall 32 points, as it fell 30 in three sessions in August.
  • How the tests interact: a Carlsberg-only fall meets the Stock RS half alone; a group-wide fall could meet both halves at once. The relative-price test needs DKK 804.76 at a flat market (20.0% below the close) and a tranche's 30% fall DKK 703.85 (30.0% below). A whole-holding sell signal sells Carlsberg when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link.
  • The record: this test caught Carlsberg's last two tops late. After the 12-Feb top it was met at the 31-Mar month-end (Stock RS 44, Sector RS 48) at DKK 803.2, 18.2% below the high and 2.3% above the low; after the 30-Jul top at the 31-Aug month-end (Stock RS 49, Sector RS 47) at DKK 883.4, 8.4% below the high and 5.1% above the low.
  • The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov: Industry RS below 30 (Consumer staples 66) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (+0.49%); neither can plausibly give a sell signal.
  • Adds: the rules allow an add (no sell signal; 2.70% of room below the 6.68% limit, one 1.67% tranche).
  • Where Carlsberg stands for an add: none of the three. It closed at a 52W high, so it is not pulling back or basing, not retesting a rising MA, and not breaking out of a volatility contraction pattern. The Master Dashboard agrees: pulling back possible (2 of 4), basing possible (2 of 4), healthy retest possible (3 of 6, no MA retested), volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4. My reading: a breakout from a nine-month base, not from a volatility contraction pattern. It would move to "healthily retesting a rising MA" on a pullback to DKK 981.8 or to the rising 20D MA (DKK 933.2) on light volume that holds and bounces.
  • Your add triggers: a positive retest of a rising MA is absent (29-Oct held 1.5% above the rising 20D MA without reaching it); a broadening of the case is partly present (sell-side estimates and breadth up, on currency; technicals and peers up; guidance reiterated, not raised).
  • The dates: the FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027, the memo's leverage and buyback test. What would change this reading: the volume tests at the end of item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).

Richard decides.

Middle Innings Core Investment · 4.95% of capital in 4 tranches (tranche 1, 1.20%, bought 30-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 2.66%, bought 22-Jul-2026; tranche 3, 0.43%, bought 7-Aug-2026; tranche 4, 0.66%, bought 12-Aug-2026) · readings as at Mon 2-Nov-2026

Hexatronic's breakout above its July closing high has failed on closes: after three closes above 45.42 (28 to 30-Oct), Hexatronic closed at 44.28 on 2-Nov, 6.3% below its 29-Oct close of 47.28 and 8.9% below its 52W closing high of 48.58 (3-Jun).

  • Hexatronic's latest legs: 5 to 20-Oct, 42.81 to 38.76, (9.5%); 20 to 29-Oct, 38.76 to 47.28, +22.0% on seven straight up days; 29-Oct to 2-Nov, 47.28 to 44.28, (6.3%) in two sessions.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file.

  • Hexatronic's 20 to 29-Oct leg was its own, driven by its third-quarter report of 22-Oct: sales SEK 2,498m against a four-contributor consensus of SEK 2,256m; organic growth 17% against 8.1%; adjusted EBITA SEK 281m against SEK 233m; diluted EPS SEK 0.81 against SEK 0.59 (Hexatronic press release, earnings review of 22-Oct).   • Hexatronic's 2-Nov fall of 3.3% was its own: the equal-weighted European index rose about 0.7%, Prysmian 0.8% and NKT 1.0%; Hexatronic's beta of 1.31, measured against the Stoxx Europe 600 and applied here to the equal-weighted index's 0.7% rise, implies a rise of about 0.9%, so about four points of the 3.3% fall were stock-specific. No Hexatronic announcement after 22-Oct is in the sources read.
  • Hypothesis 1 (the stronger evidence): a normal pullback, giving back about a third of a 22.0% run. For it: both down days came on below-average volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high), Volume), estimates have not moved (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus) and every trend-health test below passes. Hypothesis 2: a repeat of the June top. For it: Hexatronic's first two sessions after its 3-Jun high also fell on light volume, and that fall ran to 31.3%; supply at 46 to 47 has now turned Hexatronic back twice (22-Oct, a 46.03 high and a 40.94 close; 30-Oct, a 46.90 high and a 45.78 close, the session after the 47.28 close). Test: the first touch of the rising 20D MA (42.40; the 2-Nov close of 44.28 is 4.4% above it).

Hexatronic's MAs are all rising except the 100D, and the SP has crossed none since it reclaimed the 20D, 50D, 100D and 150D MAs on 22-Oct on 6.18m shares. Hexatronic's 20D MA is 42.40 (+7.4% over 20 sessions), its 50D 40.67 (+3.1%), its 150D 40.05 (+3.8%) and its absolute 200D MA 37.00 (+5.6%); the 100D (39.94) slipped 0.9% as July's spike left it. At Hexatronic's earlier turns the lows closed at or near the rising 150D MA, each higher since 18-Sep (37.13, 38.29, 38.76); the 50D, 100D and 150D MAs now sit within 1.8% of each other, the zone where the last two pullbacks ended.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Hexatronic against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; 37.00, up 5.6% over 20 sessions.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes; 40.05 against 37.00.

Basis: the 31-Aug and 18-Sep mechanical-volume days sit inside Hexatronic's 50D average and lower the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's ratios by about 11%. The ratios below are the volume evidence script's, without those days; the dashboard's follow in brackets.

Hexatronic's pullback is being sold on light volume, and its advance was bought on heavy volume: the pattern you read on 31-Oct still holds after two down days.

Hexatronic's volume fell through the advance and stayed low on the pullback, but the June precedent says light selling is no guarantee for this stock.

  • Shares traded ran from 6.18m (22-Oct) to 1.83m (29-Oct), then 0.76m and 1.30m, against a 50D average of 1.41m (1.27m without the mechanical-volume days).

July's decline (20.3%) began with a flat session on heavy volume (17-Jul, 0.0% on 1.68x), then fell 5.5% on 0.82x (20-Jul) and 5.5% on 1.15x (21-Jul), on the same basis as June's figures. Both of Hexatronic's last two declines therefore began with a light-volume down day, so the current two light-volume down days do not tell a pullback from a repeat of either.

Hexatronic's relative price confirmed the advance but has not held it. On 29-Oct Hexatronic's relative price (25.9998) passed its July high (25.69, 17-Jul), its first higher high since the 3-Jun 52W high (27.96), while still 7.0% below that high. Peers on the same days: Hexatronic's heavy days were shared with its peers on 2, 15 and 26-Oct and split on 22-Oct (Nokia (9.8%) on 2.6x); on 2-Nov the peers traded on 0.6x to 1.0x, so there was no heavy selling anywhere in Hexatronic's group.

What confirms the advance continues: a Hexatronic close above 47.28 on more than 1.41m shares, in the top third of the range, and then above 48.58. What confirms a turn: a distribution day (1.25x or more on a down day) closing below the rising 20D MA (42.40), or a third session of supply at 46 to 47.
