4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts raised Hexatronic's nearer years faster than FY2028 after the report: the third quarter was added to the base, not to the trajectory.

  • Hexatronic's FY2027 EPS rose 10.3% in the month to 1-Nov (2.672 to 2.946), 10.9% over 3 months and 14.6% over 6; FY2026 EPS rose 7.2% (2.26 to 2.422); FY2028 EPS 7.0% (3.072 to 3.286), 7.7% over 3 months and 13.9% over 6.
  • The turning point: Hexatronic's FY2027 EPS was flat from 31-Jul to 21-Oct (2.656 to 2.672) while the SP rose 15.3% from its 18-Sep low to 5-Oct; it then jumped 5.5% on 22-Oct and 4.8% on 23-Oct, and has not moved since. The SP led the estimates into the report and then moved with them.
  • Implied growth: FY2026 to FY2027 EPS growth rose from 18.2% (30-Sep) to 21.6%, while FY2027 to FY2028 fell from 15.0% to 11.5%. Revenue agrees: FY2027 +3.8% in the month, FY2028 +3.2%.
  • Implied margin: Hexatronic's FY2027 EBITDA margin is 14.0%, from 13.9% at 30-Sep; FY2028's is unchanged at 14.3%. FY2027 EBITDA rose 4.5% against EPS 10.3%, so most of the EPS rise sits below EBITDA (lower interest, with adjusted leverage down to 1.3 times from 1.7; earnings review, 22-Oct), not in a higher operating run rate.

The link to the report and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo: the revision is larger than the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo expected, and it fits management's caution. Management issued no formal upgrade and said Data Center would normalise in the fourth quarter and that glass fibre and some connectors were restricting growth (earnings review, 22-Oct). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (27-Oct) expected a 5% to 9% revision burst; FY2027's 10.3% exceeds it, and consensus FY2027 EPS (2.946) now sits 6.0% above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's base case (2.78) and 3.4% above Redeye's post-results 2.85, though below the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's upside case (3.2). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's view is that "Consensus is already at our level; there is no annual upgrade cycle to harvest" and that the skew turns negative from the second quarter of 2027. Test: the year-end report of 4-Feb-2027, which the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo gives a 70% chance of beating the four-contributor consensus.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "FX is likely to be a big headwind, similar to Xvivo - major fundamental risk; read the memo". The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo judges the krona a second-order risk for Hexatronic's EPS: "a 10% stronger krona removes 9% of reported revenue but only about 3% of EPS (SEK 0.08) because production is local". The krona is one of the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's two swing variables for 2027 and sits inside its downside case (a krona 7% stronger with weaker North American volume: 2027 EPS SEK 2.1). For Hexatronic it is a risk to reported revenue more than to EPS; North American volume is the larger risk in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's arithmetic.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Every Hexatronic analyst with a rating is now a Buy, for the first time since March 2025; the last time, the SP kept falling.

  • Hexatronic's ratings went from 3 Buys of 4 (30-Sep) to 4 of 4 at the 31-Oct month-end; the broker that upgraded is not named in the sources read. Five analysts publish estimates and four ratings.
  • Over 24 months Hexatronic's analysts followed valuation in the 2025 decline and results in the 2026 advance. With 4 Buys of 4 at the February and March-2025 month-ends, the SP fell from 30.97 to 24.73, and every Buy went only at the July-2025 month-end (20.89), four months before the 16.70 low. In 2026 Buys were added after the February and July reports, and dropped at the March and June month-ends with no report.
  • Hypothesis: a full house of Buys marks a crowded consensus; unlike 2025, estimates are now rising behind it.

Hexatronic's average PT has followed the SP; the gap has widened again only because the SP fell.

  • Hexatronic's average PT rose 4.5% in October (47.6 to 48.6 in the 22-Oct daily file and to 49.75 by the 28-Oct file) and has more than doubled since the July-2025 month-end (22.62). It stood 5.2% above the SP at the 29-Oct close, and 12.4% above it on 2-Nov.
  • Precedent: at the May-2026 month-end Hexatronic's SP was 7.2% above the average PT (43.95 against 41.0), three sessions before the 3-Jun high and the 31.3% fall. On 29-Oct Hexatronic's SP came within 5.2% of it.
  • Redeye published no new PT after the report (pre-results Base Case SEK 44; earnings review, 22-Oct); the brokers behind the PT rises between 22 and 28-Oct are not named in the sources read.

Hexatronic's Net Upgrade Breadth is +100% on every measure, from +20% or less at 30-Sep. Net Upgrade Breadth for FY2027 EPS and for FY2027 EBITDA is +100%, with five of five analysts raising; Net Upgrade Breadth for revenue and for the PT at the 30-Oct month-end is +100% too, five of five on the Sell Criterion's count (four of four in the monthly series). They diverge only in size: EPS +10.3% against revenue +3.8% and the average PT +4.5%.

No Hexatronic analyst is cutting an estimate, a PT, a rating or breadth; there is no broker logic on cuts to report.

Precedents for breadth at +100%: after PT breadth reached +100% at the February and March-2026 month-ends, Hexatronic rose 98% from its 5-Feb close to the 3-Jun high; in July, as revenue, EBITDA and PT breadth went to +100% after the 15-Jul report, the SP fell 20.3% in the nine sessions to 29-Jul and then ranged for three months. Test: the 30-Nov month-end, for whether breadth holds once the report has been absorbed and whether any PT is cut while the SP sits near 45.

9. Peers

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Weird it's not sold off in October in line with the grid names". Hexatronic did sell off with them until its report, then left them behind. From 5 to 20-Oct Hexatronic fell 9.5% with Swedish technology and the optical names. Over October Hexatronic rose 15.3% while Prysmian fell 11.0%, NKT 4.7%, Nokia 13.7% and Mycronic 11.0% (chart files). The difference is Hexatronic's 22-Oct report (item 1 (SP and MAs)). When the group sold together on 30-Oct, Hexatronic fell with it (3.2%, against Prysmian (3.9%) and NKT (4.0%)).

Hexatronic's four peer checks: the economic peers' newsflow and guidance are strong; the listed cohort's technicals are split, and Hexatronic is the one holding near its high.

  • Sell-side estimates: Hexatronic's 3-month EPS revision (+11%) is the highest in its sector table; Nokia's is +3% with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +50%, Kitron's (5%), and Ericsson's and Adtran Networks' are not supplied.
  • SP technicals: Nokia is broken, 47.2% below its 52W high, 13.3% below its absolute 200D MA, Stock RS 44 from 94 a month ago. Ericsson is the other strong cohort member, +16.8% over the month and 11.0% above its 50D MA, Stock RS 68 from 42. Prysmian is 29.1% and NKT 23.8% below their 52W highs; Hexatronic is 8.9% below its own.
  • Newsflow: Nokia's third-quarter report (22-Oct) showed Optical Networks sales up 18% and artificial intelligence and cloud sales up 123%, yet Nokia fell 9.8% that day (Nokia, 22-Oct, as in last week's note). Corning's Optical Communications sales rose 44% in its third quarter (Corning, 27-Oct).
  • Company guidance and communications: Corning guided fourth-quarter core sales up about 19% (27-Oct); Prysmian beat and raised its guidance on 29-Oct (Jefferies, 29-Oct) and rose 6.6% that day, when Hexatronic made its 47.28 high. Hexatronic itself made no formal upgrade (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus).

Who turned first: Ericsson's Stock RS turned up from 15-Oct, when Ericsson rose 6.8% on 4.8x its 50D volume (42 a month ago to 68 now), Nokia's turned down through its 22-Oct report (94 a month ago to 44), and Hexatronic's own report on 22-Oct carried it out of the group's slide. Hexatronic now trades with the optical and grid names on group days (30-Oct) and on its own news otherwise. Its most correlated stock, Mycronic (0.61), fell 3.6% on 30-Oct and 0.9% on 2-Nov, but over October Mycronic fell 11.0% against Hexatronic's +15.3%: the Swedish technology link has loosened since the report.

Hypothesis: Hexatronic is the laggard in a group that has already corrected, which is the template you described for Arteche on 2-Nov ("sector small cap growth darling being sold off; ala Theon"). For it: Prysmian, NKT and Nokia are 24% to 47% below their highs while Hexatronic is within 9%; on 2-Nov Hexatronic and Arteche fell while Prysmian (+0.8%) and NKT (+1.0%) rose; Hexatronic's cohort peers Nokia (0.9%) and Ericsson (0.2%) fell too. Against it: Hexatronic's estimates rose 10% in October and its economic peers, Corning and Prysmian, reported strong optical demand; Hexatronic's 2-Nov fall came on average volume. Test: Hexatronic's behaviour on the next group sell day; a fall well beyond Prysmian's and NKT's on heavy volume would support the hypothesis.

What the peers imply for Hexatronic: no sign of a fundamental turn; the risk is the group's sell days. Corning's and Prysmian's shipment commentary is the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's lead indicator, two quarters ahead, for Hexatronic's demand.

What the rules say for Hexatronic now, and what decides the next step

  • The failed breakout: Hexatronic closed back below its July closing high (45.42) on 2-Nov; the pullback is on light volume and every trend-health test passes, but its June top began the same way (items 1 and 2).
  • The sell-side is at full stretch: FY2027 EPS +10.3% in October, Net Upgrade Breadth +100%, 4 Buys of 4, consensus 6.0% above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's base case, no formal upgrade from management (items 4, 5 and 6).
  • Hexatronic is the strong outlier in a corrected group: Prysmian, NKT and Nokia sit 24% to 47% below their 52W highs, Hexatronic 8.9% (item 9, Peers).
  • Each tranche today: hold. Hexatronic is a Middle Innings Core Investment governed by the six Sell Criteria alone; none gives a sell signal, and all four tranches (4.95% of capital) hold.
  • The nearest sell test, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, read at the 30-Nov month-end: 13.1% below on 2-Nov, from 11.3% a week ago (26-Oct), 13.7% four weeks ago (5-Oct) and 21.4% thirteen weeks ago (5-Aug). If the market is flat, a Hexatronic close below SEK 38.20 on Mon 30-Nov gives a sell signal; that is 13.7% below the 2-Nov close, 1.6 times Hexatronic's usual weekly move of 8.7%; the rules would then sell the whole holding at the Tue 1-Dec session. SEK 38.20 lies below the 50D and 150D MAs, where the last two pullbacks ended; a market rise lifts the line.
  • The other tests do not combine with it: Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 has its Hardware part met (24) but needs Hexatronic's Stock RS to fall 26 points (95 to 69, below 70), and it held 90 or above through June's 31.3% fall; a tranche 30% below its highest close (SEK 33.10, 25.3% below) also needs Stock RS below 70. One fall would give one sell signal, not several.
  • The record: Hexatronic's relative price was 24.6% below its 52W high at the 31-Jul month-end, 0.4 points from a sell signal, and beyond 25% on other days (26-Jun, 6 to 14-Jul, 28 to 30-Jul, 3-Aug, 4-Sep, and 15, 17 and 18-Sep). Where 30-Nov falls in Hexatronic's swing decides the reading.
  • Fri 6-Nov reads Industry RS below 30 (Technology 79) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling (rising 5.0% over the month): no plausible sell signal.
  • Where Hexatronic stands for an add: pulling back or basing (the first stage). Hexatronic is pulling back from its 29-Oct high, 4.4% above its rising 20D MA (44.28 against 42.40), with no rising MA yet retested; the breakout that put it at the third stage in last week's note has reversed. The Master Dashboard agrees: Pulling Back plausible (3 of 4 tests), Basing possible (2 of 4), Healthy Retest plausible (4 of 6) but on a 50D MA 8.9% below the SP, no volatility contraction pattern (0 of 4).

  • What would change this reading: a close above 47.28 and then 48.58 on more than 1.41m shares moves Hexatronic to the third stage (breaking out); a light-volume hold at the rising 20D MA (42.40) to the second and then the third (a healthy retest); a distribution day closing below 42.40 points to a June-style decline towards the 38.20 line.

Richard decides.

4. Consensus by named FY

Fevertree's SP rose 13.2% over 1M while every FY2026 to FY2028 estimate edged down: estimates did not lead the rise, and the later years are being cut faster.

  • EPS: FY2026 28.0p, (0.4%) over 1M, flat over 3M, (2.0%) over 6M; FY2027 38.3p, (0.5%), flat, +0.2%, and +2.1% over 12M; FY2028 48.6p, (0.3%), (1.0%), +1.7%.
  • Revenue: FY2026 £400.6m, (0.1%) over 1M, (0.5%) over 3M; FY2027 £433.0m, (0.3%), (1.2%), and (3.1%) over 12M; FY2028 £464.6m, (0.4%), (2.6%), and (5.0%) over 12M.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 £49.8m, flat over 1M, (0.7%) over 3M; FY2027 £63.9m, (0.3%), (1.6%); FY2028 £77.6m, (0.4%), (4.0%), and (4.9%) over 12M.

The trajectory is being trimmed, not raised: over 3 months FY2028 revenue fell 2.6% and EBITDA 4.0%, against 0.5% and 0.7% for FY2026, so analysts are lowering the out-year, where the case's growth sits. Most of the FY2028 EBITDA cut came in August, before the 10-Sep results (£80.8m at the July month-end to £78.0m at August), and reversed a July rise (£78.4m at June): against the June month-end FY2028 EBITDA is down 1.1% and FY2028 revenue 1.9%, so the revenue trim is the steadier signal. Implied revenue growth is 8.1% into FY2027 and 7.3% into FY2028; EBITDA growth 28.3% and 21.5%; the implied EBITDA margin climbs from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% (FY2027) and 16.7% (FY2028). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's modal path (12.3%, 14.3%, 15.5%) sits below consensus on FY2028; its Alarm (FY2026 margin below 12%) and Invalidation (FY2027 below 13%) are both clear on consensus.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Why are EPS estimates creeping up but revenue isn't - FX or more fundamental?" The gap between EPS and revenue is below the EBITDA line, and the sources read point to the share count, not FX. Over 12 months FY2027 EPS rose 2.1% while FY2027 revenue fell 3.1% and EBITDA 2.0%. Jefferies raised its FY2026 EPS 3% at the 10-Sep results "purely" on buyback accretion, and the £60m 2026 buyback after £100m in 2025 cuts the shares in issue about 7% since the start of 2025 (earnings review, 18-Sep); the sources read show no FX effect. Over the last month EPS stopped creeping up (all three years down 0.3% to 0.5%); the falling revenue is the fundamental signal.

Developments behind the estimates: the first-half results (10-Sep) kept FY2026 guidance (revenue £402.4m, adjusted EBITDA £49.9m) on a first-half margin of 10.9%, against the 12.5% the full year implies (earnings review, 18-Sep); Deutsche Bank (6-Oct) expects only cans to be made in the United States in 2027, about half of United States volume, deferring the glass margin gain (key-question research filed 31-Oct). Both came after most of the FY2028 EBITDA cut, which was made in August; since the 31-Aug month-end FY2028 EBITDA has fallen 0.6% and FY2028 revenue 1.5%, so they fit the continued revenue trim better than the EBITDA cut. Test: FY2028 EBITDA consensus stabilising after a United States scanner print of 10% or more for September and October.

On your 28-Oct action, "Must track Nielsen data for Fevertree brand + sub-brands for US, UK and Europe monthly as a KQ RR": the two scanner key-question reports filed on 31-Oct find no Fevertree print after July and August's 16% (Circana, company, 10-Sep). Two cautions: scanners cover about a third of Fevertree's United States volume, and Deutsche Bank (30-Oct) judged September United States spirits data flattered by the later Labor Day, which may also lift a September mixer print.

Fevertree's SP has reached its average PT (947.3p, 0.4% above the close), and the two latest times it stood there were followed by falls: 1.0% below at the July 2025 month-end, then (14.9%) by the next; 0.6% above at the February 2026 month-end, then (20.0%). The average PT fell 1.9% from its July month-end peak (967.3p) to 948.9p at the August month-end, before the 10-Sep first-half results, and has been 947.3p every day since 7-Oct; the fall reversed a July rise, and against the June month-end (951.1p) the average PT is down only 0.4%. The brokers cutting are not named in the sources read: the changes the sources name are all holds or rises (Jefferies Buy, 1,100p, unchanged at the results; Kepler Cheuvreux to Buy, 910p from 900p, 15-Sep; Deutsche Bank Hold, 900p, 6-Oct). Fevertree's Net Upgrade Breadth is split, and EBITDA breadth has turned against the trend of the EBITDA estimates.

What the rules say for Fevertree now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • The sell-side is not behind the SP: every FY2026 to FY2028 estimate edged down over the month, FY2028 EBITDA fell 4.0% over 3 months, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth are negative, and the SP sits at its average PT (items 4, 5 and 6).
  • The move is the group's: since 22-Oct Fevertree has fallen and risen with Beverages, and the rotation lifts its larger peers' RS faster than Fevertree's (items 3 and 9).
  • The technicals are healthy: a 3.2% pullback to the rising 20D MA on ordinary volume held, swings have narrowed, and the SP is 2.7% below its 52W high (items 1 and 2).

Tranche 1 (2.0%, 50D MA) is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule. Fevertree closed at 943.5p, 9.6% above the 50D MA (860.8p) and 16.6% above the stop-loss (809.2p, the MA less 6%). Its exit confirmed on 3-Sep-2026 was cleared under your 2-Nov-2026 ruling (the rule applies from 2-Nov; a confirmation whose price had since closed back above the MA is cleared), and the tranche is judged afresh from 2-Nov.

The nearest sell test is the 50D MA, about 8% below.

  • Pace: Fevertree's close stood 9.6% above the 50D on 2-Nov, 9.6% a week earlier (26-Oct), 1.1% below it four weeks earlier (5-Oct) and 5.8% above it 13 weeks earlier (4-Aug). At a flat SP the rising 50D reaches about 868p by 6-Nov and 879p by 13-Nov, so a fall of 8.0% to 6.8% would start a test.
  • What the rule then does: on Fevertree, the volume test decides fast. Fevertree's down days have run 0.53x to 1.12x the 50D average in the last two weeks, five of the six at 0.76x or more, so a second close at or below the 50D on ordinary volume would likely confirm the exit on day 2 (the days-after volume average reaching 0.75), and tranche 1 would sell at the following Tuesday session. A close at or below the stop-loss confirms it on day 1.
  • The record: at each of Fevertree's five tops of the last 12 months (22-Oct-2025, 13-Feb, 20-Apr, 17-Jun, 7-Aug) the rule would have confirmed an exit within two sessions of the first close at or below the 50D, each time by the volume test, the first such close coming 0.8% to 8.4% below the high; and each time Fevertree went on to a low of 738p to 769p. After the 7-Aug high that exit came on 3-Sep at 814.9p, the one your 2-Nov ruling cleared; Fevertree fell 5.7% further, then rose 22.8%. The counter-case is the latest dip: judged under the rule, Fevertree's closes below the 50D on 1 and 2-Oct (832.5p and 833.5p, the second on 0.81x) would have confirmed an exit on 2-Oct by the volume test, at the start of the 13.2% rise to 943.5p.

The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov-2026, reads Consumer staples' Industry RS (66; it would need to fall 37 points, to 29, to read below 30) and the test of the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (met, because the relative 200D MA is falling; at a flat relative price it still reads (0.22%), and clearing it needs a close of about 1,040p). Neither sells Fevertree: both are information for a Probing Bet.

The rules allow an add: tranche 1 is clear, and Fevertree holds 1 of 2 tranches and 2.0% of its 6% limit; the next tranche would be 3% (capped), above the 1-2% your 28-Oct note named.

Where Fevertree stands for an add: the second stage, may be retesting a rising MA. The Master Dashboard's tests point to the first stage, pulling back or basing (basing plausible, 3 of 4 tests; a healthy retest of the 50D plausible, 5 of 6, but the SP is 9.6% from it; no volatility contraction pattern, 0 of 4). My reading moves it one stage on: the 29-Oct dip to the rising 20D held and bounced. It is not the third stage, because the bounce came on 0.93x volume and the 20D is not the MA your note named.
